# 9. Meta and Facebook ads

## Why Meta, Platform Basics and When to Start

### Why Meta is the main paid channel for beginners

- Meta is the parent company of Facebook, Instagram, Messenger, WhatsApp and Oculus, so Facebook advertising now runs under the Meta name [L1020.001].
- Meta Ads Manager creates and runs ads on Facebook, Instagram, Messenger, WhatsApp and third-party websites and apps in the Audience Network [L1020.003].
- Facebook Ads is a paid social traffic source and one of the main paid channels for a Shopify store [L0597.008].
- Meta's algorithm is the oldest and most consistent at finding target customers. Other platforms' algorithms are built on Meta's data and approach, which makes Meta the most reliable choice for scaling [L0296.001].
- Meta is the best platform for beginners among all paid traffic options because of consistent results, scalability and ease of use, despite ban risks [L0296.022].
- Facebook still has the strongest algorithm compared with TikTok, Pinterest and Snapchat. A beginner should concentrate on Facebook and use at most two platforms in total [L0349.007].
- Facebook is the main channel when the core audience is active on it. Target women over 40 on Facebook, and test Google Ads and TikTok ads later [L0492.049].
- Customer acquisition is built on profitable ads on Meta (Facebook and Instagram), so ad performance decides whether the business can grow [L0914.009].
- Paid traffic compared with organic: results within days instead of weeks, precise audience targeting, control over when to spend (day parting), and fast scaling from small to large budgets [L0296.028].
- Facebook suits higher-ticket items if the product can justify the price [L0363.054].
- Products priced above roughly 40 USD and aimed at an audience over 35 are better tested on Facebook and Instagram. They can succeed there even when they fail on TikTok [L1212.008].
- Facebook was the main platform and fastest route to scaling in one operator's experience, with TikTok second. Google Ads were only tested for the brand at that time [L0519.023].
- Facebook was the main source of traffic and sales for one store. Keeping Facebook as the core channel and testing a second platform reduces risk [L1439.016].
- In one lesson's test for a female audience, Facebook produced better results than Pinterest, so Facebook was the main channel. Pinterest was not tested and could be checked as a second channel [L0388.039].

### Platform basics: how Meta ads reach people

- Meta delivers an ad much like organic content. Its algorithm finds viewers based on how people respond to the creative. The advertiser's only levers are audience restrictions (targeting) and budget [L0639.001].
- Engagement drives delivery. Meta favours ads with strong engagement, and higher engagement usually brings lower CPM and lower cost per click. Creative quality should create engagement naturally; manipulating comments is an untested tactic [L0353.025].
- Meta punishes content that viewers skip or ignore. This raises CPMs and lowers CTR and engagement, so a poorly matched creative or audience drives costs up [L0639.020].
- Meta targets across three funnel stages [L0296.002]:
  - Top funnel: cold prospects with a relevant problem.
  - Middle funnel: people who engaged (viewed, commented).
  - Bottom funnel: high-intent actions (add to cart, initiated checkout, purchase).
- Facebook has fewer targeting options than Google because it restricts some audience data, such as members of certain groups. Targeting is less precise than on YouTube, and a Facebook view can register when someone scrolls past a video [L0510.039].
- Facebook's algorithm tends to deliver to people with stronger purchase intent [L0363.054].

### How the auction works

- Ads are sold through an auction. Advertisers compete for the chance to show an ad to one particular person, and the top-ranking bid claims the spot. A bid is the sum an advertiser will spend to reach that audience [L0977.002].
- Facebook runs an auction for each ad impression, so the price depends on competition for the same audience. Bidding 5 cents above your maximum bid gives you priority in the auction, but you do not pay that maximum. You pay the auction price [L0643.015].
- Example of a two-advertiser auction: each side raises its bid to win more impressions. A bid of 0.50 dollars is outbid by 1.00 dollar, then by 1.10 dollars, then by 1.25 dollars. Every raised bid means a higher price for the same audience, so an advertiser leaving the auction lowers the price for everyone who stays [L1407.028].

### How delivery and testing work

- A new video ad is first shown to a small test group, for example about 1,000 people. Meta optimises delivery toward the people who engage [L0398.002].
- A strong creative earns more engagement and watch time from that group, so Meta finds similar people and reach grows. A weak creative gets fewer engaged viewers and reach shrinks [L0398.002].
- Thresholds for pushing an ad to a larger audience [L0639.017]:
  - About 3% CTR or about 5% engagement, where engagement includes likes, comments and watching the video for more than 10 seconds.
  - At later stages, the examples given were 3 to 5% CTR and 6 to 10% engagement.
- Widening stages. The sources differ on the sizes [L0639.016, L0646.058]:

| Stage | Size per L0639.016 | Size per L0646.058 | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Test | About 500 to 1,000 people | About 1,000 people | L0639.016, L0646.058 |
| Next | About 1,000 to 5,000 people | About 10,000 people | L0639.016, L0646.058 |
| Next | About 10,000 people | Keeps widening while CTR or engagement stays around 3 to 10% | L0639.016, L0646.058 |
| Next | About 100,000 people | Keeps widening while CTR or engagement stays around 3 to 10% | L0639.016, L0646.058 |

- This organic-style spread is why an ad with strong early engagement can keep growing [L0646.058].
- Cold traffic from the Meta traffic objective tends to be low quality and should not be scaled up. If it is already running and performing acceptably, leave it unchanged [L0360.052].

### Automation features

- Modern Facebook advertising uses five core automation features, known as Power5 [L0238.001]:
  1. Auto advanced matching
  2. Dynamic ads
  3. Automatic ad placement
  4. Account simplification
  5. Campaign budget optimization
- The strategy is to set your objective and let Facebook's system find profitable customers and distribute ads across all placements [L0238.001].

### Facebook vs TikTok

- TikTok ads are hard for beginners. Creatives fatigue after a few weeks, and constant video production is required, while Facebook offers the more powerful algorithm [L0349.006].
- Facebook is preferred over TikTok in this lesson because its algorithm is older and more stable, and older audiences (40+, 50+) are easy to target. TikTok suits younger-demographic products such as beauty, jewelry, fashion, health and wellness, fitness and toys, and its CPMs fluctuate more [L1177.043].
- Facebook tends to deliver stronger purchase intent, while TikTok gives broader reach where audience quality depends more on the product [L0363.054].
- Testing counts: Facebook needed about four to five tested products to find a clear winner, while TikTok needed about seven to eight. Overlap between TikTok audiences is also lower because the audience is very large [L1436.081].
- Facebook gives more chances per product test than TikTok. The risk on Facebook is account bans, which can be reduced by following a correct account setup process [L1382.050].
- Case: in the lesson's Apple Watch strap test (summer), Facebook CPA was too high while TikTok CPA was good [L0577.041].
- Platform choice should be tested. Facebook also serves Instagram, so it reaches younger users than assumed. TikTok may serve a younger audience better. If ads do not work on Facebook, testing them on TikTok is a reasonable next step [L0999.054].
- Once a product works for one or two months on one channel, test it on TikTok, Google, Pinterest and organic social. A product that sells to one audience usually sells to others too [L1213.018].
- Native ads, Pinterest, Snapchat and Twitter ads are better treated as supplements to Facebook, not primary channels [L0349.006].

### Facebook and Instagram

- Facebook is the strongest platform for audiences over 30 and for reaching people outside major cities. Instagram works better for younger audiences in major cities [L0790.038].
- Instagram ads are created in the same ad platform as Facebook, with the same terms and targeting options, so audiences built for Facebook can be reused. Instagram is best for visual products that look attractive in an image or short video [L0643.047].
- Facebook owns Instagram, so running both together is a two-in-one approach for paid social [L0852.058].
- Instagram placements are slightly easier to get approved, but the return was lower in one case [L1440.022].
- Set up an Instagram account and post a few times per week from the beginning, but do not use it for paid expansion until sales exceed about 50,000 dollars per month from Google Shopping and Facebook dynamic remarketing [L1279.072].

### Pinterest

- Pinterest is not beginner-friendly. Its setup requires pixel tag implementation and substantial effort to understand its algorithm, which deters most beginning operators [L0296.034].
- Pinterest is a discovery and search platform where users come for inspiration and to buy. In the US its CPM typically runs around $3 to $5, which is cheaper than most ad platforms. Niches that fit well include home decor, fashion, beauty and health, and about 60% of users are women [L1133.001].
- Pinterest has rare bans and accepts traditional dropshipping products that other platforms often reject. More than 25% of time spent on Pinterest is shopping-related [L1133.002].
- Pinterest suits products for home decor, beauty, home improvement, inspiration, DIY and art. Results are less consistent than on Facebook or TikTok, but the platform is cheap to test [L1218.009].
- First Pinterest campaign structure [L1133.007]:
  - 1 campaign
  - 1 interest (stacked interests allowed)
  - 3 creatives
  - Campaign budget of $5 per day
  - Launch at 00:00 UTC
- Pinterest has no ad set budget option (no ABO). The only structure is a campaign budget optimization (CBO) campaign [L1134.008].
- Testing a product on Pinterest ads can start at about $5 per day, but run at least 5 products at a time, about $25 per day in total. Finding winners usually takes more than a week [L1218.008].
- Older test budget: a Pinterest test budget can start at about 5 to 10 dollars per day (older, 2021) [L0962.077].
- Pinterest can be connected to a store, and small-budget Pinterest ads can be tested (older, 2021) [L0671.234].
- Pinterest ads are not a first channel. Use them only after Google organic, Google Shopping and remarketing are dialled in. They can work for some stores (older, 2022) [L0969.077].
- Do not spend on Pinterest ads until Google Shopping and Meta ads are scaled as high as they can go while keeping ROAS at 10x or above. Pinterest organic posting can be done earlier (2025) [L0972.083].

### Twitter (X)

- Twitter (X) has lower CPM than Meta, TikTok or Google because few e-commerce advertisers use it, but it is not beginner-friendly. The algorithm is unproven, Shopify integration is unavailable, pixel tracking is not yet implemented, tracking tags must be added manually, and only certain product types succeed [L0296.030].
- X/Twitter ads were reported to have cheap CPMs and good click-through rates, mainly for specific countries such as the US or Gulf states. This was not tested yet and should be validated before budget is committed [L1108.019].

### YouTube, Audience Network and LinkedIn

- YouTube ads are not a core early channel. Most stores should not start them in the first six months to a year, because other channels offer more opportunity (older, 2021) [L0671.096, L0671.233].
- The Audience Network shows ads on third-party news sites and partner websites that Meta controls. A shopper who left a store can later see that store's ads there. This placement reaches past visitors again; it does not find new buyers [L1020.004].
- LinkedIn ads are very expensive and have rarely been profitable for e-commerce, so they are not recommended for beginners. Relationships with LinkedIn groups and their sponsors are a better way to reach a B2B audience (older, 2019) [L1069.084].

### Platform choice by product and audience

- Paid social is a separate channel group that includes Facebook, Instagram, Pinterest and Twitter. It is grouped apart from search traffic in the overall funnel plan [L0676.007].
- Search platforms such as Google, Amazon and YouTube search reach only about 5 to 10 percent of the market, while about 90 percent sits on social platforms such as Facebook, Instagram and Pinterest. Each platform needs its own conversation and an offer adjusted to it. Facebook works best as a conversation-starting channel [L0643.009].
- Social media is effective at reaching unaware prospects and turning them into problem-aware prospects. It is a weak way to find the small group of buyers ready to purchase now, apart from retargeting (Source B, older, 2021 to 2022) [L1392.008].
- Facebook can work across about 20 industries. Service businesses such as plumbing or bricklaying are harder to sell through ads because the need is urgent, not social. First decide whether your customers search for the product or discover it socially, then choose the platform [L0643.053].
- Organic Instagram growth was realistic around 2019, but algorithms are more complex now, so a modern brand typically needs paid advertising to reach buyers. Plan the marketing budget with paid media in mind from the start (older, 2019) [L1438.043].
- Paid social is not required for success. Dynamic remarketing is the main recommended use, and flashy video ads are not necessary to start (older, 2020) [L1111.097].
- Sending Facebook and Pinterest traffic directly to an Amazon product listing does not convert well. These platforms work best for content marketing, email list building and retargeting, not cold traffic to a marketplace listing where the customer cannot see the brand story or benefit from the community [L0228.033].
- Facebook works well for product launches with heavy discounts and hyper-targeted audiences. Facebook users are in social mode, not buying mode, so ads need to build awareness and desire before purchase. This makes Facebook better suited to cold traffic acquisition than Amazon's buying-mindset audience [L0227.030].
- Facebook is currently difficult for beginners to test products on, because it is expensive and bans are common. In this case, Instagram placements were slightly easier to get approved, but the return was lower [L1440.022].

### Channel order: Google first, Meta remarketing early

- Google has buying intent because people search for what they want. Facebook targets broad audiences who are not actively searching. This is why Google is the first platform to prioritise (older, 2021) [L1090.111].
- Facebook ads are useful for cold traffic, but for buyers who actively search for a product, Google ads deliver more profitable traffic. Facebook is not dropped, but it is used with less priority (older, 2020) [L0967.078].
- Facebook ads are best used for remarketing to people who already visited the store, since broad audience targeting is weaker for finding new buyers. Keep Facebook at about 10% of the total advertising budget (older, 2021) [L1090.110].
- Starting Facebook campaigns before Google Shopping is a poor order, because Facebook is better used to capture visitors who left the store (older, 2019) [L1069.083].
- Meta remarketing is worth setting up early even with a small budget, because return on ad spend is usually high when the budget is small and each sale is worth a lot. Set up Meta remarketing once the Google negatives are in place (2025) [L0807.028].
- Start Meta with remarketing ads only, and do not test cold Meta ads at the beginning. Scale Meta after Google Shopping has reached its practical budget limit (2025) [L0972.082].
- Facebook ads should start early with very small budgets and be used for dynamic remarketing. Start them when your Google ads go live (older, 2021) [L0671.094, L0671.231].
- Remarketing should run from day one, since it usually produces the most profitable ads, but it cannot work without Google traffic feeding the audience (older, 2019) [L1086.028].
- Paid promotion of Facebook page posts, such as boosting them to remarketing audiences of past visitors, should not start from day one. Google Ads offers easier early wins for a new store, so paid Meta posts come after the organic page is set up and active [L0653.020].
- Do not run Facebook awareness ads while the store is still being built or before suppliers are uploaded. Start them once the store has products and the pages are ready (older, 2020) [L0468.107].
- Do not set up Facebook or Google ad accounts until supplier products are live in the store, because ads need real products to send traffic to (older, 2021) [L0962.075].
- Do not launch Meta ads before the store has been reviewed and approved. Launching early is a common rookie mistake that wastes ad spend. Launch only after store approval (Source D 2026) [L0491.020].

### Budget split and starting budgets

| Source | Figure | When it applies | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source A, 2019 (older) | Split the starting paid budget roughly 30:5 between Google Shopping for cold traffic (about $30 per day) and Facebook and Instagram DPA remarketing (about $5 per day). Do not split the total evenly | Starting split for a new store | L1086.027 |
| Source A, 2019 (older) | The 30:5 split is a starting point; the ratio should shift as the account scales | As the account scales | L1086.028 |
| Source A, 2021 (older) | Keep Facebook at about 10% of total advertising budget | Remarketing-focused Facebook spend | L1090.110 |
| Source A, 2022 (older) | Facebook budget for a new store (beginners): about $10 to $30 per day after the site is built and suppliers are found. Review ad accounts every few days for optimisation | Beginner store with a built site and found suppliers | L0969.079 |
| Source A, 2024 | $250 per month is sufficient to start advertising. Even $10 per day is enough for initial testing. Reinvest profits as they arrive | Starting out with a small budget | L0253.012 |
| Source A, 2021 (older) | Pinterest test budget about $5 to $10 per day | Early Pinterest test | L0962.077 |
| Source C (current) | Pinterest first campaign at $5 per day; at least 5 products at once, about $25 per day in total | Pinterest product testing | L1133.007, L1218.008 |

- Paid Facebook ads had become expensive. Product-funded influencers are a cheaper way to start [L0790.040].
- Spending $1,000 on ads without a tested offer often produces no return [L0790.040].

### When to hold off on cold traffic

- Do not send cold Facebook traffic to the store until it generates at least 50,000 dollars in sales per month from Google Shopping and Facebook dynamic remarketing combined. Those channels are easier wins first (older, 2021) [L1279.071].
- Sources differ on the gate. The 2021 source uses the $50,000 monthly sales gate above. The 2025 source says not to test cold Meta at the beginning and to scale Meta after Google Shopping reaches its practical budget limit [L1279.071, L0972.082].
- Cold Meta traffic from the traffic objective tends to be low quality and should not be scaled up [L0360.052].
- Pinterest scaling gate: do not spend until Google Shopping and Meta are scaled with ROAS at 10x or above [L0972.083].

### When to hold off on social commerce

- Use Facebook dynamic remarketing as one of the core paid channels before trying social commerce such as Instagram Shopping. Instagram social commerce is not a requirement and can distract early on (older, 2022) [L0665.075].
- A store should reach about 50,000 dollars per month in revenue before testing social commerce (older, 2022) [L0665.076].
- As of August 2019, the Facebook Shop channel was not a proven extra revenue source, so ads that send traffic to the store site remain the priority (older, 2019) [L1069.082].
- Selling directly on Facebook is not recommended because the platform is not built for it. Send traffic to your own store or sales page instead. Facebook was rolling out seller listings, but at the time of the lesson they were not yet reliable [L0643.049].
- Selling directly inside a social platform was not recommended for Facebook at the time, because its seller listings were still unproven. Pinterest had run on-platform selling for longer and was described as comparatively good for it [L0643.064].

### Operator sequence for starting Meta

1. Store reviewed and approved; supplier products live in the store [L0491.020, L0962.075].
2. Google ads live: Google Shopping first for cold traffic [L1090.111, L1069.083].
3. Meta set up with remarketing ads only, at a small budget, once Google negatives are in place [L0972.082, L0807.028, L0671.094].
4. Instagram account created and posted to a few times per week from the beginning, without paid expansion yet [L1279.072].
5. Cold Meta traffic only after the revenue gate and Google Shopping limit are reached; check against the sources above [L1279.071, L0972.082].
6. Social commerce only after remarketing is strong and revenue is around 50,000 dollars per month [L0665.075, L0665.076].
7. Pinterest only after Google Shopping and Meta are scaled with ROAS of 10x or above; Pinterest organic posting can start earlier [L0972.083].

#### Further details

- Bing search text ads: do not use until the store is already very profitable; use a store-name campaign only to block competitors from taking branded traffic. [L0969.078]

## Business Manager, Page, Instagram and Ad Account Setup

### Prerequisites and account structure

Five things must be in place before you run Meta ads:

1. A warmed-up personal Facebook profile, so Meta sees a real user and not a bot. [L1021.010]
2. A Business Manager, which is the control centre for your ads. [L1021.010]
3. An ad account inside the Business Manager, where the ads run. [L1021.010]
4. A finished Shopify store with its own domain, because the ads send people there. [L1021.010]
5. A warmed-up Facebook Page, which is the face of the brand and makes people trust it more. [L1021.010]

Most failed launches come from an incomplete setup, not from the ad itself. Beginners who skip the prerequisites and backend setup often see an ad that does not spend, no data in reports, or errors in the account. [L1021.016]

Meta's ad algorithm can consistently find suitable buyers once a pixel feeds it purchase data. The whole account structure must therefore be set up cleanly from the start to avoid problems later. [L1024.001]

Meta's setup order: [L1288.005]

1. Create the Facebook ad account. [L1288.005]
2. Create the Facebook page. [L1288.005]
3. Set up the pixel: connect the domain, verify the domain, integrate the Shopify conversion API and turn on the data switches for tracking. [L1288.005]
4. Connect Facebook to Shopify. [L1288.005]
5. Connect Instagram and populate it with images. [L1288.005]

A 2024 procedure for the store's account starts with the same core: [L0806.060]

1. Create the Business Manager account separately from your personal Facebook account. [L0806.060, L1025.001]
2. Create the ad account inside Business Manager. [L0806.060]
3. Create the store's Facebook page inside Business Manager, with you as admin. [L0806.060]
4. If the account gets restricted, contact support through facebook.com/business/help and choose Get support. [L0806.060]

**Account hierarchy**

| Level | What it is | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Business Manager | Top-level hub under which all business assets sit: Facebook page, Instagram account, ad accounts, pixels and people access. It is where the business account is managed. | L1024.002 |
| Ad accounts | Ads Manager manages all ad accounts. An ad account is where campaigns, ad sets and ads are created and analysed. A fresh Business Manager should start with one ad account. | L1024.004 |
| Pages and Instagram | Linked to the Business Manager so ads can run on them. | L1024.014, L1024.015 |

Use a separate Business Manager for each business you run, rather than reusing one from another business. [L1025.002, L0638.047]

Run ads through a separate Meta Business Manager instead of a personal ad account. The extra layer of separation helps protect the ad account and lowers the chance it gets flagged or restricted. [L1025.001]

Keep ad accounts and Business Managers separate: one Business Manager for ads and another for each business if needed. Avoid combining multiple stores in a single ad account, because it becomes hard to organise. [L0638.047]

Add multiple Facebook pages, Instagram accounts and ad accounts to one admin only when you need to share data between them, such as pixel data for cross-promotion. Unrelated stores should get their own admin. [L0965.052]

Add a second trusted person as an admin on Meta Business Manager accounts. This keeps access if the personal profile is restricted or lost. A personal profile is needed to create a new Business Manager, so losing it can block recovery. [L1287.044]

### Business Manager: creation

#### Creating the Business Manager

A Business Manager is free to create at business.facebook.com. [L0551.033, L0962.073, L1025.001] *(Older advice, 2020 and 2021.)*

Meta's Business Manager is needed to run ads. [L0962.073] *(Older advice, 2021.)*

Creating a Business Manager for a new store (Source C method): [L1024.006]

1. Go to business.facebook.com while logged in with your personal Facebook account. [L1024.006]
2. Click Create an account. [L1024.006]
3. Enter the business name: use your registered business name, or if you have none, your Shopify store name. [L1024.006]
4. Enter your name and a business email address. A regular email you check often is acceptable. Avoid using only the Shopify email. [L1024.006]
5. Click Submit to create the Business Manager. [L1024.006]

Creating a Business Manager for a new store (Source A method): [L1025.003]

1. Open business.facebook.com in a new tab or browser. [L1025.003]
2. If you have no Business Manager yet, click Create a new account. If you already have one, open the account dropdown in the left navigation and click Create at the top of the list to make a new one for this business. [L1025.003]
3. Name the Business Manager after your store and enter your business email, preferably the same email as your Google account. [L1025.003, L1025.002]
4. Log in. The top shows your business name, and the middle section is empty until you add assets. [L1025.003]

Naming and email rules: [L1025.002]

- Name the Business Manager after the store.
- Enter a business email address when creating it.
- Use the same email you use for your Google account, so access stays consistent across platforms.

Do not submit a second Business Manager for the same business. If Meta reports that your business may already have a Business Manager, continue with the existing account, because each business should have only one. [L1025.020]

#### Business Manager limits

The number of ad accounts a Business Manager allows increases with successful payment transactions. The sequence is 1, then 2, then 5, 7, 8 and finally 12 accounts. [L1213.010]


If the Business Manager limit blocks creating another ad account, open a new Business Manager at business.facebook.com/create. [L1213.012]

Before creating a new ad account, check the current ad account limit in Business Settings: [L1213.085]

- If the limit is not reached, create the new ad account there and add payment details. [L1213.085]
- If the limit is reached, open a new Business Manager at business.facebook.com/create. [L1213.085]

By default, Facebook allows only one Business Manager per personal account. To create a Business Manager, use business.facebook.com/create. If it is blocked, check the business details in Business settings and complete them so the account looks like a real business. [L0638.046]

Business info also contains an Ad account creation limit field, which caps how many ad accounts the portfolio can create. Check this value before planning to add more ad accounts under the same business. [L651.022]

#### Navigating Business settings

Meta has renamed Facebook Business Manager to Meta Business Manager. The tools and interface remain largely the same, but menu and screen labels may differ from older guides or recordings. The setup steps stay the same. [L1020.002, L1025.021]

Navigation in Business Manager: [L1025.004]

- The hamburger menu (three lines, top left) expands all tools. Business settings is listed at the top under Shortcuts.
- In Business settings, the sidebar sections such as Accounts and Brand safety are collapsed by default. Expand them before their sub-items appear: Pages, Instagram accounts, Ad accounts and Domains.

Meta Business Suite is used to check the status of ad accounts. Its Home screen shows Ad account performance, alerts and a prompt to go to Business settings if you lack permission to an ad account. [L651.015]

### Business info and contact details

#### Finishing the business basics

Finish the Business Manager basics in this order: [L1024.010]

1. Open Settings and go to Business settings. [L1024.010]
2. Open Business info and fill in the business details: legal name, address, phone, website, tax ID if available. [L1024.010, L1024.008]
3. Start verification via View details. [L1024.010, L1024.008]
4. Scroll down and turn on two-factor authentication. [L1024.010]
5. Submit the business information. [L1024.010]

Enter the legal business name, address, business phone number, website and, if available, the tax ID in Business info. Then use View details to start business verification. [L1024.008]

Enter the business address in your Facebook business account settings. [L0696.014]

Business email: open Business info, scroll down to the Business email field and replace it with your own email address. Notifications about the business portfolio are sent to this address. [L651.007]

Business info also contains a Primary business location field that can be edited. [L651.007]

#### Business notifications

In Business settings > Business options, the Pages and notifications toggle decides whether people in the portfolio see Page notifications on Facebook as well as in business tools. When it is off, notifications appear only in business tools. [L651.021]

A separate Business notifications on Facebook toggle controls notifications about the portfolio's Pages on Facebook. [L651.021]

### Facebook Page setup

#### Why a Page is required

Before publishing a warm-up or test ad in a new Meta ad account, make sure a Facebook Page exists. If there is none, create one, then select it along with the matching Instagram account. The ad cannot run without a page. [L0348.025]

Before an ad account can publish ads, Ads Manager asks for a verified phone number and a confirmed Facebook Page. [L1027.017]

Create at least a basic Facebook page from the beginning, even though it is not required for the first sales. One post per week is enough to show customers that the store is real. *(Older advice, 2020.)* [L0551.035]

A test page can use a placeholder name while the brand is still being tested. [L487.069]

Use a placeholder name for a test page: [L0487.069]

- A Facebook business page can be created in about five minutes. [L0487.069]
- Set up a matching Instagram account alongside it before posting content for early engagement. [L0487.069]

#### Creating the page

There are three routes, and the right one depends on the situation:

| Route | When it applies | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Profile switcher, personal profile | Public page created from your personal profile through the profile switcher. | L651.001 (steps 7 to 8), L1024.012 |
| Inside Business Manager | When you create the page from inside Business Manager with you as admin. *(Older method, 2020.)* | L551.034, L806.060 |
| Personal account fallback | When the page category Brand blocks creation from Business Manager. | L1347.024 |

Route 1, creating a public Facebook Page from your personal profile: [L1024.012]

1. Open your Facebook account and click See all profiles. [L1024.012]
2. Click See all pages. [L1024.012]
3. Click Create new page. [L1024.012]
4. Name the page after the store. [L1024.012]
5. Choose a category that fits the niche, for example Beauty shop or Clothing brand. If no fitting option exists, use Discount shop. [L1024.012]
6. Write a one- to two-line bio that describes the brand, since it shows under the page name. [L1024.012]
7. Click Create page, then fill in the rest of the details: Shopify store or website link, phone number, store email, business address. [L1024.012]
8. Upload a profile picture and a background image. [L1024.012]

Route 1, Source D method: [L651.001]

- Create a public Facebook Page through the profile switcher by choosing See all profiles and then Create Page. [L651.001]
- Upload a profile picture and banner, add a short store description and a website link. [L651.001]

Route 2, creating inside Business Manager (2020 method): [L551.034]

1. Open Facebook Business Manager at business.facebook.com. [L551.034]
2. To create a new page, use the search box at the top right, search for create a page, and create it inside Business Manager. [L551.034]
3. To use a page you already own, search for the page inside Business Manager so Facebook confirms ownership. [L551.034]
4. Assign the page to your Business Manager account, then log in to Business Manager to run ads on it. [L551.034]
5. Do not delete an existing page to fix setup problems. [L551.034]

Route 3, personal account fallback: if the page category Brand blocks creation from Business Manager, create the page from your personal Facebook account instead. The category has little impact, so an unrelated one can be used, as long as the page is connected to your Business Manager and appears when campaigns are created. [L1347.024]

#### Connecting the page to Business Manager

A Facebook page created from a personal profile is initially owned by that profile, so it must be claimed into the Business Manager. Once linked, the page appears in the left navigation and ads run from that page. [L1025.006]

Linking an existing page: [L1025.005, L1024.014]

1. In Business settings, expand Accounts and click Pages. [L1025.005]
2. Click Add at the top and choose Add a page. Choose this option when your business already owns the page. The other two options are for requesting access or creating a new page. [L1025.005]
3. Paste the link or ID of the page, select the page when it appears, and click Add page. [L1024.014]

Check that the new Page appears under Business settings > Pages, owned by the business portfolio, with your own user assigned at Full control. A Page that is not connected to the portfolio cannot be managed from it. [L651.013]

#### Page health

Filling in the full page information (website link, phone, email, address) raises the page health status from fair to good. A healthier page supports smoother ad delivery. [L1024.013]

Finish setting up the page with the checklist shown on a new Facebook Page. It lists Category, Personal details, Details, Links, Services, Bio, About you, Pinned details, Book, Offers, Work and Education. Filling these in makes the page look complete to visitors. Start with Category and Links, since those were required or are used most in the setup. [L651.026]

### Instagram account

#### When to connect Instagram

The timing depends on the stage of the brand: [L1171.016, L1171.017, L0487.069]

| Stage | Action | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Testing products | Do not connect an Instagram account. Ads still appear on Instagram, and clicking the profile sends the user straight to the website. | L1171.016 |
| Building a brand | Connect an Instagram account. Buyers check recent posts, follower count and active stories, and a weak profile makes the store look unserious. | L1171.017 |
| Early engagement | Set up a matching Instagram account alongside the page before posting content. | L0487.069 |

#### Connecting the account

Connecting it to the Business Manager lets ads run on Instagram as well as Facebook. [L1024.015]

Instagram linking, Source A method: [L1025.007]

1. In Business settings, expand Accounts, click Instagram accounts, click Add and then Connect to Instagram account. [L1025.007]
2. Log in with the business Instagram account, not a personal one. [L1025.007]
3. Meta then asks for permission to create content, see messages and comments, create ads and see insights. [L1025.007]

Instagram linking, Source C method: [L1024.015]

1. In Business settings > Accounts > Instagram accounts, click Add and then Connect your Instagram account. [L1024.015]
2. Log in to the business Instagram account in the login window that opens. The username should ideally match the store name. [L1024.015]

Source A procedure, short form: [L1025.003]

1. Expand Accounts and click Instagram accounts, click Add, then click Connect to Instagram account and log in with the business Instagram login. [L1025.003]

Do not link your personal Instagram account to the Business Manager. Connect only the business Instagram account that was set up for the store. [L1025.008]

### Ad account

#### Creating the ad account

Create the ad account in Business settings, under Accounts, then Ad accounts, then Add, then Create a new ad account. [L1024.016, L1025.010]

Source C procedure: [L1024.016]

1. Enter the ad account name. [L1024.016]
2. Select the time zone and currency. [L1024.016]
3. Click Next. [L1024.016]
4. Choose the business (your own business) and click Create. [L1024.016]

Source A form fields: [L1025.010]

- Advertising on behalf of (your business name). [L1025.010]
- Payment method, which is how you will pay Meta once ads run. [L1025.010]
- Currency. [L1025.010]
- Ad account name. [L1025.010]
- Time zone. [L1025.010]

Then click Create ad account. [L1025.010]

Source A 2020 field summary, for reference: fill in Advertising on behalf of (business name), Payment method, Currency, Ad account name (your store name) and Time zone (the zone you operate in), then click Create ad account. [L1025.003]

Create a new ad account for each new store, even if an existing ad account is available, so the store gets fresh data and a unique account. When adding an ad account you can claim an existing one, request access to someone else's, or create a new one. [L1025.009]

#### Naming and time zone

Name the ad account after your store so it is easy to identify. [L1025.011]

Set the time zone to the zone where you are based and do business, because it is used later when creating promotions. [L1025.011]

#### Assigning yourself access

After creating the ad account, assign yourself: select your own name in the people list and grant the Manage ad account permission, so you have full access to the account. [L1024.017]

To give yourself access to an ad account, open Business settings, go to the Ad accounts section in the left sidebar, open the ad account, choose Add people, select your own name and set permissions. [L651.006]

In Business settings > People > Add people, enter the email address of a partner or team member and assign access. The person who created the account is added automatically by default. [L1024.011]

#### Verifying the account before launch

Before launching, check the ad account name shown after opening Ads Manager from Business settings > All tools. If the name is different, you may be advertising from a personal ad account. Always confirm that it is the business ad account. [L1024.019]

Check the ad account in Meta Business Suite and confirm it is active, with you assigned to it. [L651.014]

In Business settings > Ad accounts, each ad account row offers Assign people, Create ad, Assign partner and Open in Ads Manager. The ad account ID and owning business appear when you select it. Connected assets on that screen lists the Pages and other assets linked to the ad account. [L651.023]

Before creating ads in Ads Manager, a notice asks you to review Meta's non-discrimination policy and click I accept. The policy prohibits ads that deny opportunities to individuals or groups based on protected characteristics such as race, religion, age, sex or disability. [L651.018]

Ads Manager is where campaigns, ad sets and ads are created and reviewed. A new ad account shows no campaigns and the message that no ads have been created yet, with a Create button to start. [L651.019]

### Currency

Use ad accounts in USD if possible. Meta charges at the same rate for each currency, but USD is the cheapest of USD, EUR and GBP, and Meta prefers spending in USD. Set the ad account currency to USD in Business Manager or the ad account settings, not via the credit card. [L690.019]

The currency setting in the ad account: [L690.021]

- Business Manager or ad account settings > currency > USD. [L690.021]
- The card can remain in another currency, but the bank may charge a foreign conversion fee. [L690.021]

Changing an ad account's currency after creating it does not lose the Meta pixel. The pixel can be attached to any ad account regardless of currency. [L690.020]

In Billing & payments for the ad account, the example showed US Dollars (USD) by default. Set the currency and country to match the business. [L651.016]

### Payment methods

Add a payment method under Billing & payments with the currency and country matching the business. [L651.001]

To add one: open Billing & payments for the ad account, open Payment methods and choose Add payment method. Billing details include business name, address and currency. [L651.016]

A payment method can be skipped at first, but Meta may ask for it when the first ad is published. [L1027.017]

A card that is declined or missing blocks all ad spend, because if the payment card fails, ads stop running. Check the card before launch, since an inactive card is a common reason ads do not spend. [L1021.015]

### Portfolio invitations and people access

#### Joining a business portfolio

Set up access to a Meta business portfolio in this order: [L651.001]

1. Wait about 6 hours after receiving the business portfolio invitation link before joining. [L651.001, L651.002]
2. Open the invitation link through the proxy assigned to the account, log in with Facebook and enter the verification code from the email if asked. [L651.001, L651.003]
3. Enter your name as it should appear in the business portfolio, continue and accept the invitation. [L651.001]
4. Open Business settings and check the ad accounts listed. The example portfolio had three. [L651.001]
5. Open one ad account, add yourself under Add people and assign permissions. [L651.001]
6. In Business info, replace the business email with your own email address. [L651.001, L651.007]
7. Create a public Facebook Page through the profile switcher. [L651.001]
8. Upload a profile picture and banner, add a short store description and a website link. [L651.001]
9. Confirm the Page is connected under Business settings > Pages. [L651.001]
10. Confirm the ad account shows as active in Meta Business Suite. [L651.001]
11. Add a payment method under Billing & payments with currency and country matching the business. [L651.001]

Wait about 6 hours after receiving the Meta business portfolio invitation link before trying to join it. [L651.002]

Open the invitation link through the proxy assigned to the account and log in with Facebook. If a verification code is requested, it is sent to the email address, so retrieve it there. [L651.003]

When an invitation link is opened while logged out, the Meta business login page offers Log in with Facebook, Log in with Instagram, Create new account and Log in with a managed Meta account. Use the Facebook option to join a business portfolio with a personal Facebook profile. [L651.020]

#### Checking an invitation before accepting

Before accepting a business portfolio invitation, check the business name, country, website and business verification status on the review screen. Decline the invitation if you do not know the business, because accepting adds you to its portfolio. [L651.004]

Once you accept an invitation, your name, email, profile picture and actions taken for the business are visible to other people managing that portfolio. Actions taken on behalf of the business are not shared to your personal profile. [L651.005]

#### Permission levels

For an ad account, the two levels differ as follows: [L651.006, L651.025]

| Level | What it offers |
|---|---|
| Partial access | Manage campaigns (ads), View performance and Manage Creative Hub mockups. [L651.006] |
| Full control | Manage ad accounts, which covers ad account settings, finances and permissions. [L651.006] |

Full control on an ad account is broader and includes ad account settings, finances and permissions. [L651.025]

Full control on a Facebook Page means everything except sensitive actions. A person with Full control can manage the Page but cannot perform the restricted sensitive actions. [L651.025]

Assign ad account access with this difference in mind. [L651.025]

### Domain verification

#### Why verify

Domain verification shows Meta that you own the website sending traffic and conversion data. Because data is shared between the site and Meta, this ownership check is what allows the data to be passed through. [L1025.013]

A domain only needs to be verified once. The example account had several verified because it ran ads through several domains. An agency model would add domains differently. [L1025.012]

Of the three domain verification options Meta offers, DNS verification was used. It provides a TXT record value that must be added to the domain's DNS configuration. [L1025.014]

#### Procedure (Source A method)

1. Expand Brand safety in Business settings and click Domains. [L1025.012]
2. Click Add, choose Create a new domain, type the store URL starting with https and click Add. [L1025.012]
3. Choose the DNS verification option and copy the text value shown. [L1025.003]
4. Open the domain settings of your store. For a domain bought through Shopify, go to Sales channels > Online store > Domains. Otherwise, go to the site where the domain is hosted. Select the main domain, not the myshopify.com address, then click Manage and open DNS settings. [L1025.003, L1025.017]
5. Click Add custom record, choose TXT, leave the Name as @, paste the copied value into the text value field and click Confirm. [L1025.003]
6. Return to Business Manager, make sure you are still in the DNS verification section, and click Verify. If it shows a mismatch, wait a few minutes and try again. [L1025.003, L1025.017, L1025.018]
7. After it verifies, refresh the page to confirm the domain shows as verified. [L1025.003]

#### Common errors and timing

- Adding the DNS TXT record in the wrong place is a common error. Enter the main domain, not the myshopify.com address, or the record will not be found. [L1025.017]
- After adding the record, go back to the DNS verification option before clicking Verify. Verifying from the meta tag section fails. [L1025.017]
- After adding a DNS TXT record, Meta may report a mismatch on the first Verify attempt. [L1025.018]
- Documentation says DNS changes can take up to 72 hours to spread. In practice, it can take only a few minutes. In one setup, verification went through after about 2 minutes. [L1025.018]

### Business Manager security and recovery

Turn on two-factor authentication when finishing the Business Manager basics. [L1024.010]

Add a second trusted person as an admin, so the business keeps access if the personal profile is restricted or lost. [L1287.044]

If the account gets restricted, contact support through facebook.com/business/help and choose Get support. [L806.060]

### Tax on advertising

Meta applies a tax to advertising when the ad account is registered in the UK or in EU countries. The rate depends on each country. Ad account country settings were discussed as a way to avoid this. The speaker warned that this should be verified with a tax professional, as the rules are legal and tax questions. [L552.019]
A "business limit reached" message when creating a new ad account can come from many pages linked to one business. Removing pages from the Business Manager is one option to avoid deleting them, though the speaker had not tested it. [L1213.011]

Facebook traffic setup, in order:
1. Sign up for Facebook Business Manager.
2. Create your first Facebook remarketing campaign.

Traffic setup on Facebook:
1. Sign up for Facebook Business Manager.
2. Create the first Facebook remarketing campaign.
[L0532.019]

5. Do not delete an existing page to fix setup problems.
[L0551.034]

## Account Trust, Warm-up and Ban Prevention

### Ban Rates and What They Mean for Planning

- Older sources put the ban rate for new advertisers very high. One source said Facebook bans beginners about 90% of the time, because bots are placed to protect user experience [L0296.004]. Another claimed 90-95% of beginners are banned, but this is an unverified estimate [L0637.001]. A speaker estimated 80-90% at that time [L1440.019]. These figures are older and unverified.
- A newer source says bans for new advertisers have become less common and are no longer a universal problem. Recovery solutions exist for properly set-up accounts that do face suspension [L0296.033]. Plan from the newer position, but do not treat any percentage as a guarantee [L0296.033, L0637.001].
- Meta's ban systems are largely automated. New advertisers are screened by algorithms before any human looks at the account [L0637.001].
- Prevention steps lower the risk. No step guarantees that a ban will not happen [L0637.001, L1440.019].
- Meta reacts to new accounts that show unstable or suspicious behaviour, such as changing devices, IP addresses, payment details, or sudden spend jumps. Keeping device, IP, proxy, payment and spend patterns consistent makes the account look like one stable, legitimate user [L1477.010].

### Profile Age and Identity

- Use an older personal profile, at least 2 years old, not a new dummy profile [L0637.003, L0637.059].
- A brand-new dummy profile usually gets the account banned right away, because Meta has no activity history to trust [L0637.059].
- Set up a new business account using an existing personal profile that already has history, not a brand-new account. A new account with no data and sudden trading can look suspicious [L0950.054].
- Do not run ads from a bare personal account. Set up a business profile in Meta Business [L0341.027].
- Running ads from a personal profile without a business account and business details raises the risk of a flag. Meta reviews new advertisers for legitimacy, so set up the business and provide as much verifiable information as possible [L0343.022].
- Confirm the profile identity early, before any problem. When Meta asks for ID verification after a ban, it checks that a real, legitimate person runs the ads, so the platform should already know who you are [L0637.002, L0637.003].
- Profile identity confirmation is reached from the personal profile via Settings & privacy > Settings > identity confirmation. Upload a government or national ID document there. The menu path is as stated in the source; check the current Meta UI, since menu names change [L0637.004].
- Meta can suspend a new ad account, sometimes instantly, if identity verification is not completed in time. Submit the requested ID promptly [L0341.026].
- Meta may suspend a new ad account right after creation and may ask for identity confirmation within about 48 hours. Respond in time, because missing the deadline can lead to a ban [L0343.021].
- Older (2018): Meta can grant account verification on Facebook and Instagram after substantial ad spend, usually through an account representative who requests the verification [L0466.057].

### Two-Factor Authentication

- Enable two-factor authentication on the personal profile via profile > Settings & privacy > Settings > Security and login > Two-factor authentication. The purpose is to show Meta that the same person who verified the identity is the one running the ads [L0637.005].
- Set up two-factor authentication for every admin on a Meta business account, especially admin accounts. One account was reportedly banned because a non-admin user had no two-factor authentication set up [L0343.023, L0341.027].
- Under Business settings > Business options, the Two-factor authentication setting can require a login code to access the business portfolio from unknown browsers. The option was set to No one in the example. The Access recommendations setting suggests extra assets to people who manage permissions [L0651.008].

### Business Manager and Business Verification

- Keep the Business Manager separate from the personal profile. A ban on the ad account or Business Manager should not take down the personal profile. In the worst case only the Business Manager is shut down and a new one can be created. A personal profile ban can block Facebook use entirely [L0637.006].
- Create the Business Manager at business.facebook.com/create, not as a personal ad account [L0637.003]. Create it with business details rather than the default personal one [L0650.012].
- Create the Business Manager and verify it with a legitimate business. A business account without a real business can cause verification problems and more ad account bans [L0950.053].
- Business verification is optional but strongly increases trust with Meta. Verify in Business settings by submitting one business document, such as an LLC, Ltd or similar. The review took about 4-5 days in the source. Verification does not restrict which countries you can target [L0637.007, L0637.003].
- Verified businesses get more flexibility and access to live chat support. Verification also helps Facebook see the business as legitimate rather than as a scam risk [L0709.032].
- One account went through a 13-step verification process and had no restrictions or bans for about 1.5 years afterwards [L1440.020].
- Add 1-2 trusted people to the Business Manager with page and pixel rights [L0637.003]. Extra people add trust and protect the assets. If the main personal profile gets banned or loses access, they keep access to pages, pixels and ad accounts [L0637.009].
- Keep the same ad account and Business Manager for a store, rather than creating new ones for each product or test. A stable, consistently used account is less likely to be blocked [L0650.010].
- Separating Business Managers by product risk is a containment practice. Use a separate Business Manager for products that carry ban risk, such as products with health claims, revealing skin or controversial topics. Keep the main Business Manager for safe products and for other stores [L1347.021]. Do not use a separate Business Manager to run a product that the account is already restricted for.
- Do not link your personal profile to a new Business Manager, and do not send invitations between the old and new accounts. In one reported case this led to both accounts being blocked [L1347.022].
- Browser for Business Managers: use Brave for new Business Managers and keep Google Chrome for existing ones. Microsoft Edge and Safari (on a Mac) are also options for separating accounts [L1347.023].
- Do not create a brand-new personal profile to get around limits. Profiles with no history are often banned. The approach is a trusted friend or family member with an established profile who creates the Business Manager and adds you as admin or partner. This avoids the need for their profile login [L1213.013, L1218.013]. Do not use this arrangement to get around a restriction on an account, because that is circumvention of Meta's enforcement.
- Creating a second Meta Business Manager soon after the first can trigger a security restriction or suspension. If the account is restricted, request help through Get support on the Meta business help page and complete the identity verification Meta asks for. Do not open more accounts [L0806.105].

### Page Warm-Up

- A warmed-up Facebook Page and profile help, because Meta and visitors trust an established account more than a new one. Skipping the warm-up makes it harder for ads to run and convert [L1021.011].
- Fill the page with business information: phone number, business address, description and website link [L0650.012].
- Fill the About section in detail, including the website. Before running conversion campaigns, a new page should have around 10-20 likes. Avoid running ads right after the page is created [L0637.010, L0637.003].
- Warm up the page with 2-3 simple posts over 1-2 days before starting conversion campaigns. Good posts are happy or product-related content, or a screenshot or banner from the store, without hard sales messages or scammy claims such as weight-loss promises [L0637.011].
- Engagement posts can be a product photo with a positive quote and no traffic link [L0650.012].
- Keep warm-up creatives simple: product-only images or a positive quote with no people in them, because an image that shows a person can cause problems during warm-up [L0650.064].
- Make the page look legitimate and branded, so it does not look scammy to Meta or to users. A bare or generic page raises the risk of problems [L1477.003].
- The page is the asset that gets restricted first when ads are banned. One profile can run several products under one page, but each product needs a clear business identity behind it. Build the page and Business Manager as the trusted front of the store [L0637.058].
- Older (2021-22): Paid traffic networks are more likely to trust a store that shows a physical postal address in the footer. An address supports ad platform review, even though it may not be kept long term [L0681.012].
- Meta tends to prioritise conversion campaigns that send traffic off the platform, so new pages, Instagram accounts and Business Managers attract extra scrutiny. Every new asset linked to the ads adds risk of a temporary block [L0650.063].
- Launching a conversion campaign with purchases on a brand-new Facebook page and account, without any warm-up, was linked to a temporary block. A new page, a new Instagram account or a new Business Manager each adds risk, because Facebook does not trust new assets yet [L0650.013].
- A newly created Facebook page and Instagram account can be blocked from running ads, including remarketing, until the accounts show some activity. Post regularly on both before planning paid campaigns. Look in community groups for others who had the same block (2023) [L0804.112].

#### Instagram

- Creating a new Instagram account or connecting Instagram to the ads adds work and risk. A connected account must be posted to regularly and needs a real follower base [L0650.015].
- Running traffic-only ads without connecting Instagram sends visitors straight to the website [L0650.015].
- Messenger requires only the Facebook Page. Choosing Instagram as a message destination requires connecting an Instagram account first through the Connect account option [L0614.017].

#### Page Warm-Up Procedure

Warm up new Facebook assets before running conversion campaigns:

1. Secure the personal profile with two-factor authentication [L0650.012].
2. Fill the Facebook page with business information such as phone number, business address, description, and website link [L0650.012].
3. Create a separate business manager with business details rather than using the default personal one [L0650.012].
4. Complete business verification [L0650.012].
5. Run an engagement campaign of about $1 to $5 for one to two days with simple, positive posts (for example a product photo with a positive quote) and no traffic link [L0650.012].
6. Then move to conversion campaigns [L0650.012].

### Ad Account Warm-Up: Engagement Campaigns

Sources disagree on budget, duration and audience. Both approaches are recorded below. Use the Source D 2026 setup when you want a fixed Messenger-based warm-up with a defined stop point, and the Source C setup when you want the smallest possible spend.

| Setting | Source D 2026 approach | Source C approach |
|---|---|---|
| Budget | $10 daily for the simple setup [L0348.001, L0348.002]; $20.00 daily under Ad set budget, with Ads Manager showing a maximum of $35 per day and $140 per week [L0614.003] | $1-5 per day [L0637.012, L0650.012]; $5-10 per day in another source [L1213.001] |
| Stop point | Switch off once it has spent about $8 to $10 [L0614.008, L0614.012]; roughly $10 is spent on the warm-up in the simple setup [L0348.003] | Run for at least 1-2 days [L0637.012]; run for one to two days at most, because longer runs add low-quality audiences to the pixel data [L1213.001]; off after 2 days when published without the timeline [L0637.014] |
| Audience | Four countries (United States, Canada, Australia, United Kingdom) with the custom audience blank and Advantage+ audience on [L0348.002]; age 18-65+ [L0348.026]; or United States with Broad audience, estimated 73,700,000 to 86,700,000 [L0614.005] | Worldwide or United States, Canada and the top four countries, with broad targeting giving about 100 million to 500 million [L0637.012] |
| Creative | Image ad with one image [L0614.009]; animal picture such as a dog [L0614.007]; or a simple non-explicit image such as a company logo or neutral photo [L0348.003] | Store banner, smiley faces or a smiling dog, with a short positive quote as copy [L0637.012] |
| Message destination | Manual, Messenger [L0614.004] | Not specified |

#### Source D 2026 Engagement Warm-Up

- A small engagement campaign with a low budget can warm up a new or inactive Meta ad account before running campaigns that matter. The goal is account activity, not sales [L0614.001].
- Simple setting: after creating the pixel, open the ad account, press Create, then start an Engagement campaign with recommended settings and a budget of about $10. Later ads from the account should go active much faster [L0348.001].
- Ad set: target four countries (United States, Canada, Australia, United Kingdom), leave the custom audience field blank, keep the Advantage+ audience setting on, and use a daily budget of $10 [L0348.002].
- Age 18-65+ with Advantage+ audience switched on lets Meta show ads beyond the suggested audience when that is likely to improve results [L0348.026].
- Warm-up ad: untick the Advertiser Ads option, remove the media, upload any non-explicit simple image such as a company logo or a random neutral photo, publish, and wait for it to finish processing. This spends roughly $10 [L0348.003].
- Campaign objective: Engagement. Create a Manual engagement campaign instead of the recommended setup for finer control. The campaign name can stay at its default [L0614.002].
- Budget: set the Daily budget under Ad set budget to $20.00 USD. Ads Manager then shows a maximum daily spend of $35 and a maximum weekly spend of $140 for that setting [L0614.003].
- Budget strategy: Campaign budget (Advantage+ campaign budget, which spreads spend automatically across the campaign) or Ad set budget (set per ad set, with its own bid strategy and budget schedule options). For a single-ad warm-up, Ad set budget gives direct control over the Daily budget [L0614.015].
- Message destinations: choose Manual destination, not Automatic (recommended), then select Messenger. Automatic lets Meta send people to whichever messaging app they use most, so Messenger is not guaranteed [L0614.004]. Automatic can send people to Instagram instead of Messenger. Set Manual destination when Messenger must be the target [L0614.011]. Automatic sends people to whichever messaging app Meta expects will lower ad cost, while Manual limits the ad to the apps you tick [L0614.017].
- Audience definition: keep the setting on Broad rather than Narrow. Under Locations, add United States, which the tutorial recommends, but any audience works for a warm-up. Ads Manager showed an estimated audience of 73,700,000 to 86,700,000 with the broad setting [L0614.005]. Broad audiences can improve performance and reach more people likely to respond. Meta notes that its estimates do not include Advantage+ audience options and can change over time [L0614.006].
- Audience controls can be set for all campaigns in the ad account to reflect legal or practical constraints only, such as restricted locations or age ranges. They are not needed for a standard warm-up unless a constraint applies to the account [L0614.018].
- Ad format: image ad with one image. Ads Manager accepts one image or video per ad, and the image may be cropped automatically for Square, Vertical and Horizontal placements [L0614.009].
- Call to action: Send message. People who see the ad can message the Page directly, which matches the goal of an engagement ad that starts conversations in Messenger [L0614.010].
- Identity: the field sets which profile appears in the ad, usually the Facebook Page that represents the business. Messages started from the ad are routed to the chosen destinations, so the Page must be the one that will answer the customer [L0614.021].
- Partnership ads and Multi-advertiser ads are separate toggles that default to Off. Keep both Off for a plain warm-up ad. Partnership ads use signals from both accounts, and Multi-advertiser ads can appear beside other advertisers and may crop or resize the creative [L0614.022].
- Switch the warm-up engagement ad off once it has spent about $8 to $10 in total. Letting it keep running after that spends budget without any further purpose [L0614.008, L0614.012].

Warm-up engagement campaign in Meta Ads Manager:

1. Go to Campaigns, click + Create, and choose the engagement objective [L0614.007].
2. Select Manual engagement campaign instead of Recommended settings [L0614.007].
3. Leave the campaign name at its default [L0614.007].
4. Set the Daily budget under Ad set budget to $20.00 USD [L0614.007].
5. Under Message destinations, select Manual destination and tick Messenger [L0614.007].
6. Under Locations, add United States and keep Audience definition on Broad [L0614.007].
7. Under Ad creative, choose Create image ad, select one picture of an animal (for example a dog), and upload it [L0614.007].
8. Enter a short headline (see the pattern unit for the headline style) [L0614.007].
9. Click Publish and accept Meta's terms [L0614.007].
10. Turn the ad off once it has spent about $8 to $10 [L0614.007].

#### Source C Optional Warm-Up

Ad account warm-up, as an optional safety step:

1. Create a single campaign with one ad set and one image ad [L0637.012].
2. Set the budget at $1-5 per day for an engagement campaign [L0637.012].
3. Target either worldwide or the United States, Canada and the top four countries, keeping the targeting broad, which gives an audience of about 100 million to 500 million [L0637.012].
4. Use a simple image such as a store banner, smiley faces or a smiling dog, with a short positive quote as copy [L0637.012].
5. Run it for at least 1-2 days [L0637.012].
6. Then start conversion campaigns [L0637.012].

- Post engagement campaigns used only to warm up a new ad account or page cost about $5 to $10 per day and are optional. Run them for one to two days at most, because longer runs add low-quality audiences to the pixel data [L1213.001].
- To run a warm-up engagement ad without publishing it on the main page, use the normal ad creation flow, select the page as the advertising identity, and upload the image directly. The ad is not published to the page's timeline and can be switched off after 2 days [L0637.014].
- Running conversion-objective campaigns directly, without a warm-up engagement campaign, can still produce good results and engagement. Engagement campaigns can help in pet, kids and some fashion niches but are not required [L1213.002].
- Large advertisers spending roughly $100k to $1M or more can skip warm-up and launch campaigns directly. Smaller accounts should still warm up for 1-2 days before conversion campaigns [L0637.061].
- Large, established accounts with a long payment history can skip warm-up, but new accounts should not [L0637.013].

### Warm-Up Sequence and Timing

#### Full Warm-Up Sequence (Source D 2026)

Warm-up sequence for a new Meta ad account:

1. Log in to the Facebook profile only from one fixed device and one fixed IP address, using the same proxy every time, with no VPN switching [L1477.001, L1477.002].
2. Set up the page so it looks legitimate and branded, not scammy [L1477.001, L1477.003].
3. After the ad account is created, wait 1 to 2 hours before launching any ad [L1477.001, L1477.004].
4. Start with a $20 budget and let it spend about $8 to $10 before launching sales ads [L1477.001, L1477.005].
5. Spend small amounts first and avoid aggressive scaling: Day 1 spend $10 to $20, Day 2 spend $20 to $30, Day 3 onward run normal campaigns [L1477.001, L1477.006].
6. Do not run high budgets during the warm-up and do not edit business settings too much too early [L1477.001].

Rules that apply to this sequence:

- Always log in to the Facebook profile from the same device and the same IP address, and use the same proxy every single time. Switching VPNs or IP addresses between logins is a risk factor for Meta [L1477.002].
- Start a new ad account with a small spend, for example a $20 budget, and wait until about $8 to $10 has been spent before launching sales ads. Avoid aggressive scaling in the first days [L1477.005].
- Do not run high budgets right away on a new or warming ad account. Increase spend gradually, following the schedule of $10 to $20 on Day 1 and $20 to $30 on Day 2, before running normal campaigns from Day 3 [L1477.006].
- Before moving to sales campaigns, run an engagement campaign first to warm up the pixel and collect website activity data [L1027.005].

#### Waiting Times by Source

Each waiting time applies to a different action, so they do not conflict.

| Action | Waiting time | Source ids |
|---|---|---|
| Before any ad on a new ad account (Source C) | 3-7 days, do not advertise on day one | L0296.031 |
| After creating a new ad account (Source D 2026) | 1-2 hours before launching any ad | L1477.001, L1477.004 |
| After editing business profile or business settings | 1-2 hours before further changes | L1477.009 |
| After a payment method fails | About 1 hour before trying again | L1477.007 |
| Engagement warm-up before conversion campaigns | 1-2 days | L0637.012, L0650.012, L0650.064 |
| Page posts before conversion campaigns | 2-3 posts over 1-2 days | L0637.011 |
| Before creating Business Manager and ad account (feed scrolling) | 15-30 minutes of scrolling the news feed | L0351.035 |

- Scale spending gradually over weeks rather than jumping to large budgets immediately [L0296.031].
- Do not launch 10-15 ad sets or campaigns on day one of a new ad account. That pattern can trigger Meta's automated restriction. Start small and scale slowly [L0637.013].
- Do not edit the business profile or business settings in quick succession early on. Many edits in a short time raise the risk of a ban [L1477.009].

#### Full Warm-Up Procedure (Source C, Prevention Sequence)

Prevention sequence for a new Meta ad setup, in order:

1. Use a personal profile that is at least 2 years old, not a new dummy profile [L0637.003].
2. Confirm the profile identity with an identity document [L0637.003].
3. Enable two-factor authentication on the profile [L0637.003].
4. Create a separate Business Manager at business.facebook.com/create, not a personal ad account [L0637.003].
5. Verify the business in Business settings with a business document (LLC, Ltd or similar) [L0637.003].
6. Add 1-2 trusted people to the Business Manager with page and pixel rights [L0637.003].
7. Fill in the page's About section fully, reach 10-20 page likes, and post 2-3 simple posts over 1-2 days before any conversion campaign [L0637.003].
8. Complete the free Facebook Advertising Policy Blueprint course and keep its certificate [L0637.003].
9. Optionally warm up the ad account with an engagement campaign [L0637.003].
10. Do not copy competitor creatives exactly [L0637.003].
11. Keep the feedback score above 3 by communicating well and setting shipping time correctly [L0637.003].
12. Reply fast to comments and messages [L0637.003].
13. Pay every bill on time [L0637.003].

#### Full Account Setup Procedure (Source C)

To reduce account suspension risk on Meta: complete account verification and profile verification, provide detailed business information, wait 3-7 days before running any ads (do not advertise on day one), and scale spending gradually over weeks rather than jumping to large budgets immediately [L0296.031]. Meta can ban a new advertiser, and the steps below are the setup sequence for it [L0296.004]:

1. Do not run ads from a bare personal account [L0341.027].
2. Set up a business profile in Meta Business [L0341.027].
3. Enter your business details and provide as much accurate information as you are willing to share [L0341.027].
4. Set up two-factor authentication, especially for any admin accounts [L0341.027].
5. Complete identity verification when requested [L0341.027].

Meta trusts accounts that look legitimate, and it associates bad experiences with the platform [L0341.027].

### Payments and Spend Limits

- Payment failure is a serious risk on Meta. A card declined automatically by Facebook can lead to an immediate account ban. Always have two payment methods on the ad account, a main one and a backup if the first fails, because repeated failures lead to a ban [L0691.013].
- Do not add payment methods repeatedly in a row. If a payment method does not work, wait about 1 hour before trying again, because repeated attempts lead to a ban [L1477.007].
- Adding a card for ad payments can fail with an insufficient-funds error even when the card has enough money, which can then restrict the ad account. The cause was unconfirmed, so check the payment setup before spending on a new account [L0709.033].
- Pay every bill on time [L0637.003].
- A personal profile that cannot add a valid payment method may be banned, and the linked business account can then be banned too, even after business verification is completed. Verification alone does not protect an ad account if its payment method is invalid or fails [L0691.032].
- A new Facebook ad account has a spending limit that increases only after payments are made on time. If the ad account stops spending on a day, check the payment method in the payment settings, because a failed payment can silently stop delivery [L1216.009].
- A CBO or ABO campaign that spends far below its daily limit can have a payment or account-history problem. Check that the ad account is set up correctly, and that no payments to Facebook are overdue in the payment gateway. If the problem persists, duplicate the campaign and run the copy [L1220.001].
- Suspensions for unusual activity may require verifying your card again. Keep business details and payments fully legitimate to reduce risk [L0950.055].

#### Spending Limit Ladder

- A personal-profile Business Manager starts with a 50 USD spending limit and rises progressively to 100, 150 and 250 USD before the limit is removed [L1347.018].
- Spending limits are raised by making payments on time and staying consistent, without billing failures. Add two payment methods, so that if the first one fails the second one is charged [L1347.019].
- A seller's credit card was compromised and replaced, which caused several payment failures on Facebook. The seller believed these failures kept the spending limit low for longer, and then added a second payment method to avoid this in the future [L1347.020].
- Late payment on the ad account can impose a spending limit. In one case the limit was 50 USD per day, rising about 130 USD per day weekly, but it reset to 15 USD per day after just a few account actions such as adding an ad set or duplicating a campaign [L1212.033].
- Making changes to a restricted Facebook account, such as adding an ad set or duplicating a campaign, can drop the daily spend limit back to a lower level within 24 hours [L1212.035].

### Trust Tiers and Asset Ranking

- Meta assigns advertisers spend tiers based on account age, Business Manager history and successful payment history. Very large advertisers spending roughly $100k to $1M or more can skip warm-up and launch campaigns directly [L0637.061].
- Meta ranks business assets such as the Business Manager, Facebook pages and ad accounts by their spend history and places them in trust tiers. An asset with a longer, steadier spend record is trusted more [L0650.062].
- Facebook was described as relatively lenient with copied creatives in general-store testing. TikTok and Pinterest were described as banning accounts much faster for the same behaviour [L0660.027].

### Feedback Score and Shipping Time

- Meta assigns the feedback score after a store has about 50-100 sales, on a scale of 0 to 5. It is judged from product quality, shipping times and customer communication, and is collected from direct customers via feedback forms [L0637.017].
- Feedback score thresholds [L0637.018]:
  - Below 2 restricts advertising for a page that is less than 1 year old.
  - A page below 2 can be restricted from running ads.
  - A score above 3 is considered good. Scores of 3-4.5 are the typical healthy range.
  - Accounts below 3 for several weeks risk a ban.
- Check the feedback score and any restrictions in Business Manager > Account Quality. You can also see ad rejections and each asset's status there. Shipping time is set in the same Account Quality area, below the feedback score [L0637.019].
- Set shipping time in Business Manager > Account Quality, on the page's section, for your store. Set it to more than 6 weeks, up to 8 weeks, because Meta sends the feedback form only after the shipping time has passed [L0637.020].
- If the shipping time is set too short, feedback forms reach customers before the parcel arrives and generate bad ratings [L0637.021].
- Keep the real shipping time under 2 weeks with good suppliers and agents, but set the platform setting longer as a buffer [L0637.021]. The platform setting (6-8 weeks) and the real shipping time (under 2 weeks) are two different values for two different purposes.
- Keep the feedback score above 3 by communicating well and setting shipping time correctly [L0637.003]. Reply fast to comments and messages [L0637.003].

### Policy-Risk Habits

#### Creatives and Claims

- Ad creatives and missing payments are the most common causes of Meta bans. Follow Meta's rules on prohibited content such as nudity and before-and-after images [L0691.017].
- Avoid health promises or other claims in ad text that Facebook may flag, since this can lead to the account being blocked [L0650.011].
- Do not make scammy claims such as weight-loss promises in page posts or ad copy [L0637.011].
- Check how a product looks in ad creatives and listing photos before running it on Facebook. Facebook can flag a product that looks like a weapon, such as a walking stick that resembles a gun, and the flag can lead to another account ban [L1440.058].
- Running random, unvetted ads can get the whole ad account banned. Strict product research and ad checks protect the account from bans [L1131.022].
- Selling branded products or running copied product images from AliExpress is a common cause of Facebook bans. Use only unbranded products [L1431.038].
- Do not copy competitor creatives exactly [L0637.003].
- Meta does not algorithmically compare new ad videos against other advertisers' videos to detect copies.
