The practical risk of reuse comes from a competitor reporting the ad, either through a DMCA notice or an ad report. This usually happens when the competitor is already in a dispute or was banned earlier [L1218.001].
- Posting an identical ad file from a different account can link the accounts, because the ad files share metadata. If one ad is banned, the linked ad can be banned too [L0361.028]. The source suggested changing the file metadata or making a small change to the video before uploading. This is not recommended, because it is an attempt to hide a connection that Meta can detect. Run each account's ads from that account's own original creatives [L0361.028, L1195.025].
- Running identical creatives across several accounts or with many sellers can trigger Meta to restrict the Business Manager. In severe cases, copyright complaints can take down the Shopify store and ad accounts within days [L1195.025].

#### Products and Testing

- Use a separate ad account, or a separate Facebook account, for testing new products instead of your personal ad account. Testing products that get flagged can ban the account [L1173.011].
- Products that carry ban risk, such as products with health claims, revealing skin or controversial topics, belong in a separate Business Manager (see Business Manager section) [L1347.021].

#### Compliance Habits

- Facebook accounts that did not follow Facebook's ad prevention practices have a higher chance of being banned. Following the practices reduces the ban risk. The coach reported few ban problems among mentorship members who follow them [L0363.056].
- Complete the free Facebook Advertising Policy Blueprint course and keep its certificate [L0637.003].
- Keep business details and payments fully legitimate [L0950.055].

### Login, Device and IP Hygiene

- Log in to the Facebook profile only from one fixed device and one fixed IP address, using the same proxy every time [L1477.001].
- Always log in from the same device and the same IP address, and use the same proxy every single time. Switching VPNs or IP addresses between logins is a risk factor for Meta [L1477.002].
- Logging in from multiple devices is a common cause of instant bans. Avoid it in the first phase of an account [L1477.008].
- Using a VPN or proxy on Meta is a fast route to a block. For a US target, choose USA in the ad set targeting and do not use a VPN [L0691.016].
- Conflicting advice: one source suggested that, after a flagged IP or card history, a fresh agency account and a VPN could be used to get back to scaling. This is not recommended, because it evades Meta's enforcement and leads to permanent loss of assets. Use the consistent-device, consistent-IP approach above instead [L0351.036, L1477.002, L0691.016].
- A flagged payment card or IP history, such as a DMCA record tied to an area, can cause repeated bans on new Business Managers and ad accounts [L0351.036].
- Before creating a Business Manager and ad account, one source recommended scrolling the news feed for about 15 to 30 minutes. This was described as reducing bans on new accounts from a flagged IP or card [L0351.035].

### What Causes Instant Bans and Cascades

#### Common Triggers

- Common causes of instant Meta account bans are logging in through a VPN, logging in from multiple devices, repeatedly adding payment methods, running high budgets immediately, and editing business settings too much too early. Avoid all of these in the first phase of an account [L1477.008].
- Launching 10-15 ad sets or campaigns on day one of a new ad account [L0637.013].
- Payment failure, where a card declined automatically by Facebook can lead to an immediate ban [L0691.013].
- Ad creatives and missing payments, the most common causes of bans [L0691.017].
- Not completing identity verification in time, which can lead to an instant suspension [L0341.026, L0343.021].
- A non-admin user without two-factor authentication on a business account [L0343.023].
- Running random, unvetted ads [L1131.022].
- Selling branded or copied products [L1431.038].
- A product that looks like a weapon [L1440.058].
- Creating a second Business Manager soon after the first [L0806.105].
- Launching a conversion campaign on a brand-new page and account without warm-up [L0650.013].

#### Cascades

- Account bans on Meta can cascade. A personal profile that cannot add a valid payment method may be banned, and the linked business account can then be banned too, even after business verification is completed [L0691.032].
- A ban on the ad account or Business Manager should not take down the personal profile, but a personal profile ban can block Facebook use entirely [L0637.006].
- Linked accounts can be banned together when identical ad files share metadata [L0361.028].

### Recovery

- If an account is restricted, request help through Get support on the Meta business help page and complete the identity verification Meta asks for, instead of opening more accounts [L0806.105].
- When Meta asks for ID verification after a ban, it checks that a real, legitimate person runs the ads. Verify identity early so the platform already knows who you are [L0637.002].
- Recovery solutions exist for properly set-up accounts that do face suspension [L0296.033].
- Keep the Business Manager separate from the personal profile so that a Business Manager shutdown does not take down everything else. In the worst case, a new Business Manager can be created [L0637.006].
- Do not open a new personal profile to get around a restriction, and do not open more accounts after a restriction. Both approaches are circumvention of enforcement [L1213.013, L0806.105].

#### Further details

- Account blocking occurs when ad quality scores drop below Facebook's normal threshold; Facebook determines quality through satisfaction surveys sent to recent customers about their purchase experience. Maintain account health by monitoring and improving performance. [L0238.005]

## Agency, Purchased and Alternative Ad Accounts

### When to Use an Agency Account or a Personal Account

- Personal ad accounts have spending caps and face random account restrictions. This distinction becomes critical when you prepare to scale real volume. [L0216.001]
- Agency accounts handle rising ad spend more reliably than personal accounts. They isolate business spending from personal-account policy enforcement and from platform-imposed architectural limits. [L0216.002]
- Agency accounts get higher trust and spending limits than personal accounts. They are far less likely to be restricted or shut down at random, and this matters most for a store about to start running large ad volume. [L1253.002]
- Agency and personal accounts perform the same on Meta. The choice depends only on how you pay and how much you scale. [L1028.001]
- Both account types connect a Shopify store to Meta through the same Facebook & Instagram setup. The choice affects payment and scaling, not the tracking connection. [L1028.001, L1028.018]
- Personal ad accounts vary extremely in stability. Some run without restrictions for years, while others are terminated or restricted the same day they launch. This unpredictability is itself a major risk. [L0216.013]
- Using a personal account for scaling is risky. It has no support and cannot be recovered after a ban. Meta support will not engage unless the account has Meta Verified (blue tick), and account age is considered. [L0351.034]
- A personal account paid by credit card is the simpler starting point for a store with small or moderate ad spend. [L1028.003]
- An agency account can technically be paid by credit card, but this is more cumbersome than with a personal account. [L1028.003]
- Use an agency account only in these cases:
  1. All personal ad accounts are banned and you cannot create new ones. [L0335.048]
  2. You hit an ad account spend cap after trying other ways to raise it. [L0335.048]
  3. The product is risky, such as beauty, weight loss or health products. [L0335.048]
- An agency account is useful when you want to scale above about $100,000 of ad spend per month, because agency support helps at that level. For normal spend it is not necessary. [L0335.049]
- When ad spend is scaled to very high numbers, you will eventually need to switch to an agency ad account. Plan this switch before it becomes urgent. [L1028.004]

### Payment and Deposits

- A personal ad account can be paid directly with a credit card. An agency ad account works like a debit source: you must deposit funds into it to have a balance before ads can run. [L1028.002]
- Opening deposits differ by provider:
  - Ruas Agency: $250 or $500. [L0216.004]
  - Kuro Network: $500 opening transfer. [L0351.041]
- Ruas Agency accounts are funded by international bank transfer or cryptocurrency, arranged through WhatsApp coordination with the agency contact. [L0216.010, L0216.008]
- Kuro agency accounts are topped up by crypto or bank transfer. The first top-up requires the $500 opening transfer. [L0351.041]
- For Kuro, the $500 opening transfer is created only after payment is confirmed. [L0351.037]

### Providers, Pricing and Terms

#### Ruas Agency

- Ruas Agency provides stable, enterprise-grade ad account infrastructure. Setup starts by texting the agency contact on WhatsApp. [L0216.005]
- Package B costs $250 per month plus 4% of total ad spend, with no setup fee. [L0216.003]
- Timezone for Ruas Agency accounts must be EST (Eastern Standard Time). [L0216.007]
- Switching from Kuro to ROAS Agency is optional, and staying on Kuro is also possible. ROAS Agency is offered as the more stable option because of recurring issues with Kuro. Choose it if account stability is the priority. [L1253.001]
- Staying with free ad account solutions such as Kuro, while facing recurring stability issues, costs more in lost time than Ruas Agency's $250 monthly fee. Predictable infrastructure and responsive support accelerate recovery from account problems. [L0216.012]

#### Kuro Network

Tier figures are partly unreadable in the source. Blank cells mean the figure is not shown.

| Tier | Price | Spend cap | Key features |
|---|---|---|---|
| Traction | $299/month | up to $5,000 monthly spend | 0% management fee, free setup, unlimited ad account replacement, crypto or bank top-up, you run your own campaigns, monthly rolling, cancel anytime |
| Motion | $499/month | higher cap, exact figure unreadable | managed Meta ad account, 0% management fee, unlimited ad account replacement, crypto or bank top-up, monthly rolling |
| Surge | $599/month | higher cap, exact figure unreadable | managed Meta ad account, free setup, unlimited ad account replacement, dedicated support rep on standby, priority policy and reinstatement queue, 1 month deposit required |
| Legacy | $2,500 one-time | no spend ceiling, client billed | takeover of an account, clients add their own cards, dedicated support, priority policy and reinstatement queue, 1 month deposit required |

- Each additional ad account costs $50 per month on top of the Traction plan. [L0351.038]
- The Traction tier suits early testing with daily deposits up to about $5,000 per month. Move to a higher tier only when spend and cash flow justify it. [L0351.039]
- A one-time $2,500 Legacy account is worth it for large spend, because the tax savings on high ad budgets outweigh the upfront cost. For small or testing budgets, a monthly tier is more practical. [L0351.040]
- Kuro's ad account request process is as follows:
  1. Name: enter the ad account name. [L0351.037]
  2. Industry: select the industry. [L0351.037]
  3. URL and ads: enter the store URL and the ads details. [L0351.037]
  4. Confirm: review and confirm, then send the opening transfer. [L0351.037]
- The first ad account is included in the plan. [L0351.037]

#### Pricing Comparison Across Sources

| Source | Provider | Monthly fee | Spend fee | Applies to |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Source D 2026 | Ruas Agency Package B | $250 | 4% of total ad spend | Ruas Package B |
| Source C | Agency accounts (general) | about $250 per ad account | about 1% | Agency accounts in general, as stated in that source |

- The two sources conflict on the spend fee (4% versus about 1%). Use the Ruas figures for a Ruas Package B quote, and treat the Source C figures as approximate general figures. Confirm the current quote with the provider before budgeting. [L0216.003, L0637.029]

#### Other Providers

- Providers named in the source are Scale Infinity, ROAS Agency and HTTP Pool. Pricing is about $250 per ad account per month, plus about a 1% ad spend fee. High spending limits are a feature; one example account had a limit of $50,000. [L0637.029]
- Agency accounts come from marketing agencies that already have a direct relationship with Meta. They are less likely to be banned, and the agency handles verification and appeals. Recovery of a banned agency account can take 1 to 2 hours to a day through the agency. [L0637.028]
- Third-party providers such as ACC farm, ROAS agency and Scale Infinity sell aged Business Managers, profiles and ad accounts. Their use carries ban and policy risk, so treat them only as a last-resort fallback. [L1213.014]
- Agency-tier account providers are reported to replace banned accounts within one or two hours. Large agencies can also ask Meta's marketing team for agency accounts through direct contacts or LinkedIn outreach. [L1213.015]

### Referral Setup and First Contact

- If someone referred you, name them in your first message to the agency contact. If nobody referred you, skip the referral mention. [L1253.004]
- Request template for an agency ad account under Package B, citing the referral contact:

```
Request an agency ad account under Package B, citing your coach from Source D as your referral contact.
```
[L0216.006]

- First message template, stating the Package B request and the referral name:

```
In your first message to the agency contact, state that you want an agency ad account on Package B, and include the name of the person who referred you if there is one.
```
[L1253.005]

### Opening a Ruas Agency Account

1. Contact the agency contact on WhatsApp, stating that you want Package B and were referred by your coach from Source D. [L0216.008]
2. Gather the required details: your ad account name, website URL, Business Manager ID (BM ID), currency, timezone (EST), and initial deposit amount ($250 or $500). [L0216.008, L0216.004, L0216.007]
3. Submit these details to the agency contact when he requests them. [L0216.008]
4. the agency contact configures the account and provides access through Facebook Business Manager, linked to your BM ID. [L0216.008]
5. Coordinate with the agency contact via WhatsApp to complete the funding, choosing international bank transfer or cryptocurrency. [L0216.008, L0216.010]
6. Once the account is funded, you can begin running advertising campaigns. [L0216.008]

- The same process in prose: text the agency contact to say you want an agency ad account. Ask for Package B, which has no setup fee, costs $250 per month, and charges a 4% fee on ad spend. Prepare the ad account name, URL, Client BM (Business Manager ID), currency, timezone and first top-up amount, and send them over. After you submit, the agency contact creates the ad account and gives access in your Facebook Business Manager under the Business Manager ID you provided. Fund the account by international bank transfer or crypto, arranging the top-up over WhatsApp. Once the top-up arrives, you can start running ads. [L1253.004]
- Reply to the agency contact with the setup details he asks for, filled in:

```
Ad account name: [name of the ad account]
URL: [store URL]
Client BM: [Business Manager ID]
Currency: [account currency]
Timezone: [account timezone]
First topup: [amount of the first top-up]
```
[L1253.006]

- In Facebook Business Manager, open the Ruas Agency ad account via the left sidebar: Accounts > Ad accounts. [L0216.009]
- After the agency contact sets up the account, it should appear under Accounts > Ad accounts. The same menu also lists Pages, Business asset groups, Apps and Commerce accounts. [L1253.007]

### Agency Compensation Structures

- Structure agency compensation as base compensation plus performance-based incentives, not as a percentage of ad spend alone. The base covers labour; incentives tie the upside to business outcomes. [L0217.025]
- If you use percentage-of-spend compensation, implement caps that reset the structure at higher revenue levels. Otherwise the agency earns exponentially more while doing similar work as you scale. [L0217.026]
- Verify that the agency has a coherent media buying methodology or doctrine that guides all its decisions. Examples are running everything on cost-cap automation, or using AOV multipliers to set spend minimums per creative. A consistent philosophy is better than ad-hoc changes. [L0217.043]

### Risks of Buying or Renting Accounts

- Buying ad accounts from third parties carries a risk of bans and loss of access, which can stop a store's advertising completely. Keep a clean, owned ad account and keep the ID verified. [L0774.048]
- Running Facebook ads through purchased ad accounts combined with a residential proxy was reported to lead to account bans and considerable instability. Account bans are a real risk for this approach, so a plan should not depend on a single ad account. [L0819.030]
- Ad accounts can be bought from a vendor when an account has problems, but this is a ban risk because the same ad policies still apply. [L1171.015]
- Some sellers on Fiverr offer access to Facebook ad accounts for $20 to $30 each. Purchased accounts risk restriction under Meta's policies, so treat this as a high-risk option, separate from your main business. [L1185.010]
- Pre-warmed Facebook ad accounts bought from Fiverr cost around 80 to 90 USD each. The buyer carries the ban risk. The lower price is considered a fair trade-off compared with subscription services charging 500 to 600 USD. [L1212.036]
- Cheap pre-warmed accounts (about 80 to 90 USD each) carry ban risk that falls entirely on the buyer. Use them only for products with little ban risk, and avoid products likely to get an account blocked. [L1212.049]
- Buying an existing Facebook ad account from a third party can bring its restriction history with it. If a purchased account spends far below its set budget, check its restriction status and payment setup before scaling spend. [L1220.044]
- A new personal Facebook profile could not create a Business Manager or ad account in one country. Access to established family accounts was used as a workaround, but it depends on those accounts staying active. [L1431.040]

### Renting Access to Client Accounts

- Access is gained by asking the client to give you access to their Facebook account, or to add you to their ad account. You can then run ads through a business account without using your own ad account. [L0440.031]
- Use the client's own ad account when you want to protect your own ad account from problems. A ban on one account could otherwise damage the other as well. [L0440.032]

### Account Priority Order After a Ban

1. Use your own profile first. [L0361.046]
2. If it is banned, use an old profile from a relative or friend you trust. It should look like a real profile and not have been created recently, for example not one month old. [L0361.046]
3. Use an agency account as the last option, because it is expensive. [L0361.046]

- If an ad account or Facebook page is banned during a test, create a new account and relaunch the same product. Keep access to multiple Facebook accounts so one ban does not stop sales. [L1173.012]
- Facebook accounts can be banned even when the first ads run without issues. Plan a backup account process and consider TikTok as a parallel platform. [L1436.082]

### Other Platforms

- TikTok agency accounts allow targeting across several countries, including Europe and Canada, without VPN workarounds. Agency accounts are the easiest way to target multiple regions. [L0950.058]

## Ban Recovery, Appeals and Meta Support

### Ban recovery: first moves

- Open Ads Manager, click the ban notice at the top, then See details. This leads to Business settings, where you choose Request review. [L0637.024]
- If you know the reason (ad issue or billing issue), explain it in the appeal. If you do not, paste the appeal template below. [L0637.024]
- Use Meta's appeal forms where available: ad account, page restriction, or business restriction. [L0637.024, L0637.026]
- Use the form that matches the restricted asset, and describe the business in detail. [L0637.026]
- Check replies in the Support inbox, which is in the dropdown on your personal profile. [L0637.024]
- If the account is not reinstated, appeal again and make a final, polite request for a human review. [L0637.024]
- Before appealing, check the store against the ad policy and state the reason in the appeal, because some suspensions are false flags. [L0468.109] (older, 2020)
- Read Meta's terms of service, make sure you follow them, then submit the appeal and let the process run. Many suspensions were resolved when the account was not doing anything wrong. [L0801.081] (older, 2021)

### Appeal wording

Template for a banned ad account:

```
I believe you have mistakenly disabled my ad account. My account does not violate any Facebook terms of service. Please have a human review it. Thanks.
```

[L0637.025]

- In the appeal, state that the block was a mistake and ask for a manual review. [L0650.014]
- Use a final, polite request for a human review on the last attempt. [L0637.024]

### Restricted and blocked pages

- When the Facebook page behind the ads is blocked, the ad sets show ad errors and no ads can run until the block is resolved. [L0650.065]
- Submit the appeal for the page itself before changing anything in the ad sets. [L0650.065]
- Page restrictions can follow multiple ad rejections or a feedback score below 2. Use the page restriction appeal form. [L0637.026]
- A flagged Facebook commerce account does not block remarketing ads that rely on a linked product feed. [L0962.074] (older, 2021)

### Commerce account rejections

- If a commerce account is rejected, work through the checklist first:
  1. Confirm that every required element is on the site, such as required footer links and business information. [L0803.050] (older, 2022)
  2. If nothing obvious is missing, keep contacting Facebook through live chat in Meta Business Manager. [L0803.050] (older, 2022)
  3. Keep working through the checklist until someone identifies the specific reason for rejection. [L0803.050] (older, 2022)
- For a rejected store review, read the commerce eligibility policy carefully, then request a review and state that your store complies after reviewing the policy. [L0855.142]

### Identity verification

- Upload identity documents when requesting a review of a blocked account. [L0650.014]
- Ask for a manual review when you appeal. [L0650.014]
- For a disabled account that requests identity verification:
  1. Upload the front and back of the required ID. [L0963.077] (older, 2022)
  2. Wait for a human review, which can take days. [L0963.077] (older, 2022)
  3. If the resolution link fails, contact Business Manager support through live chat to reach a person. [L0963.077] (older, 2022)
- A ban for an ID issue can often be resolved by submitting ID for review, which typically takes 24 to 48 hours. If the review does not restore the account, contact the account provider or the support contact for another solution. [L0774.047]
- For an early-phase ban, upload a personal ID to recover the account. Recovery often takes one to two days. [L1431.035]
- The Facebook account name must match the name on the personal ID used for verification exactly. Stylized or fancy names are rejected. [L1431.036]
- For identity verification, when asked which ad categories you run (social issues, elections or politics), select 'None of the above' and complete two-factor authentication. Choosing the political or social issue option only complicates the review. [L1218.014]
- For Instagram accounts disabled for misrepresentation with no posts, submit the business license and resale certificate, and verify identity with the requested photo and security code. Verification can take a few days to about a week, after which a person usually reviews the case and the account is restored. [L1090.112] (older, 2021)

**Reported review and reinstatement times, by route**

| Route | Reported time | Source |
|---|---|---|
| ID review for an ID-related ban | 24 to 48 hours | [L0774.047] |
| Personal ID after an early-phase ban | One to two days | [L1431.035] |
| Identity verification with human review | Days | [L0963.077] (older, 2022) |
| Contacting Facebook software engineers through LinkedIn | Two to three days | [L0650.014] |
| After the offending ad is removed and review is requested | Hours to about two days | [L1431.039] |
| Instagram misrepresentation verification | A few days to about a week | [L1090.112] (older, 2021) |

Sources disagree on the timing because they describe different routes and ban types. Use the row that matches your case. [L0774.047, L1431.035, L0963.077, L0650.014, L1431.039, L1090.112]

### Live chat and support routes

- Chat support from a Facebook representative is normally provided only after spending $5,000 to $10,000. Beginners without this spending level must rely on automated review processes. [L0296.005]
- Older advice says Facebook account representatives can help advertisers with spend history. Use this only when you have that spend history. [L1111.098] (older, 2020)
- To reach Facebook advertising support, go to Business > Help > Support, select your issue, and use the live messenger option when offered. It is only available when support is open. [L0950.056]
- Facebook support is slow and first-line. Persistent follow-up is often needed, and a verification review can take a long time, so plan for waiting. [L0950.057]
- Meta Business Support live chat is available through the Business Center. If chat is not shown, retry later in the day, since availability changes with time. [L855.145]
- Meta Business Support is the official help centre for Meta ad accounts, Business Manager, and advertising policy questions. Use it when an ad or account is restricted and the reason is unclear. [L1105.016]
- For a restricted ad account:
  1. Log in to the ad account. [L0804.080] (2023)
  2. Open the support flow and go through the problems you have until a live chat option appears. [L0804.080] (2023)
  3. Accept the callback if offered. Live chat was reported as faster than the account rep. [L0804.080] (2023)
- Meta Business Support live chat can ask for a phone number and then call the account holder back. Use this to escalate account problems that chat cannot solve. Callbacks may come from an unfamiliar number, sometimes in another country, so expect an unknown caller. [L0666.051] (2024)
- For a disabled Facebook account (older, 2020):
  1. Open Facebook's support page. [L1088.076] (older, 2020)
  2. Under the question about being an advertiser, choose support. [L1088.076] (older, 2020)
  3. Accept the wait of about nine minutes for the live chat team, then ask for a review of the disabled account. [L1088.076] (older, 2020)
- For a new ad account that Meta disables at creation (2023):
  1. Use the official support page in Business Manager, since there is no reliable shortcut beyond it. [L0470.098] (2023)
  2. Check that page about once a day and resubmit the request, because reaching a reviewer can take several attempts. [L0470.098] (2023)
  3. Ask paid-ads communities for working contact routes as a supplement to the official channel. [L0470.098] (2023)
- Support paths differ by source and date. Use the route that is open to you. [L0950.056, L0470.098, L1088.076, L0804.080, L0855.145]
- Live chat is often not shown because the queue is too long. Retry it each day. [L0468.108] (older, 2020)
- When you reach the agent, ask what caused the ad account or store disapproval and whether it can be fixed. Stay polite and say the issue has been resolved and will not happen again. [L0468.108] (older, 2020)

### Recovery and securing the account

- To recover and secure a Meta ad account after a ban:
  1. Complete business verification. [L0691.015]
  2. Add a valid payment method. [L0691.015]
  3. Make sure the personal account is linked to the Business Manager so the business and personal accounts are merged correctly. [L0691.015]
  4. Create the pixel inside the Business Manager and connect it to the Shopify store. [L0691.015]
  5. Add a second trusted person as an admin on the Business Manager and ad account. [L0691.015]
  6. Read Meta's advertising policies before publishing creatives. [L0691.015]
  7. Keep two payment methods on file. [L0691.015]
- For a reinstated or new ad account, the pixel must be created inside the Business Manager, not on the personal account. The personal profile is only used to open the Business Manager. The Business Manager holds the ad account and the pixel, and business verification must be completed. [L0691.014]
- Confirm the pixel shows in the Shopify pixel dropdown before creating campaigns. [L0691.014]
- Recovery case: a Meta ad account was fully locked after the business account was registered under the company name, the personal profile was not linked to the Business Manager, and the Shopify pixel sat on the business account. Recovery went through Meta's support channels and business verification. The personal and business accounts were then merged, a new payment method was added, and campaigns were held back until the setup was confirmed correct. [L0691.035]
- After a Meta account ban, recover the banned account before creating new accounts. New accounts on the same identity and card are usually blocked. [L0855.143]
- Do not try to evade a permanent ban by creating a new setup, new profiles or bought accounts. This violates Meta's terms, and the account and profile trust work of this procedure stops being useful. Focus on a proper appeal instead. [L0637.027]
- A restart from a fresh profile often gets banned again. The problem is common for new advertisers, so review the Facebook ad ban guidance before running any ads. [L1218.016]
- Under an ad-based ban, the account can often be restored once the advertised content is changed:
  1. Request a review of the banned account. [L1431.039]
  2. Change the ad creatives to more compelling content that follows the platform rules. [L1431.039]
  3. Remove the offending video or ad from the ad account. [L1431.039]
  4. Do not insist on the same creative, because the account will not be returned if it is pushed again. [L1431.039]
  5. Expect the account to be restored within hours to about two days after the offending ad is removed. [L1431.039]
- If an ad account is banned after a before-and-after video, remove the video or image that is being advertised, then request a review. The account can often be restored after that. [L1184.034]

### Ban frequency and false flags

- Facebook bans are rare for properly run stores. Most are caused by user error that can be fixed quickly. [L1111.098] (older, 2020)
- New stores are often blocked in the first three to four days. [L0650.014]
- Some ad account suspensions are caused by false flags. Check the store against the ad policy before appealing, and give the reason in the appeal. [L0468.109] (older, 2020)
- Fashion usually faces fewer Facebook ad restrictions than beauty or health products, which are more prone to ad-policy problems. Keeping the campaign structure as simple as possible also helps. [L0660.026]

### Fallback channels while restricted

- While a Meta account is restricted, run ads on Google and Microsoft instead, including Google remarketing, and try Meta again later. [L0855.144]
- If an ad account suspension appeal is delayed, a paid verification option has been reported to speed up review and unlock the account. It is not guaranteed, so use it only as a possible path while waiting for the appeal. [L0835.120]
- The paid verification option was reported to cost about £9 per month and to speed up review of a suspended ad account, possibly within an hour. It is not guaranteed, so use it only as a parallel option while the appeal is still pending. [L0835.138]
- Ask paid-ads communities for working contact routes as a supplement to the official channel. [L0470.098] (2023)

### Restricted categories: health and fitness

- Health and fitness ads must avoid before-and-after comparisons, anything showing skin, and visual comparisons such as tummy and abs. Use a reviewer or testimonial instead where possible. [L0871.072]
- Weight-loss products are considered too risky for dropshippers. Health and fitness products in general can be advertised with a solid ad account setup and careful creative and copy. [L0871.074]
- The Facebook Advertising Blueprint certification is a short course of about 15 minutes that explains the ad rules for health and fitness before a launch. [L0871.075]
- Meta is strict with health advertising, so ban and ad rejection rates are high in health niches. Plan for rejections and avoid building the whole launch on brand pages with direct health claims. [L0906.014]
- Health promises, weight-loss claims, and before-and-after beauty claims can get an ad account banned. Test these products only in a separate ad account and avoid these claim types in the ad. [L1171.011]
- Products or claims that promise health results, weight loss, slimmer bodies or looking younger lead to account bans. Before-and-after comparisons that look offensive or misleading can also trigger a ban. [L1431.037]
- Holiday gift cards promoted in Meta ads triggered an ad account suspension that lasted more than 30 days. Check the Meta advertising policies for restricted offers such as gift cards before launching campaigns. [L0468.196] (older, 2020)

### Restricted categories: before and after, and beauty

- Before-and-after images in ads on Facebook, especially for beauty or dental products, can cause ad account bans for misleading claims. Avoid promises of quick results and use review-style videos instead. [L1184.033]
- Show features and benefits without bold claims, and use a customer review video instead of a before-and-after comparison, to reduce the risk of bans. [L1184.035]
- A strong ad performance cannot be guaranteed on Facebook, so do not make bold guarantee claims in beauty or cosmetic ads. [L1184.036]
- Facebook treats its platform as an entertainment space, so shocking before-and-after or explicit imagery harms the user experience and can get ads restricted. Controversial ads are reviewed more strictly than ads that people enjoy. [L1171.012]

### Restricted categories: medical products

- Meta restricts ads for medical devices, especially personalised ads that address the viewer directly about a medical need. If the ad is restricted, rewrite the ad and remarketing copy to be less personal and focus on the offer. [L0807.030] (2025)
- To appeal a Meta restriction on medical products (2025):
  1. Compare an approved product page with a rejected one side by side, and check whether the copy addresses a specific person individually. [L0807.031] (2025)
  2. Edit the rejected copy to remove direct personal calls to action. [L0807.031] (2025)
  3. Log into your Facebook account and click Support at the top right. [L0807.031] (2025)
  4. Open Meta Business Support and request a human review of the rejected products. [L0807.031] (2025)

### Restricted categories: celebrity claims

- Do not use celebrity names or claims such as an endorsement by a famous person in ads, because this can lead to Facebook blocking the ad account. [L1172.039]
- If you still test such a product, use a new ad account rather than your main account. [L1172.039]
- A new ad account for testing is not a way to evade a permanent ban. Do not create new setups to get around a permanent ban. [L1172.039, L0637.027]

### Special ad categories

- In the Special Ad Categories section of a campaign, leave the declaration empty for ordinary product ads. [L0348.027] (2026)
- Declare a category only when the ad is about social issues, elections or politics, or concerns financial products and services, housing or employment. Requirements differ by country. [L0348.027] (2026)

## Pixel, Events, Conversions API and Shopify Integration

### iOS 14 Limits and Why Server-Side Tracking Matters

- Since iOS 14, cookie-based tracking is limited. The Conversions API lets a store send data server to server to Facebook and Google, so connect the store with maximum data sharing to keep tracking accurate. (older, 2021) [L0247.068]
- Connecting Shopify directly to Facebook through Shopify's built-in integration uses the Conversions API, which keeps conversion tracking reliable after the iOS 14 privacy changes. (older, 2021) [L0853.075]
- Linking Facebook and Shopify with the correct pixel setup from the start still recovers some data through Safari blocking, but nothing fully restores pre-iOS 14 tracking. (older, 2021) [L1112.025]
- Maximum data sharing with the Conversions API recovers only part of the tracking lost after Apple's iOS 14.5 changes. Do not assume it brings back all lost conversions. [L1318.017]
- After the iOS update, many conversions are not tracked, so the pixel needs larger audiences to find results. Audiences of around 40 to 50 million or more give the pixel more data than tightly restricted ones. [L0648.017]
- Remarketing audiences shrank after iOS 14, so the minimum usable remarketing window is now about seven days. Make windows as large as the traffic allows instead of very narrow ones. (older, 2021) [L1112.023]
- Before iOS 14, a site with a few hundred daily visitors could split Facebook remarketing into windows of 2, 4 and 7 days, then wider windows beyond 7 days. These narrow windows no longer work well because audiences shrank after the update. (older, 2021) [L1112.082]
- Device-based audience changes for Facebook and iOS are not required at small ad budgets. They matter more as spend and audience size grow. (older, 2022) [L0969.076]
- The Facebook ad training was updated after the iOS privacy changes. Current practice differs mostly in how Shopify connects the store to Facebook ads. (older, 2022) [L0803.051]
- Set Shopify's Facebook data sharing to maximum so Meta can capture more users for remarketing. (older, 2022) [L1113.063]

### Prerequisites and Account Structure

- Meta Pixel: a tracking code on the store that reports visitor actions such as add to cart, checkout and purchase to Meta. Meta uses this data to learn which people are likely to buy and to optimise ads. [L1021.013]
- Events must be configured. In Events Manager, set up Add to Cart, Initiate Checkout and Purchase so Meta knows which actions count as success. Events that are not configured give Meta no signal to optimise towards. [L1021.014]
- Backend setup order between Facebook and Shopify:
  1. Connect your Facebook Page to Shopify, so Facebook knows which store to send people to. [L1021.012]
  2. Verify your domain, which proves that you own the website. [L1021.012]
  3. Create your Meta Pixel and connect it to the store. [L1021.012]
  4. Configure events in Events Manager for Add to Cart, Initiate Checkout and Purchase. [L1021.012]
  5. Add a payment card, because Meta needs a way to charge ad spend. [L1021.012]
- Connect a Meta Pixel to your existing Business Manager (business portfolio) rather than creating a new Business Manager. The pixel is a dataset that lives under the Business Manager and is linked to the ad account from there. [L1027.001]
- Domain verification: when Meta and Shopify are linked through the Meta sales channel, the integration handles domain verification. A separate meta tag in theme.liquid is not needed. (2025) [L0667.041]
- Connecting the online store to the Business Manager is a separate step after domain verification. It is required before running dynamic remarketing ads. [L1025.019]
- Requirements for complete conversion data: a connected and verified domain, the Shopify Conversions API integration, and the tracking data switches turned on. Without all three, conversion data is incomplete. [L1288.006]
- A Meta Commerce account must be created inside the Meta Business Manager so the Shopify products can be placed in the Business Manager. This is a prerequisite for dynamic remarketing with product catalogs. [L1318.008]
- A pixel created by yourself, outside the Business Manager and the Shopify integration, may not connect properly. Always create the pixel from the business portfolio that owns the ad account and connect it through the partner setup. [L1027.009]
- Setting up the connection and the Commerce account does not spend any ad budget. Ads are created in later steps. [L1318.011]

### Creating the Pixel (Dataset) in Meta

- The pixel must already exist in Meta Events Manager before you link it in Shopify. If no pixel exists, Shopify's dataset step offers a Create new option instead of listing an ID. [L1028.008]
- Create the pixel in Meta Business settings:
  1. Go to Business settings and open Data sources. [L1024.021]
  2. Open Data sets. The pixel was moved from its former location to data sets. [L1024.021]
  3. Click Add, then Create new data set. [L1024.021]
  4. Name the data set after the store and click Create. [L1024.021]
- Ad set > Conversion > Dataset is the Meta pixel and should already be selected by default. If no pixel exists, create one, add it to the website and grant the needed permissions to the user and the Business Manager. [L0865.017]
- Create a new Meta pixel for each new product test. You create it yourself inside the account, so it does not need to be bought. [L1171.014]
- Alternative to Shopify: install the Facebook pixel directly through Facebook and the Google pixel directly through Google to keep full control over tracking and retargeting. For a beginner who wants to start immediately, Perfect Audience can track both Facebook and Google visitors with one line of code on the site. [L0923.010]
- Three ways to set up the pixel on the Set up Meta pixel screen:
  - Add the pixel code to the website yourself (marked Recommended). [L1027.016]
  - Send instructions to a developer. [L1027.016]
  - Set up with a partner such as Shopify or WordPress. For a Shopify store, the partner route connects the pixel through the Shopify integration. [L1027.016]
- Traffic route: send Facebook traffic to your Shopify store rather than using Facebook's built-in direct-sell option. This was the practice as of early 2023 and may change. [L0934.051]
- Facebook Commerce Center: the new Facebook sales channel uses it. It needs fewer apps and no extra fees, which makes setup easier than the older process. (older, 2021) [L0247.069]

### Connecting Shopify to Facebook and Instagram

Two procedures reach the same Facebook & Instagram channel. Use procedure A for the current Shopify flow. Procedure B is the order used in a separate setup.

- Shopify app: install the Facebook & Instagram app from the Shopify App Store (developer: Meta, free to install, additional charges may apply), then click Get started and log in to Facebook. [L1027.012]
- The personal Facebook profile is used only as the access route to the business accounts. It is not the owner of the pixel or catalog. [L1027.012]
- Shopify asks for a business portfolio. A portfolio that does not meet Meta's requirements for shops cannot be selected, and the Connect button stays unavailable for it. In that case choose Create new business portfolio or pick a portfolio that qualifies before connecting. [L1027.013]

**Procedure A (Source D 2026)**

1. In the Shopify admin, install the Facebook & Instagram app and click Get started, then connect your Facebook account. [L1027.002]
2. Under Set up Ads on Facebook and Instagram, connect the business portfolio (Business Manager) that owns the product catalog, choose or create a catalog, and accept Meta's Product Catalog Terms. [L1027.002]
3. Under Manage Facebook & Instagram data sharing preference, select Maximum and save. Maximum shares the most data with Facebook and uses the Meta Pixel, advanced matching and Conversions API. [L1027.002]
4. Submit for review and wait while the channel setup finalizes. This takes several minutes. Do not refresh. [L1027.002, L1028.012]
5. In Ads Manager, open the ad account, then open Events Manager from the left column. [L1027.002]
6. Choose Connect data, select Web, then Create a new dataset (pixel). Leave the name field empty if you wish, and agree to the Meta Business Tools Terms. [L1027.002]
7. Choose the option to set up the pixel with a partner (Shopify), go to Shopify, and confirm the connection. [L1027.002]
8. In Shopify, make sure the Facebook and Instagram sales channel is installed, and set the data sharing to Maximum. [L1027.002]
9. Return to Events Manager and click Next. The status stays inactive until test traffic is sent. [L1027.002]
10. Click Send Test Traffic, then Finish. [L1027.002]

**Procedure B**

1. In Shopify admin, go to Sales channels and search for Facebook (the Facebook & Instagram app), then click Get started. [L1028.005]
2. Click Connect account. [L1028.005]
3. Log in with the Facebook profile you want to use. Shopify uses this personal profile only to access your business accounts. Review the requested access (name and profile picture, view access) and continue. [L1028.005]
4. Choose the business profile that owns the ad account you want to use (the one you created in your ad account). [L1028.005]
5. Choose the data sharing preference (see the data sharing section). [L1028.005]
6. In the dataset step, select the existing pixel. If no pixel exists yet, click Create new. [L1028.005]
7. Click Confirm, then wait while Shopify shows Finalizing channel setup. [L1028.005, L1028.012]
8. Continue to the catalog step and sync products. [L1028.005]

Procedure B also notes that if you have a product catalog, connect the business portfolio that owns it during setup. You can manage how your products are advertised later. [L1028.013]

- The Set up Meta pixel screen for Shopify is handled through the partner route. The pixel connects through the Shopify integration. [L1027.016]
- Changing the connected Business Manager: remove the existing Facebook channel or business manager connection in the Shopify Facebook app, then connect the new Business Manager there. [L1218.015]
- Older Shopify procedure (2022): enable the Facebook pixel under Online Store > Preferences, choose the highest level of data sharing in the customer privacy settings, and connect Shopify directly to Facebook, since this is used for the product feed. (older, 2022) [L1281.052]
- Orders bought through Meta still appear in Shopify if the purchase runs through your Shopify store. The checkout option you choose in Meta does not change this. [L0965.054]

### Data Sharing Levels in Shopify

Shopify's Facebook & Instagram data sharing preference has three choices: Conservative, Enhanced and Maximum. Shopify shows the choice as "Manage Facebook & Instagram data sharing preference" and "Choose your data sharing preference." [L1027.003, L1028.006]

| Level | What it means | Source | When it applies |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conservative | Listed as a choice; the sources give no further detail. | [L1027.003] | Not used in the sources' setups. |
| Enhanced | Customer activity is sent through the Meta Pixel, advanced matching and the Conversions API. Customers can stop this from their own Facebook account settings. | [L1028.006, L1028.007] | Used in one setup. Maximum is also a valid choice. |
| Maximum | Shares the most data with Facebook. Uses the Meta Pixel, advanced matching and Conversions API. Continually updated with Meta's latest ad technology. | [L1027.002, L1027.003] | Recommended by most sources for the best possible results. |

- Shopify's setup guidance cites a 17.8% lower average cost per result for advertisers who used the Conversions API alongside the Meta Pixel. [L1027.003]
- Meta reports that sending customer data through both the Meta Pixel and the Conversions API together lowers cost per action (CPA) compared with the Pixel alone. This is why Enhanced combines both channels. [L1028.019]
- Data sharing is a single setting. If it is correct, the audience sizes in Meta should be correct. [L0965.049]
- Maximum sends customer personal data to Meta. Before saving the setting, the store's privacy policy must describe how personal data is used and shared with third parties such as Facebook. Shopify's share data settings are where this preference is chosen. [L1027.014]
- Maximum data sharing is also needed for purchase conversions to appear in campaigns. Without it, the Purchase event may not reach Meta, and the Purchase option will not appear in the Meta campaign event drop-down. [L0837.032]

### Dataset Settings and Verification

- Website settings in the dataset (Events Manager > Settings):
  - Turn on Automatic website matching. It uses hashed customer data such as email or phone to match customers to Meta accounts, which helps attribute more conversions and supports remarketing. [L1027.006]
  - Turn on Automatic advanced matching. [L1027.006]
  - The course recommends keeping website matching on and tracking events without code. The dataset feature turns off after 90 days of inactivity. [L1027.006]
- Auto-tracking in dataset settings: turn it on only when you want campaigns to draw on offline events from this dataset for ad tracking and attribution. Once enabled for an ad account, every new campaign there uses this dataset for offline event tracking by default. Keeping it off is a valid choice if you have no plans to use it. [L1027.007]
- Extend attribution uploads (Events Manager > Settings) can extend the offline event upload window from 7 days to 90 days. Use it only if your business cannot upload events within at least seven days after they occur. Extensions apply only to offline events from a physical shop. [L1027.008]
- Sharing a dataset: open the dataset's Settings in Events Manager, go to Sharing, and choose Share With an Ad Account. To give another business portfolio access instead, use the option Give another business portfolio access to this dataset. [L1027.019]
- Verify the connection by checking that the pixel ID is linked to the ad account ID in Events Manager and the Business Manager, and that the pixel status shows connected to the ad account. The course checks the pixel ID and ad account ID on both screens. [L1027.010]
- In Events Manager under Integrations, the Shopify (online) entry should show the status Connected and the number of pixels connected to it. The dataset page should show it as connected to one ad account. [L1027.020]
- Events Manager pixel tabs: Overview, Test events, Diagnostics, History and Settings. The Overview's event table shows Total events and the Last received time for each event, such as Add to Cart, Initiate Checkout and Purchase, to confirm data is flowing. [L1028.011]

**Find and verify the two IDs before linking**

1. Ad account ID: open Ads Manager and check the ad account selector. The numeric Ad account ID is shown under the account name. [L1028.010]
2. Pixel ID: open Events Manager and select the pixel, which Meta now lists as a dataset. The numeric ID is shown on its overview and in the page URL. [L1028.010]
3. In Shopify, confirm that the selected business profile and ad account match the ad account ID from step 1, and that the dataset matches the pixel ID from step 2. [L1028.010]
4. Confirm the link only when both IDs match. [L1028.010]

- If the pixel ID selected in Shopify is wrong, the whole ad optimization process does not work. Always compare the selected ID with the pixel ID shown in Meta Events Manager before confirming. [L1028.009]

### Auto Advanced Matching

- Auto advanced matching lets Meta collect more customer data from the website, such as phone number and address, which makes remarketing more precise. Turn it on in the Meta ad account. [L0649.006]
- The Shopify course places the same setting in Events Manager > Settings, next to Automatic website matching. Use Events Manager, and keep website matching on. [L1027.006]
- A new Meta pixel knows nothing about who buys. It learns from view, cart, checkout and purchase events, so more events on the store mean faster and better audience learning. [L0649.008]

### Conversion Event for Optimization

- For a new store, set the Meta ads conversion event to Purchase from the start, because the goal is sales. This is the presenter's experience. Some advertisers start with Add to Cart. [L0642.005]
- Set up the new Facebook pixel site-wide on the store. The optimization event options include Checkouts, Registrations, Leads, Key page views, Adds to cart, and other website conversions. Adds to Cart was reported as the strongest option. [L0643.035]
- Optimizing for Adds to Cart gave a better actual purchase conversion rate than optimizing for purchases. It only pays off if you also have a follow-up sequence for people who added to cart. Even if you already run ads, switch the optimization event to Adds to Cart. [L0643.036]
- Choose between the two: Purchase from the start (for a new store, per the presenter's experience) or Adds to Cart (when you have a follow-up sequence for cart abandoners). [L0642.005, L0643.036]

### One Pixel per Product or per Niche

Sources differ on when to split pixels. The split points are given below, each with its own condition.

| Situation | Pixel rule | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Two valid strategies | (1) One pixel per product, which is stricter and gives clean data per product. (2) Several products under one pixel, with a new pixel after 3 to 5 failed tests. Both are accepted; the choice depends on how strict you want the test to be. | [L0348.023] |
| Testing 3 to 5 failed products of different niches | Create a new pixel and start a new data set for the next products. | [L0348.021] |
| Same niche, all product tests | Run all product tests on the same pixel. | [L0349.016, L0639.021] |
| Winner found and a new product in a different niche | Use a different pixel. | [L0349.016] |
| Switching to a completely different niche | Install a new pixel. The same ad account can usually stay. | [L1172.038] |
| Five products in one niche test | Use one pixel for all five, so the pixel does not change each time a product is added. | [L1194.009] |
| Product takes off profitably at about 100 conversions | Create a new pixel for that product. Below about 100 conversions, the pixel has too little data for a change to make a difference. | [L0639.022] |
| Fewer than about 5 sales per day | Sharing one pixel between products is acceptable while testing. | [L0645.032] |
| About 10 to 15 total sales | Sharing one pixel across different niches is acceptable. With more data, keep one pixel per niche. | [L1217.045] |
| Purchase volume about 100 or more | Share one pixel until the store reaches about 100 or more purchases. | [L1177.042] |
| 20 purchases | Too little data to need a second pixel. | [L1177.042] |

- Keep one pixel per niche so its data stays focused on that niche. Keep purchase data from one niche separate from another. Products within the same niche can share one pixel while testing. [L0639.021, L0645.032]
- Keep one pixel while testing. The pixel only starts to optimize well after roughly 10 sales for a product. Until then, Facebook's own targeting does most of the work, so the pixel matters less during early tests. [L0639.022, L0645.033]
- In the first test phase, the pixel mainly collects data and does not drive sales. Pixel data becomes important once a product has real sales. [L1217.048]
- In the early stage, with only a few sales, several products can share one ad account and one pixel. The pixel is still gathering data at this point rather than driving sales. [L1217.042]
- A pixel becomes tied to the niche it has been used for over months. When switching to a completely different niche, install a new pixel. [L1172.038]
- A new pixel gives Meta's algorithm a clean data record, so it learns faster than from a pixel with many failed tests attached. Creating a new pixel after failures restarts the learning process. [L0348.022]
- Keep the same pixel when testing products in the same niche. Consider a new pixel when switching to a different niche. Both approaches can work. A new pixel gives more separate data to compare. [L1316.035]
- Each new ad account needs its own new pixel. [L1194.009]
- Sharing a dataset with another ad account is possible through Share With an Ad Account. [L1027.019]

### Testing the Pixel: Test Events and Pixel Helper

**Meta Pixel Helper**

- Meta Pixel Helper is a free Google Chrome extension. It shows which Meta pixel is installed on the current page, the pixel ID, and the events it fires along with the page URL. (2025) [L0837.030]
- Use it to debug pixel tracking before relying on the data. It shows whether the Meta Pixel is installed on a page and whether it is firing correctly. (2025) [L0844.003]
- Purchase test with Pixel Helper:
  1. Install Meta Pixel Helper in Google Chrome. [L0837.031]
  2. Create a temporary product priced at about $1 in your Shopify store and buy it. [L0837.031]
  3. On the order confirmation page, open the extension and check that a Purchase event was triggered. [L0837.031]
  4. If no Purchase event appears, the integration between Shopify and Meta is at fault. Check the Facebook & Instagram sales channel settings in Shopify. [L0837.031]

**Test events in Events Manager**

- Test events procedure (Source D 2026):
  1. Open the dataset, go to Test events, enter the website URL and click Test events. [L1027.004]
  2. Take actions on the site, such as View content, Add to cart, Initiate checkout or a simulated purchase. [L1027.004]
  3. The events should appear in the test view, typically within a few seconds. A status of Active confirms the pixel works. [L1027.004]
  4. Events can take up to 30 minutes to show in the normal overview. [L1027.004]
- Test events procedure with test_event_code:
  1. Open the pixel in Events Manager and go to Test events. [L1028.015]
  2. Copy the unique test_event_code shown on the page. [L1028.015]
  3. For server-side events, open the Graph API Explorer, make sure all parameters match the payload shown on the page, submit the request and return to the Test events page. [L1028.015]
  4. For website events, enter your website URL in the field and click Test events. [L1028.015]
  5. Keep the Test events page open in Events Manager during the whole test. [L1028.015]
  6. Open your store and perform the actions you want to verify, such as viewing a product, adding to cart and starting checkout. [L1028.015]
  7. Check the events list: each action should appear as its event (for example Add to Cart or Initiate Checkout). Expect delays of up to 30 minutes before events show in the chart. [L1028.015]
- Do not treat the setup as finished until the test events appear in Events Manager. A connection that looks linked in Shopify can still send no usable events if the pixel or test steps were skipped. [L1028.016]
- Test events show how the site and server events are set up, but test behaviour can differ from live traffic. Personal ad settings do not affect test data, so a passing test does not show that the ads are optimized. [L1028.020]

### Product Catalog and Commerce Manager Feed

**Making products sellable on Facebook and Instagram**

- Procedure (Source D 2026):
  1. Set each product's status to active. [L1027.018]
  2. Select the Facebook sales channel for each product. [L1027.018]
  3. Publish the products. [L1027.018]
  4. Wait until the catalog shows Catalog in sync, which confirms the products were synced to the catalog for ads and shops. [L1027.018]
- Procedure (before the catalog sync):
  1. In Shopify, set the product status to active for the Facebook & Instagram sales channel. [L1028.014]
  2. Publish the products to that channel. [L1028.014]
  3. Sync products to your Meta catalog so they can be featured in ads and shops. [L1028.014]
  4. Wait until the catalog shows as in sync before running ads. [L1028.014]
- The product catalog must contain the full catalog, since remarketing needs every product to be available. If products are rejected, shorten the description below the 5,000-character limit. (older, 2021) [L0834.076]
- Facebook product descriptions must stay under 5,000 characters, or the product will not publish to Facebook. (older, 2021) [L0834.077]

**Checking the catalog in Commerce Manager**

- Meta Commerce Manager, reached through a Meta Commerce account linked to the Business Manager, holds the product catalog that allows dynamic remarketing with products. Data sources and items show whether the Shopify products arrived correctly. [L1318.007]
- For a store with its own brand, create a new product catalog during the sync step rather than adding it to a catalog that belongs to another business. Name it after the store domain. [L1318.009]
- Choose the Business Manager, Facebook page, Instagram profile and pixel that belong to the store. Several businesses or pixels may be listed, and picking the wrong one connects the store to the wrong account. [L1318.010]
- The Shopify product feed in Commerce Manager should be named Shopify dynamic Facebook ads product feed and show an all good status with its latest update time. [L1318.012]
- Data sources can still show an old manually uploaded feed that has not been updated for 365 days. Ignore that stale feed and check the newly updated Shopify feed instead. [L1318.013]
- Procedure for checking products in Commerce Manager:
  1. Open Commerce Manager from the Business Manager. [L1318.014]
  2. Click data sources and find the Shopify dynamic Facebook ads product feed with its latest update time and an all good status. [L1318.014]
  3. Click items to confirm all Shopify products are listed. [L1318.014]
  4. Expand a product to see whether it is in stock or out of stock and to see its content details. [L1318.014]
- Connecting the Shopify store to Meta, enabling data sharing and checking the Commerce Manager feed only prepares the account and spends no ad budget. Confirm that the product feed and items are correct before building audiences or launching any ads. [L1318.016]

**Feed content and dynamic ads**

- Dynamic product ads require the account to be linked to the store's product feed, which may need more account activity first. Posting a few times a week on Instagram builds this activity. (2022, older) [L0429.054]
- Meta dynamic remarketing works when the store connects directly to Meta through Shopify, which shares the product catalog. A custom feed can be used instead for more control, submitted as a catalog URL in Meta. (2022, older) [L0429.055]
- Meta ads can run before dynamic product ads are possible. [L0429.054]
- Facebook Marketplace is a default placement for Meta ads run from a Shopify catalog. Separate Marketplace listings are not needed. (2022, older) [L0429.056]
- Running an ad to a product with no shop page is risky. A catalog-based ad with checkout inside Facebook is an alternative that avoids a landing page, but it requires setting up a catalog first. [L0552.021]
- Connect the store to the Facebook page and import the product catalog there, so products can be viewed on Facebook and visitors can return to the store. Post content on the Facebook page that matches what the successful reference store posts. [L1325.015]

### Catalog and Feed Errors

- Conversion errors (wrong conversion types, ID issues) are often platform-level Facebook issues that affect multiple users. Contact Facebook support by clicking the question mark (?) icon at the bottom right of the campaigns page. [L0209.006]
- Procedure when Meta reports a product feed error such as "add product description" (2022, older):
  1. Check how the product feed is submitted to Meta. [L0429.053]
  2. If submitted through a Shopify app, open the app's feed and check that the product description column contains data. [L0429.053]
  3. Fix the blank column so Meta reads the correct field. [L0429.053]
- Procedure when a Facebook catalog reports products without descriptions although they have text:
  1. Check whether all products or only a subset are affected. [L0470.004]
  2. If all products are affected, check in the feed app (for example Simprosys) that the correct Shopify section is linked to the product feed. [L0470.004]
  3. If only a subset is affected, check whether those descriptions are too long for Facebook or contain HTML that Facebook does not accept. [L0470.004]
  4. For the affected products, write custom descriptions inside the feed app so that the feed sent to Facebook carries them. [L0470.004]
- Procedure when Facebook rejects a product catalog without a clear reason (older, 2021):
  1. Do not repeatedly request reviews from the rejection notice. [L0853.097]
  2. Open the Facebook support page and click Get started. [L0853.097]
  3. Use the live chat there to learn the reason and ask for the issue to be resolved. [L0853.097]

### Tracking Errors and Common Mistakes

- Pixel ID mismatch: if the pixel ID selected in Shopify is wrong, optimization does not work. Compare it with the ID in Meta Events Manager before confirming. [L1028.009]
- Missing Purchase event: check the Shopify sales channel settings and data sharing, as in the Pixel Helper procedure. [L0837.031, L0837.032]
- Setup seems complete but sends no events: test events must show up in Events Manager before the setup counts as finished. [L1028.016]
- Interrupting Finalizing channel setup by refreshing the page can leave the channel incomplete. [L1028.012]
- Blocked parameters and events: data sharing category and domain controls in Events Manager can block parameters and events. If no category is assigned, set the dataset category via Edit. The allow list or block list controls which domains can send events to Facebook. [L1027.011]
- Bot and non-buying traffic: newly launched ads, UTM parameters or platform crawlers can inflate visitor counts. Launch ads at least six to seven hours before the planned start, so the platform's bot traffic arrives before measurement begins. Exclude it from funnel analysis. [L1260.011]
- Stale feed: an old manually uploaded feed (not updated for 365 days) can be mistaken for the live feed. Check the newly updated Shopify feed. [L1318.013]
- Accuracy after iOS: maximum data sharing recovers only part of the tracking lost after iOS 14.5, so do not expect all lost conversions to return. [L1318.017]

#### Further details

- Online Store > Preferences > Facebook Pixel: click 'Set up Facebook' to connect the pixel through the Facebook app. The app handles the setup and enables advanced tracking options that help target new and existing customers, including retargeting. The pixel tracks visitor behaviour on the store to support marketing. [L0370.021]

- The Facebook Pixel is set up in Shopify under Online Store > Preferences > Facebook Pixel by clicking Set up Facebook. Connecting through the Facebook app ensures correct setup and enables advanced tracking for retargeting audiences. [L0726.016]

- In Online Store > Preferences, the Facebook Pixel section lets you connect your pixel through the Facebook app. The app handles the setup and enables advanced tracking that supports retargeting of new and existing customers. [L1145.024]

## Ads Manager Columns, Custom Metrics and Naming

### Column presets: build order and saving

- Build all base columns first, save them as a preset, and only then create custom metrics. Creating a custom metric before the preset is saved deletes all unsaved column work, and you must start over. The correct order is: add columns, save preset, create custom metrics. [L0290.002, L0290.011, L0290.015]
- Save the column setup as a Column Preset with a consistent name. The standard name is `main dash`. The preset saves automatically and persists across logins, account switches and page refreshes. You can reselect it from the Columns dropdown without rebuilding. [L0290.012]

#### Base column procedure (Source E 2025-26)

1. Log into Ads Manager. [L0290.001]
2. Click Columns > Customize Columns (at the bottom). [L0290.001]
3. Delete all columns except Delivery and Actions. [L0290.001]
4. Add columns in this exact order: [L0290.001]
   - Bid Strategy
   - Budget
   - Amount Spent
   - Purchase (check the Value checkbox and uncheck the others)
   - ROAS (uncheck other options)
   - Average Purchase Conversion Value (AOV)
   - Cost per Purchase
   - Attribution Setting
   - Reach
   - Impressions
   - Frequency
   - Link Clicks
   - Cost per Link Click (CLC)
   - CTR (Link Click-Through Rate)
   - CPM (Cost per 1,000 impressions)
   - Add to Carts (select Total; uncheck in-app, offline, and Meta versions, keep Website only) [L0290.007]
   - Cost per Add to Cart
   - Initiate Checkout (select Total and Cost)
   - Cost per Initiate Checkout
   - Results (select Purchases)
   - Cost per Purchase again
5. Do not create custom metrics yet. Save this preset first: click Save as Column Preset and name it `main dash` (or any name you choose). [L0290.001, L0290.012]
6. The preset saves automatically and remains available across logins and account switches. [L0290.001, L0290.012]

#### Add to Carts selection

- When adding the Add to Carts column, select Total and uncheck the individual options:
  - in-app add to carts (for app downloads only)
  - offline add to carts (for stores that uploaded purchases to Facebook)
  - Meta add to carts (for Meta Shop or Instagram Shop only)
- Choose Website add to carts for standard e-commerce. [L0290.007]

#### Tool columns

- Link Ad Settings: add the column to see the landing page used for each ad without editing individual campaigns. Type `link` or `ad` in the search field. The column is visible only at the ad level, not the campaign level. It saves time compared with clicking into each ad to check its landing page. [L0290.013]
- Place the Link Ad Settings column immediately after Bid Strategy in the main dashboard preset. This keeps setup information together at the top for quick visibility of landing page assignments. [L0290.021]
- Last Significant Edit: add the column to see changes to campaigns, ad sets and ads, such as bid caps, cost caps, minimum ROAS or cost per goal settings, without checking account history. It is visible at the ad set and ad level and helps identify when a campaign's bidding strategy was last modified. [L0290.014]

#### Common mistakes

- Do not join calls with a Facebook mentor or account manager without setting up these custom columns beforehand. Setting them up live wastes 5-7 minutes of call time for the mentor. [L0290.017]

### Custom metrics

#### Definitions at a glance

| Metric | Formula | Source ids |
|---|---|---|
| Hook rate (attention) | 3-second video plays ÷ total impressions × 100, shown as a percentage | L0067.001, L0652.002 |
| Hold rate (engagement) | Video plays at 50% ÷ 3-second video plays × 100, shown as a percentage | L0067.003, L0652.003 |
| CVR (Source E version) | Purchases ÷ Link Clicks, format Percentage | L0290.002 |
| CVR (Source C version) | Unique outbound clicks from the Facebook ad that resulted in a purchase, as a percentage | L0067.002 |
| CBR (custom conversion rate) | Purchases ÷ unique outbound clicks × 100, shown as a percentage | L0652.004 |
| ATC% (Add to Cart Percentage) | Add to Carts ÷ Link Clicks, format Percentage | L0290.002 |
| IC% (Initiate Checkout Percentage) | Initiate Checkout ÷ Link Clicks, format Percentage | L0290.002 |

#### Hook rate and hold rate

- Hook rate is the percentage of people watching the first 3 seconds of a video out of total impressions served. Create it via Columns > Create custom metric. [L0067.001]
- Hook rate measures the share of impressions where viewers watched the first three seconds of the video ad. [L0652.002]
- Hold rate is the percentage of people who watch at least 50% of a video out of those who watched the first 3 seconds. [L0067.003]
- Hold rate measures how many viewers who passed the three-second mark kept watching to the halfway point. [L0652.003]
- Hold rate is only meaningful with high traffic volume. [L0067.003]
- When spend is small, read differences between ads carefully. A CTR of 2.27% against 0.41% can be skewed by low data. [L0640.049]

#### CVR and CBR: two denominators

- The sources use different denominators for the conversion rate. Both values are kept here. Do not compare a rate built on unique outbound clicks with a rate built on Link Clicks.
  - Source C method: purchases ÷ unique outbound clicks. Use unique outbound clicks, not total clicks, because Facebook tracking of page views is unreliable. Unique clicks count one user once even if they click several times. Source C calls this version CBR. [L0067.002, L0652.004]
  - Source E method: purchases ÷ Link Clicks, named CVR, and placed next to AOV. The same source builds ATC% and IC% on the same Link Clicks base. [L0290.002]
- Meta tracks outbound clicks more reliably than website sessions. This is the reason given for the Source C denominator. [L0652.004]

#### Creating the custom metrics (Source E 2025-26)

Do this only after the base preset is saved. [L0290.002]

1. Go to Columns > Customize Columns > Create custom metric.
2. Create Conversion Rate (CVR): name it `CVR`, set format to Percentage, enter formula `Purchases ÷ Link Clicks`, press Create metric.
3. Create Add to Cart Percentage (ATC%): name it `ATC%`, set format to Percentage, enter formula `Add to Carts ÷ Link Clicks`, press Create metric.
4. Create Initiate Checkout Percentage (IC%): name it `IC%`, set format to Percentage, enter formula `Initiate Checkout ÷ Link Clicks`, press Create metric.
5. Place CVR next to Average Purchase Conversion Value (AOV). Place ATC% after the Add to Carts column. Place IC% after the Initiate Checkout column.
6. Save the updated preset.

### Funnel column order

#### Source C: custom funnel view

- To build a funnel column view in Meta Ads Manager:
  1. Open the campaign view.
  2. Click Customize columns.
  3. Add custom metrics first, then reorder the columns on the right-hand side.
  4. Keep the ad set name, budget per day, amount spent, results and cost per result.
  5. Remove bid strategy when not running advanced bidding.
  6. Save the final selection as a preset (for example `mix funnel`) and apply it. [L0652.001]
- Note: the Source E base preset keeps Bid Strategy as its first column, while this Source C view removes it when no advanced bidding runs. Both apply to different setups. [L0290.001, L0652.001]
- The Source C snapshot section includes:
  - ad set name
  - last significant edit
  - daily budget
  - total spend over the period
  - result (conversions)
  - cost per result
- The funnel section includes:
  - impressions
  - CPM
  - 3-second video plays (hook rate)
  - hold rate
  - unique clicks
  - unique CTR %
  - cost per click
  - add-to-cart
  - purchases
  - custom conversion rate metric
  - ROAS [L0067.004]

#### Recommended funnel order (Source C)

Read left to right. Unneeded default columns are unchecked so the funnel is not cluttered.

| Position | Column |
|---|---|
| 1 | Amount spent |
| 2 | Budget per day (summary at top) |
| 3 | Purchases |
| 4 | Cost per purchase (result) |
| 5 | Impressions |
| 6 | 3-second video plays |
| 7 | Hook rate (%) |
| 8 | 50% video plays |
| 9 | Hold rate (%) |
| 10 | Unique link clicks |
| 11 | Unique CTR (%) |
| 12 | Unique cost per click |
| 13 | CBR (%) |
| 14 | Purchases |
| 15 | Return on ad spend (ROAS) |

[L0652.005]

- The result column depends on the optimization goal. When optimizing for clicks instead of purchases, the result column becomes cost per click. For purchase-optimized campaigns, the result column is cost per purchase. [L0652.006]

#### Source D: reporting preset order

The table lists columns with their role. Read the columns left to right in this order so the funnel is easy to follow. [L1030.002]

| Column | Role |
|---|---|
| Amount spent | Base cost of the ad |
| CPM (cost per 1,000 impressions) | Cost of reach |
| CTR (all) | Click rate on all clicks |
| CPC (all) | Cost per click on all clicks |
| CPC (cost per link click) | Cost per link click |
| Link clicks | Soft metric |
| Website adds to cart | Soft metric |
| Cost per add to cart | Soft metric cost |
| Checkouts initiated | Soft metric |
| Cost per checkout initiated | Soft metric cost |
| Purchase ROAS (return on ad spend) | Hard metric |
| Purchases | Hard metric |
| Cost per result | Secondary cost |
| Frequency | Ad fatigue indicator |
| Delivery | Delivery status |

[L1030.002]

- Add CTR (link click-through rate) next to CTR (all). Link CTR divides link clicks by impressions, so it shows how many people actually reach the store. [L1030.027]

### Video retention columns

- To see video retention metrics, open Customize columns and add the video metrics, such as video plays at 25, 50 and 95 percent, through plays, and video average play time. Facebook has renamed and added columns over time, so the available names may differ. [L1215.009]
- Add the Through plays column via Customize columns. It counts how often a video was played to completion or for at least 15 seconds. Read it together with video average play time. [L1215.034]

### Attribution setting columns

- Use 7-Day Clicks as the attribution setting for all campaigns. The setting must be the same across all campaigns and ad sets. [L0290.006]
- The attribution setting appears as its own column (Attribution Setting in the base procedure). A dedicated column lets you spot at a glance when campaigns have different attribution windows. [L0290.006, L0290.001]
- The Columns menu also offers Compare attribution settings, which is useful for checking the effect of different windows. [L1030.001]

### Reporting presets and dashboard setup

#### Two presets (Source D)

- Set up two column presets in Meta Ads Manager:
  - a desktop preset for photo editing, UGC work and managing the ad account
  - a phone preset for checking lifetime data when away from the computer
- Build them via Columns > Customise columns. The menu also offers presets such as Performance and clicks, Engagement and Delivery, plus View your column presets, Compare attribution settings, Conditional formatting and Reset column width. [L1030.001]

#### Layout and reading

- Arrange related metrics such as ROAS, purchases and AOV side by side on the dashboard. Do not scroll back and forth between them when making decisions. This reduces decision time significantly. [L0290.016]
- Compare one ad across several ad sets by sorting the Ads Manager table by ad name, so the same creative appears side by side. [L0640.049]
- Use Ads Manager to read ad set and ad performance, check CTR and spend, add interests via the Suggestions function, create and duplicate ad sets, and change budgets. [L0650.006]
- The Facebook block in the daily record shows CPC (cost per click), CTR (clicks ÷ impressions), CPM (cost per 1,000 impressions), ATC (add to cart), IC (initiate checkout) and purchases for each day, alongside daily ad spend. [L1123.020]

#### Reporting principles

- A clean, consistent naming convention is the base of any reliable creative report, because AI analysis is only as good as the data it reads. Start the convention now at any brand size so that year-on-year comparisons are possible later. [L0885.001]
- Keep one convention stable. Renaming products or changing the naming system midway makes older ads hard to compare. History beyond about 12 months became difficult to read after such changes. [L0885.002]

### Campaign naming

#### Core rules
