- Name campaigns so you can tell from the name what is inside: the product, the brand or account identifier, the budget type (for example CBO), the creative type (for example UGC) and the launch date. This makes it possible to read data later without guessing. [L0865.004]
- For sales that repeat every year, put the year in the campaign name, for example `Black Friday Sale 2026` instead of `Black Friday Sale`. Without the year, comparing the same sale across years becomes very hard. [L0865.005]

#### Naming patterns in the sources

- Source C: name campaigns and ad sets consistently with these fields: product name, ABO or CBO, objective, date, interest name, country code and age group.
  - Date: only once a product has run for more than about two months. [L0648.006]
  - Country codes: short codes such as `T4` for the US, Canada, Australia and UK, or `DE` for Germany. [L0648.006]
  - Age group: for example `20+`. [L0648.006]
- Source D: name Meta campaigns by audience, purpose and budget type, for example `Broad - Scale - CBO` or `Testing ABO`, so test and scale campaigns can be told apart quickly. [L1124.021]
- Source D: campaign name = `[today's date] [product name] CBO`, for example `2404 SleepMask CBO`. The date is placed first here, unlike the Source C rule above, which adds the date only after about two months. [L1193.009, L0648.006]
- Source D: name a duplicated campaign that carries a custom cover and custom UGC as `CBO - [product code] / Custom`, so the custom test stays separate from the original in the Ads Manager list. [L1030.028]

### Ad set naming

- Name each ad set after the creative inside it, for example the format, the number of ads, the launch date and the message type. You can see the contents without opening the ad set. [L0865.014]
- Ad set names in the Source D pattern are a broad targeting label, for example `Top 5 Broad`. [L1193.009]
- Ad set names in the Source C pattern include the interest name, alongside the campaign fields listed above. [L0648.006]

### Ad naming

#### Principles

- Put the product name first in every Meta ad name, because creative reports group by that field. Keep the other fields (ad type, launch date, creator name) in the same fixed order for every ad, so names can be parsed consistently. [L0885.024]
- Keep the product type in the ad-level name, because the report groups creatives by product from that field. Use a separate product type, such as `mixed`, for ads that feature several products. [L0885.005]
- Start the naming now, and do not change it midway. Launch date is recommended from the start to avoid rebuilding names later. [L0885.004, L0885.002]

#### Fields

- A usable ad name carries the product name (examples: sleep, tea, bathrobe), the ad type (video, UGC or sale ad), the launch date and the creator name. License tags and other extra fields are not needed for a creative-count report. [L0885.003]
- The launch date is not strictly needed for a creative-count report. It is recommended because the report is pulled from the Meta API. [L0885.004]
- Name each ad with its creative type and a unique counter, for example UGC followed by a number such as `A123`. This makes it possible to match an ad in Ads Manager to its source file and to its results. [L0865.066]
- Source D pattern: ad name = `[today's date] [product name] CBO > [ad set name] > [number] Ad`, for example `2404 SleepMask CBO > Top 5 Broad > 1 Ad`. [L1193.009]

#### Pipe-separated example

A pipe-separated Meta ad name can contain fields such as product type (for example OODIE or BLANKET), audience (ADULTS), license status (LICENSED), format (IMAGE or video), production source (STUDIO), launch type (NEW PRODUCT), a category tag (for example F&B) and a design or character name. For a creative-count report only product type, ad type, launch date and creator name are needed, so the other fields can be left out. [L0885.027]

#### Older advice (Source B, 2021-22)

- Older advice, 2021-22: name each Facebook ad so the name shows what differs from the other ads in the ad group. Without this, split test results cannot be linked back to the creative that produced them. [L1342.032]

### Audience naming

- Name custom audiences with product name, funnel stage, event and window, so they can be told apart later. Example: `product, MOF, 25 plus video views, 30d`. [L1335.045]
- Use the same pattern for engagement audiences, such as `FP engagement` with a 30-day window, and for website visitor audiences. [L1335.045]

### UTM parameters and URL tracking

#### Where the URL goes

- Enter the URL of the product page that belongs to that specific ad in the ad's Website URL field. URL parameters are no longer typed there. They are managed in the Tracking section, which is opened with Go to Tracking. [L0348.030]

#### Method A: dynamic placeholders at ad level

- In the ad-level UTM parameters on Meta, `utm_source` is fixed to `Facebook` and `utm_medium` to `cpc`. `utm_campaign`, `utm_content` and `utm_term` are filled with Meta's dynamic name placeholders for the campaign name, ad name and ad set name. [L0376.032]
- This lets each sale be traced back to the exact campaign, ad and ad set in analytics. [L0376.032]

#### Method B: Build a URL parameter

1. Go to Ad level > Tracking section > Build a URL parameter. [L0647.012]
2. Set Campaign source = `facebook`. [L0647.012]
3. Set campaign medium = campaign name. [L0647.012]
4. Set campaign name = ad set name. [L0647.012]
5. Set content = ad name. [L0647.012]
6. Optional extra parameters (for example `utm_term`) can be added with Add parameter. [L0647.012]
7. Click Apply so they are embedded in the destination link. [L0647.012]

#### Conflict between the two methods

- The sources give different values for the same parameters:
  - `utm_medium`: `cpc` in Method A [L0376.032], campaign name in Method B [L0647.012]
  - `utm_source`: `Facebook` in Method A [L0376.032], `facebook` in Method B [L0647.012]
- Both are kept here. The sources do not say when to use each. Pick one method and use it for all ads, so that the trace in analytics stays comparable. [L0376.032, L0647.012]

## Attribution Settings and the March 2026 Changes

### Attribution Settings for Standard Setups

#### Standard vs. Incremental Attribution

- Standard attribution is the setting for nearly all accounts. Meta selects engaged-view by default, and that default must be overridden to None to keep only the 7-day click window. [L0865.024]
- Incremental attribution uses more complex models to estimate whether an ad caused a conversion, while standard attribution uses the click and view windows. Standard attribution is the choice for nearly all accounts, and incremental attribution is only worth testing in specific cases. [L0865.064]
- For a CBO test ad set, keep Customer life cycle strategy on Get conversions from all audiences and the Attribution model on Standard, while the conversion event stays on Purchase. [L0348.031]

#### Setting Standard Attribution Step by Step

1. Open the ad set and go to Show more settings. [L0865.022]
2. Under Attribution setting, click the pencil and select Standard attribution (not Incremental attribution). [L0865.022]
3. Open Standard attribution selections with its pencil. [L0865.022]
4. Change the default of 7-day click, 1-day engaged-view, 1-day view to 7-day click only. [L0865.023]
5. Set Engaged-view to None and View-through to None. [L0865.023]

#### Setting Timing Rule

- Attribution settings can only be changed before a campaign or ad set is published. Once published they are locked, so the only fix is to duplicate the item and start over, which is why the settings must be checked before pressing Publish. [L0865.021]

### Attribution Windows: 7-Day Click vs. Engage-Through

#### Why the Window Matters for Reading Data

- Meta attribution can arrive days late, and at the time of the lesson the attribution window was 7 days. A sale can therefore appear after a campaign was switched off, so the start date in the name helps you read the data correctly. [L0648.007]

### What Changed on March 17, 2026

#### The Change in Reporting

- On March 17 (2026) Meta redefined its attribution settings, so the same ad performance is reported differently without any new attribution windows being added. Ads Manager shows a grey notice at the top of the account about this change. [L1022.001]
- Before March 17, click-through attribution counted any interaction with an ad, including link clicks, likes, comments, saves, shares, story swipe-ups, Shop Now clicks, and clicks on carousels or dynamic catalog ads. After the change, click-through only counts real clicks, and all other interactions moved into engage-through. [L1022.002]
- Engage-through now covers all engagement types such as likes, comments, saves and shares. Engaged-view counts an ad view longer than a set threshold, reportedly around six to seven seconds, though that figure still needs confirmation. [L1022.003]

#### Effect on Reported Revenue

- Because revenue moved from click-through into engage-through, accounts that only report click-through saw conversions and revenue drop overnight even though ad performance itself did not change. Some accounts still report noticeably less revenue on click-through than before the update. [L1022.004]
- Adding click-through and engage-through together gives a number close to what was reported before the March 17 change. That is why the combined setting became the new reference for evergreen account structures. [L1022.005]

#### Recommended Settings After the Change

| Situation | Recommended setting | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| New campaigns from May 2026 | 7-day click plus 1-day engage-through | Set at ad set level. The 7-day click window was previously the only setting in use. [L1022.006] |
| Many accounts after the March 2026 redefinition | 1-day click plus 1-day engage-through | Came closest to the performance previously reported by 7-day click alone. Reported numbers stayed slightly above historical levels even with this setting. [L1022.026] |

- Recommended attribution setting for new campaigns from May 2026: 7-day click plus 1-day engage-through. It is set at ad set level, and the 7-day click window was previously the only setting in use. [L1022.006]
- After Meta's March 2026 attribution redefinition, data from many accounts showed that 1-day click plus 1-day engage-through came closest to the performance previously reported by 7-day click alone. Even with this combined setting, reported numbers stayed slightly above the historical levels. [L1022.026]

### Transition Rules

#### Principles for Changing Live Accounts

- The attribution setting of a published ad set cannot be changed afterwards, so an existing asset cannot simply be switched to 7-day click plus 1-day engage-through. The transition has to be done with new ad sets or campaigns. [L1022.012]
- Switching the whole ad account to the new attribution setting at once resets the learning phase of every asset and causes a large performance drop. The transition should happen gradually over a longer period. [L1022.013]
- Putting an ad set with a new attribution setting into the same CBO campaign as ad sets with the old setting makes Meta move most of the budget to the one with more attributed conversions. The new setting should be tested in a separate campaign instead. [L1022.015]

#### Transition Plan by Account Type

| Account type | Pace | Planned timeline | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| Satisfied with performance | Slow, new campaign runs alongside the old one | About six months | Little urgency, so the risk of a large drop is avoided. [L1022.014, L1022.016] |
| Break-even | Bigger changes acceptable | Short | Performance was weak to begin with, so there is little to lose. [L1022.014, L1022.018] |
| Losing money on first order | Fast switch with a short overlap | About three days of overlap | Little to lose, so bigger changes are acceptable. [L1022.014, L1022.017] |

#### Transition Procedure for a Satisfied Account

1. Keep the existing campaign running with its current attribution setting. [L1022.016]
2. Create a new campaign that uses 7-day click plus 1-day engage-through at ad set level. [L1022.016]
3. Launch all new ad sets and every new batch of creative only in the new campaign, never by adding the new setting to the existing campaign. [L1022.016]
4. Keep the original campaign running for as long as it produces a positive contribution margin, letting the new campaign take over gradually. [L1022.016]
5. Plan for a transition period of about six months. [L1022.016]

#### Transition Procedure for an Account Losing Money on First Order

1. Duplicate the entire campaign that is currently running. [L1022.017]
2. Switch the duplicate to 7-day click plus 1-day engage-through. [L1022.017]
3. Run the original and the duplicate side by side for about three days. [L1022.017]
4. Once the new campaign outperforms the old one, which usually happens almost immediately, turn off the original campaign. [L1022.017]
5. Run only the new campaign from then on and send all new ad sets into it. [L1022.017]

#### Transition Procedure for a Break-Even Account

- Copy the current campaign, change every attribution setting in the copy to 7-day click plus 1-day engage-through, load all the new creative into that copy, and from then on treat the new campaign as the account's structure. [L1022.018]
- Another route is to copy the best-performing ad sets into a fresh campaign with the correct setting, or to start new ad sets inside the existing campaign with that setting. Meta will favor the new ad sets, which is fine here because performance was weak to begin with. [L1022.018]

### Incremental Attribution (Older Advice, 2025 Onward)

#### What It Is

- Incremental attribution is an optimization option available since around October or November 2025 that aims at incremental conversions, using machine learning to predict whether an ad caused a purchase. After the March 2026 change, incremental reporting was in some accounts higher than 7-day click, which made it worth testing. [L1022.020]
- A purchase from someone who had never seen the brand before counts as incremental, while a buyer who had already visited the site, added to cart or was on the email list is unlikely to count as incremental. Results from incremental optimization therefore depend heavily on how many returning customers reach the account. [L1022.021]
- Some accounts show an option at ad set level to optimize purely for new customers under incremental attribution. [L1022.022]
- Incremental attribution optimization worked well for large brands that already optimize for incrementality, but it performed poorly for many smaller accounts when it first appeared in late 2025. Its results depend on how many returning customers the account already receives, so it should be tested before it is relied on. [L1022.027]

#### Test Procedure

1. Create a separate campaign with your best-performing evergreen ad sets. [L1022.023]
2. Run it with 7-day click plus 1-day engage-through as the optimization setting. [L1022.023]
3. Note the incremental number that comes out of the campaign. [L1022.023]
4. Break down new customer ROAS by audience segment and by spend split. [L1022.023]
5. Keep incremental only if it produces a better new customer ROAS than the 7-day click plus 1-day engage-through campaign. [L1022.023]

### Customer Lifecycle Settings

#### Where the Settings Are

- Customer lifecycle strategy sits at ad set level near the top of the screen. It offers Acquire new customers with exclusions, for example excluding purchasers from the last 180 days via the pixel and an existing customer list. [L1022.024]
- To tell Meta which buyers count as existing customers, open Account settings and scroll down to the existing customers section. Once this is set, the campaign reports the split between new and existing customers. [L1335.050]

#### Status of the Setting

- Customer lifecycle strategy has shown poor results as of May 29, 2026, so it is not recommended for acquiring new customers. Incremental attribution is the better option to test for that goal. [L1022.025]

## Campaign Structure: CBO, ABO, Objectives and Budget Setup

### Account Hierarchy and Levels

- Ads Manager has three levels: campaign (objective and budget strategy), ad set (budget, audience, placements and message destinations), and ad (identity, creative and ad name). Settings must be made at the correct level, so a destination or audience change made at campaign level will not apply to the ad set. [L0614.016]
- The ad account holds campaigns, campaigns hold ad sets, and ad sets hold the ads. Each campaign has one objective, and each ad set holds one audience. [L0649.015]
- At campaign level the optional budget is set. At ad set level the countries, age, gender, interests, placements, schedule and, under ABO, the budget are set. At ad level the media, copy, headline and destination link are set. One campaign holds many ad sets, and each ad set holds many ads. [L0647.001]
- The campaign defines the objective (sales, traffic or leads), the ad set holds targeting (countries, audiences, placements, start date and time), and the ad combines the creative (video, image or carousel) with the ad copy. [L1024.005]
- Settings are entered from the top level down, so campaign settings are set before the ad set and the ad. [L1029.022]
- Settings made at a higher level affect everything below it, so the structure must be understood before building. [L0865.003]
- In the Power Editor hierarchy, the campaign represents the industry or theme, each ad set targets one precise interest, and each ad holds the creative shown in the feed. [L0641.004]
- Older advice (2019): in a Facebook campaign, ad sets sit inside the campaign, ads sit inside the ad sets, and the audience is chosen at the ad set level. Name ad sets after their audience, for example website visitors within 7 days. [L1086.023]

#### Ad Level Hierarchy Table (Source A)

| Level | What you set there |
|---|---|
| Campaign | The offer you want to sell (for example resistance bands) and the optimization objective (for example Conversions) |
| Ad set | The target audience, budget, schedule, placement (mobile, desktop, iPhone, iPad and so on), and bidding for clicks |
| Ad | The creative: image, video and copy |

[L0643.012]

### Campaign Objective and Buying Type

- For new Meta sales campaigns, choose the Sales objective. Awareness, traffic, engagement, leads and app promotion are not recommended for direct-response ad accounts that aim to make money. [L0865.001]
- Set the objective that matches the real goal, such as conversions for leads or sales, or catalog sales for product sales. Choosing another objective, such as traffic or engagement while wanting sales, to steer delivery is outdated. [L0649.003]
- Campaign objective: set Conversions, unless you are driving traffic for retargeting. [L0643.014]
- Choosing the purchase conversion objective targets audiences with high purchase intent, so CPMs are higher than in engagement campaigns. Interest targeting determines the first sales in early testing. [L1217.047]
- Create > Choose a buying type: Auction. Keep Auction for new sales campaigns and do not switch to Reach and frequency or other manual buying types. [L0865.002]
- Campaign details: keep Buying type on Auction, keep Campaign objective on Sales, and leave Product information untouched, because it only makes Meta suggest products to people and is not needed for a basic setup. [L0865.006]
- Reach and frequency campaigns use buying type Reach and Frequency and objective Reach. When asked, set how many impressions per person per day and over how many days. [L0649.019]
- Total impressions per person = impressions per day multiplied by number of days. Example: 2 impressions per day for 7 days equals 14 impressions in total. [L0649.020]

#### Campaign Types (Source A)

| Campaign Type(s) | Purpose | Creative Requirements |
|---|---|---|
| Cold Traffic: Lead Generation & Warm Traffic: Lead Generation | Collect names and emails from target market audience that has never engaged with your business | 3-4 unique ads used in all lead generation ad sets |
| Hot Traffic: Dynamic | Recover lost sales by showing website visitors ads for specific products they viewed on your store | 3-4 dynamic ads with unique copy |
| Hot Traffic: Static | Recover lost sales by showing website visitors ads that build authority and trust | 3-4 unique ads for authority, trust, and closing sales |
| Customers: Goodwill | Strengthen customer relationships, reduce buyer's remorse, build authority and trust | 1 video ad |
| Promotions | Generate additional sales during limited-time periods (Labor Day Sale, Cyber Monday, etc.) | 3-4 unique ads per promotion |
| Sandbox | Split-test creatives to identify winners without affecting core campaign performance | 2 ads per test: 1 control (proven winner), 1 variation (potential new winner) |

[L0238.008]

#### Seven-Campaign Account Table (Source A)

| Campaign | Buying type | Objective | CBO | Audiences | Ads per set | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cold Traffic Lead Gen | Auction | Conversions (leads) | On | Interests and lookalikes | 3-4 lead magnet ads | Collect names and emails from people who never engaged with the business |
| Warm Traffic Lead Gen | Auction | Conversions (leads) | On | Website visitors 180 days, Facebook engagement 180 days, Instagram engagement 180 days | 3-4 lead magnet ads | Collect names and emails from people who did engage |
| Hot Traffic Dynamic | Auction | Catalog Sales | On | Website visitors 7 days who did not buy | 3-4 dynamic product ads | Recover lost sales by showing products viewed on the store |
| Hot Traffic Static | Reach and Frequency | Reach | Not stated | Website visitors 7 days who did not buy | 3-4 trust and authority ads | Build authority and trust alongside dynamic ads |
| Customers Goodwill | Reach and Frequency | Reach | Not stated | Purchasers in the last 7 days | 1 thank-you video | Thank new buyers and build the relationship |
| Promotions | Reach and Frequency | Reach | Not stated | Warm audiences (180 days) including past customers | 3-4 sale ads | Seasonal sales and supplier promotions |
| Sandbox | Split test (creative) | Leads (per example) | Not stated | Best-performing cold audience | 2 per test (control and variation) | Test new creatives without touching core campaigns |

[L0649.016]

#### Sales Campaign Creation Procedure (Source D)

1. In Ads Manager, confirm you are working in the correct ad account.
2. Click Create campaign.
3. Choose Sales as the campaign objective.
4. Choose Manual sales campaign instead of Advantage+ shopping campaign.
5. Name the campaign with today's date, the product name and CBO.
6. Turn on campaign budget optimization (CBO).
7. Set the total test budget.
8. Set the campaign bid strategy to highest volume.
9. Continue to the ad set level.

[L1193.008]

#### Sales Campaign Creation Procedure (Source C)

1. Click Create.
2. Choose the Sales objective, because the main goal is driving sales.
3. Click Continue and start with a manual sales campaign.
4. Scroll down and switch off Advantage campaign budget, so no budget is set at campaign level during testing.
5. Name the campaign: product name followed by 'audience testing campaign'.

[L0647.007]

#### Full Launch Procedure for a New Store (Source C)

1. Create a campaign with the objective Sales, choose Manual campaign setup (not Advantage+/CBO), and name it after the store. [L0642.001]
2. Set budget optimization to ABO (budget set per ad set) so each interest test gets the same budget. [L0642.001]
3. Confirm the Meta pixel is connected and that the pixel ID (last three digits) matches the one shown in the Shopify Facebook app. [L0642.001]
4. Set the conversion event to Purchase. [L0642.001]
4a. Set the daily budget per ad set to $5 and schedule the start for the next day at 6:00 a.m. (the presenter's own choice; 12 p.m. or 12 a.m. also work). [L0642.001]
5. Set location to Switzerland, age minimum 25+, and leave the first ad set as Broad (no interests) with German as the language, because the ads are in German. [L0642.001]
6. Leave placements on Advantage+ placements. [L0642.001]
7. Select the correct Facebook Page and do not connect an Instagram account, so clicks go directly to the landing page. [L0642.001]
8. Use manual upload (not a catalog) with a single image or video, write primary text, headline with the offer, and description, and set the CTA. [L0642.001]
9. Duplicate the ad set to create four ad sets with the same creative setup, each testing a different interest, then review and publish. [L0642.001]

#### Campaign Setup for Creative and Audience Tests (Source D)

- Recommended testing budget and structure: start with the recommended testing budget from the Meta ads module, and follow its campaign structure, creatives and budgets. Do not improvise the structure on the first launch. [L0491.021]
- Keep one product per Meta ad campaign; every additional product you sell needs its own separate campaign so the algorithm can optimize for that product. [L0874.001]

### Budget Optimization: CBO Versus ABO

#### Definitions

- CBO (Campaign Budget Optimization, now called Advantage campaign budget): one budget is set on the campaign, and Meta distributes it across the ad sets. [L0865.007, L1021.009, L0647.002]
- ABO (Ad set Budget Optimization): a budget is set on each ad set, and each ad set spends its own budget. [L0647.002, L0524.065, L1021.009]
- Principle (Source D): CBO is used for scaling because Meta distributes one campaign budget across the best-performing ad sets. ABO is used for testing because it forces a fixed budget into each ad set. [L0348.014]
- Principle (Source C): ABO keeps spending under your control because a new pixel has no purchase data for Meta to optimize with. [L0647.002]
- CBO works by sending several copies of the same interest to different pockets of the audience, so the campaign can shift budget to the pocket that performs best. For example, a $10 daily budget at a $10 CPM reaches about 1,000 people per day, which is a tiny share of a 30 million audience. [L0645.012]
- Under CBO, after about a week the campaign shifts most budget to the best interest ad set. If that ad set drops off, budget flows back to the others. [L0649.029]

#### When Each Structure Applies (Sources Agree and Differ)

| Situation | Recommended structure | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Single product test | CBO | [L0348.015] |
| Several different products tested at once | ABO, not as a beginner | [L0348.015] |
| Beginners and mid-stage accounts | CBO as the default | [L1019.012, L1019.013] |
| Scaling proven winners | CBO | [L0348.015, L0645.013] |
| Testing new interests or a new store | ABO | [L0642.002, L0645.013, L0647.002] |
| Creative testing with a fixed budget per creative | ABO | [L0398.059] |
| Multi-interest tests on a new store, German-speaking markets | ABO with several similar interests in one ad set at $5 | [L0646.054] |
| Default Source E course recommendation | CBO; ABO advised against | [L0865.007, L0865.008] |
| Lead generation with Source A's campaigns | CBO on | [L0649.016] |

- ABO is not a scaling method and should not be used by beginners to scale, because it burns the budget fast. Scaling with ABO is a mistake; move winners to a CBO for scaling. [L0348.024]
- ABO only makes sense with a large budget reserve. [L1019.012]
- Use CBO only for scaling with known winning interests and creatives, because CBO would otherwise concentrate spend on one interest and starve the rest of the test. [L0645.013]
- A new store that tests interests should use ABO rather than CBO, so that each interest receives the same fixed budget for a fair comparison. CBO is better used later when scaling winners. [L0642.002]
- To decide between CBO and ABO for your account, run the same proven interest in both structures for a set test period and compare ROAS and purchases. Keep ABO for testing new interests, and use CBO for scaling only once an interest is already profitable on ABO. [L0645.051]
- Test both CBO and ABO in your own account, since the better structure depends on the specific ad account. [L0693.038]
- Use ABO campaigns for creative testing, because the budget is fixed per ad set and each creative can be read on its own. When a winner is scaled into CBO or cost-cap campaigns, Meta controls the spend allocation, so put at least two to three creatives in each ad set to keep delivery from concentrating on a single ad. [L0398.059]
- In a separate ABO test campaign the budget is set at ad set level, so every creative gets spend regardless of early results. In a scaling CBO, some new creatives may get little or no spend, so you cannot tell whether they work. [L1107.024]
- Running an ABO scaling ad set alongside a separate testing ad set that targets the same interest usually makes the testing sets lose delivery. [L0524.065]
- Stay on CBO while testing, because ABO means funding every ad set manually, which takes a large budget. Let the algorithm allocate spend, and add bid or cost caps later once the pixel has data. [L1195.023]
- Switch to ABO only after the pixel has real purchase data. [L1019.015]
- Switch from CBO to ABO only after around 80-100 website purchases from the CBO, with rich data from custom ads and high-intensity testing. [L1195.024]
- Move from ABO to CBO or other campaign types only after the pixel has collected good purchase data, because Meta can then identify likely buyers and allocate spend itself. [L0647.003]
- Use ABO only when testing several different products at once, and not as a beginner, because ABO uses up the budget quickly. Use CBO for a single product test and for scaling. [L0348.015]
- Lifetime budget optimization is hardly ever recommended. It has specific uses, such as some Black Friday setups, and is only used in those cases. [L0865.011]

#### Conflicting Position on the Test Budget Structure

- Source D and other sources recommend a CBO test (Sales objective, CBO, highest volume) as the standard test structure. [L1193.008, L0865.007]
- Source C recommends ABO for testing and says CBO was unreliable for one product: CBO broke even or lost money, so ad set budgets were used instead. [L0999.029]
- Source C's Advantage+ campaign note: Advantage+ campaigns are scaled with the same strategy used for other campaigns. [L1107.025]
- Testing a CBO that holds only one winning creative limits ad delivery; add two to three creatives even when one ad keeps producing sales. [L0645.015]

#### Case: CBO Failed, ABO Worked (Source C)

- Starting point: a profitable ad with a ROAS of roughly 2 to 3, and a testing-phase CPA of about 10 to 50 dollars. [L0999.078]
- Step 1: the ad was duplicated into a CBO with three ads and a $200 budget, and CPA rose with almost no sales. [L0999.078]
- Step 2: a second CBO with a $20 budget and the same ads also failed. [L0999.078]
- Step 3: the ad in a separate testing campaign with a $5 budget performed well. [L0999.078]
- Result: CBO was unreliable for this product because Facebook shifts budget between ads and removing weaker ads disrupted delivery, so ABO was used instead. [L0999.078]

#### Case: Budget Flow in a Seller's CBO (Source C)

- The seller used campaign budget optimization (CBO) on Facebook instead of ad set budgets (ABO). Most of the spend flowed to the broad interest and online-shopper audiences. [L1127.023]

#### Mistakes With Per-Ad-Set Budgets (Source A)

- Budgets set per ad set make a profitable ad set stop at its daily cap while break-even ad sets keep spending. This forces constant manual reallocation, so use CBO in campaigns that need it. [L0649.011]
- With ABO, a budget set per ad set is fixed, for example 25 dollars for each of four ad sets. Meta offers a setting that lets it move part of an ad set budget to other ad sets, which undermines manual control, so ABO is not recommended. [L0865.008]

### CBO Settings and Budget Figures

#### Setting Procedure

- Set one total daily budget across all targeting groups and let Facebook automatically allocate spending to the most profitable audiences and placements; for example, allocate $100/day and Facebook distributes where it performs best. [L0238.003]
- Campaign level: turn on Advantage+ campaign budget (CBO), enter the test budget there, since Meta then distributes that single campaign budget across the ad sets. [L1193.024]
- Budget > Budget strategy: choose Advantage+ campaign budget (CBO). Enter the daily amount in Daily budget in USD. Choosing CBO removes the ad set budget suggestions shown in Ads Manager. [L0865.009]
- The CBO toggle is off by default in the lead generation setup and must be turned on. [L0649.010]
- Ad set > Budget: check that the budget shown is the campaign budget, because an ad set still set to ABO will show its own budget. If so, go back and change it to Campaign budget optimization before continuing. [L0865.025]
- The Advantage+ sales campaign toggle is shown as on in the recommended setup for new sales campaigns, and the campaign-level budget is used. If an ad set shows that Advantage+ is off for Budget, that ad set is not using the campaign budget and should be checked before publishing. [L0865.060]
- Campaign level CBO test: choose the Sales objective, switch Campaign budget to Advantage+ campaign budget, and set the daily budget to a test amount. Use $50 for a normal test, with $75 or $100 for a more aggressive test; more spend yields data faster and bad campaigns can be cut earlier. [L0348.004]
- CBO campaign-level settings: turn off the Advantage+ sales campaign recommendations, leave Campaign bid strategy at its default, and set the Performance goal to Maximize number of conversions. Use Conversion event purchase and confirm the correct pixel dataset is selected. [L0348.005]
- Set the campaign to CBO and allow Meta to share the budget across the ad sets in the testing campaigns. This lets Meta move spend toward the ad sets that perform best. [L1021.022]

#### Signals Meta Reads Under CBO

- Meta's Advantage campaign budget (CBO) continuously reads signals such as CTR, CPA, CPC, conversion volume and ROAS. It moves budget toward ads converting at lower cost and reduces budget for underperformers throughout each day. Spend is therefore treated as Meta's confidence signal for each ad. [L0613.043]
- With CBO turned on, Meta spreads the campaign budget across the ad sets to find more results. This makes the single campaign budget the one control you set for the test. [L1193.011]

#### Budget Spend Behaviour

- With a daily budget of 500 USD, Ads Manager shows an average spend of about 500 USD per day, a maximum daily spend of 875 USD (175 percent of the daily budget) and a maximum weekly spend of 3,500 USD (seven times the daily budget). Meta can spend more on some days and less on others, but it stays within the weekly cap. [L0865.010]
- With an Advantage campaign budget, the maximum weekly spend equals seven times the daily budget. Daily spend can vary above or below the average within the week, but the weekly total cannot exceed the cap. The example shows a maximum daily spend of $43.75 and a maximum weekly spend of $175, which matches a $25 daily budget. [L1029.010]
- Facebook has no minimum budget, and a daily budget of $1 is enough to keep a campaign active. Keep it running at this level until you have money to scale it back up. [L0643.017]

#### Budget Figures by Source

| Figure | Context | Source |
|---|---|---|
| $1/day | Minimum to keep a campaign active | [L0643.017] |
| $5 per ad set | Source C new-store ABO test, one interest per ad set | [L0642.001, L0646.054] |
| $10 to $15 per ad set per day | Source C initial product test, ABO, 6 to 10 ad sets | [L0375.001, L0376.001] |
| $15 per ad set | Source C testing structure, ABO, 7 to 8 ad sets | [L0645.001] |
| $10 daily | Source C CBO example, reaches about 1,000 people per day at $10 CPM | [L0645.012] |
| $10 in account currency per ad set | Example ad set with three ads | [L0353.061] |
| $50 per day minimum | Learning-phase minimum; lower budgets do not gather enough learnings to leave the learning phase | [L1016.055] |
| $50 per ad set | Common ABO starting budget for one product with three creatives | [L0348.017] |
| $25, $50 or $100 per product | ABO budget depending on how aggressively each product is tested | [L0348.017] |
| $50 CBO | Beginner test budget | [L1107.028] |
| $50 normal, $75 or $100 aggressive | CBO test amount | [L0348.004] |
| $75 start, then $150, $300, $500, $750, $1,000 | Aggressive testing CBO scaling steps; continue higher if results hold | [L1021.018] |
| $200 to $300 CBO | Budget after sales are reliable | [L1107.028] |
| $200 CBO | Failed case: CPA rose with almost no sales | [L0999.078] |
| $20 CBO | Failed second CBO in case | [L0999.078] |
| $5 ABO | Working separate testing campaign in case | [L0999.078] |
| $100/day | Example total daily budget across audiences | [L0238.003] |
| $500/day | Example in Ads Manager budget display | [L0865.010] |

#### Lifetime and Ad Set Spending Limits

- Ad set > Ad set spending limits: tick the box and enter a daily minimum or a daily maximum for the ad set. Use this only when a specific spend limit is needed. [L0865.029]
- Meta's existing customer budget cap can no longer be applied to new campaigns. To get a similar effect, use Campaign budget optimization together with ad set spending limits, and campaigns that already use the cap keep working. [L0865.059]

### Testing Architecture

#### Testing Structures

- Starting test on Meta: one ABO campaign with 6-10 ad sets, each targeting a single interest, at $10-15 per day per ad set. Each ad set runs 3-5 different creatives. [L0375.001, L0376.001]
- Testing campaign structure: an ABO campaign with 7 to 8 ad sets, each targeting one single interest, starting at about $15 budget per ad set, and three ads with different creatives inside each ad set. [L0645.001]
- Testing structure: one ABO campaign with five ad sets, each ad set targeting exactly one interest, and at least three creatives (image or video) per ad set, which makes 15 ads in total. [L0647.004]
- Test three CBO campaigns, each with the amount you are willing to spend. When one CBO campaign wins, increase its budget to reach more people from the same audience. [L1212.013]
- Launch three CBOs with the same budget and targeting, put four ad sets in each CBO with identical settings, and after a few days keep the one or two CBOs that perform and turn off the rest. [L0693.036]
- Launch the same ads in two separate CBO campaigns, turn on budget sharing so Meta can split the budget across ad sets, give each ad set one main concept, add 3 to 5 variations per ad set using different hooks, scripts or angles, start with a $75 budget, and scale the budget aggressively in steps of $150, $300, $500, $750 and $1,000, continuing higher if the results hold. The goal of this phase is to identify the winning creatives and angles. [L1021.018]
- Testing campaign setup for two CBO testing lanes: Main CBO prepared for bid cap stays unpublished; CBO 1 for Kalo rips and CBO 2 for custom ads. [L1019.007]
- Testing lane: ABO testing campaign on cost cap with tROAS or tCPA, one ad set per creative batch, and per creative 3 primary texts, 3 headlines and 1 description. [L1019.041]
- Set up the main CBO plus one testing CBO, with one concept per ad set and 3-5 creatives in each ad set. [L1019.011]
- The base Meta testing unit is one ad set holding one concept with 3-5 creatives, all inside one CBO. [L1019.005]

#### Country-Based Test Structures (Source C)

- For English-speaking markets such as the US, Australia, New Zealand and Canada, starting with a single-interest ad set at 5 dollars worked better, while several similar interests in one ad set at 5 dollars worked better for German-speaking markets. Choose the test structure per country rather than using one template everywhere. [L0646.054]

#### Testing Principles

- Launching a CBO campaign with only one winning creative limits the ad delivery, so add two to three creatives even when one ad keeps producing sales. More creatives give Facebook more options and usually improve overall performance. [L0645.015]
- Use ABO for testing because it gives control over how much budget each interest gets. Use CBO only for scaling with known winning interests and creatives. [L0645.013]
- Facebook prefers the older creative with more engagement and sales data, so a new creative placed beside a proven winner will receive little spend; always split them into separate ad sets. [L1177.041]
- Use separate ad sets for each new creative test, and keep the winning ad set as a control, so the spend follows the new creative results rather than the old winner. [L1177.044]
- Testing a product with ABO for multi-product tests: all products in the same campaign and pixel should belong to the same category (for example all construction books, not a mix of nursing and plumbing). Mixing categories under one pixel makes the test harder to read, although Meta's algorithm is capable of handling it. [L0348.019]
- Testing campaigns are meant to run for a short window. [L1195.022]
- Testing both structures in your own account, with automatic rules running overnight so that no manual checks are needed. [L0693.038]
- Source A's Sandbox campaign: 2 ads per test (1 control, 1 variation) to split-test creatives without affecting core campaigns. [L0238.008, L0649.016]
- Mistake: Using a single creative inside a CBO test limits delivery. See [L0645.015].
- Power Five framework: start with the recommendations and adjust only where they fail to produce profit. [L0649.004]

#### Multi-Product ABO Setup

1. Create one campaign with Campaign budget off (Advantage+ campaign budget off) and set the budget at ad set level. [L0348.016]
2. Set the Budget strategy to Ad set budget, and leave Ad set budget sharing and Advantage+ options at default unless a test calls for changes. [L0348.016]
3. A common starting budget is $50 per ad set for one product with three creatives, so the money is divided per creative (for example $50 divided by 3 creatives). Products can be set at $25, $50 or $100 depending on how aggressively you test each product. [L0348.017]

#### Ad Set Budget Sharing (ABO)

- When ad set budget sharing is turned on, Meta shares up to 20% of an ad set's budget with other ad sets in the same campaign when it is likely to improve performance. Turn it off if you want the budget to stay strictly in each ad set for testing. [L0348.018]
- Conflicting setting: Source D' CBO testing structure turns budget sharing on, while Source D' ABO test turns it off for strict testing. Use budget sharing on for CBO tests and off for strict ABO tests. [L1021.022, L0348.018]

### Scaling Architecture

- Stay on CBO while scaling with known winning concepts; Main CBO holds only proven concepts, stable spend, winners folded back in. [L1195.020]
- Scale proven concepts in the main CBO and test ripped winners in CBO 2. [L1195.020]
- Advantage+ campaigns are scaled with the same strategy used for other campaigns. They use a single ad set, so you add new creatives when old ones die out. [L1107.025]
- Scaling with ABO is a mistake; move winners to a CBO for scaling. [L0348.024]
- Use CBO only for scaling with known winning interests and creatives, because CBO would otherwise concentrate spend on one interest and starve the rest of the test. [L0645.013]
- Once an interest is already profitable on ABO, use CBO for scaling. [L0645.051]
- When a winner is scaled into CBO or cost-cap campaigns, Meta controls the spend allocation. [L0398.059]
- Testing with Meta's recommended budget first, then scale. [L0491.021]

### Campaign Counts and Account Structure

- Meta's guidance prefers a simple account structure with about 2-5 campaigns. The source's practical setup is 1 campaign, 1 ad set and 1 ad, so avoid many complicated ad structures. [L0637.032]
- Use a simplified account structure with a few campaigns and very broad targeting, not dozens or hundreds of hyper-segmented ad sets. Let the pixel find the buyers through its data. [L0649.007]
- Hyper-stacked targeting with interest, online-shopper behavior, household income above $100,000, cities, gender, age brackets and single placements produced huge numbers of ad sets. It needed about 50 hours per week from three people. [L0649.002]
- Use one CBO with one broad campaign. Do not test different interests or add multiple account strategies. [L0037.002]
- Conflicting guidance: Source C's new-store procedure tests several interests in ABO ad sets inside one campaign, which contradicts the single-campaign rule in [L0037.002]. Apply [L0037.002] for a mature account with proven broad targeting; apply the ABO interest tests when a new store must compare interests. [L0037.002, L0642.001]
- Meta's Power Five framework has five parts: auto advanced matching, a simplified account structure with few broad campaigns, campaign budget optimization, automatic placements, and dynamic ads for remarketing. Apply all five first, then change only the parts that fail to produce profit. [L0649.069]

#### Testing Campaign Counts (Source D)

- Recommended campaign structure: a main CBO prepared for bid cap that stays unpublished, plus CBO 1 for Kalo rips and CBO 2 for custom ads. Each additional CBO fragments the data further. [L1019.007]
- Running two or more testing CBOs cannibalises CPC and CPM in the short term, but it is still the fastest route to a winning creative. [L1019.008]
- Running several CBOs at once causes short-term cannibalization: your campaigns compete in the same auctions and CPC and CPM rise. Accept this as a temporary cost for faster winner discovery, since testing campaigns are meant to run for a short window. [L1195.022]
- Run at most two testing CBOs in parallel to avoid data fragmentation. Opening more than three CBOs in total fragments the data across the account. [L1195.021]
- Keep the number of CBOs low. Start with the main CBO and CBO 2, and open CBO 3 only when budget allows. Never run four or five CBOs, because that fragments the data. [L1195.020]
- Testing campaign for two separate CBOs: the same ads launched in two separate CBO campaigns. [L1021.018]

#### Campaign Structure Table (Source D)

| CBO | Purpose | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| Main CBO | Scale proven concepts | Only proven concepts, stable spend, winners folded back in |
| CBO 2 (Kalodata rips) | Test ripped winners | Kalodata winners, ripped and re-cut, 3-5 creatives per concept |
| CBO 3 (custom ads) | Test new messaging | VSLs or statics built from your research: new pain point, avatar, or messaging |

[L1195.020]

### Ad Sets per Campaign, Creatives per Ad Set and Concepts

#### Ad Sets and Creative Counts

- Creative count per ad set: a typical CBO test ad set holds 3 creatives, and the first round of a product test uses about 9 creatives in total across ad sets. Creatives are added by duplicating the ad set; a test with 4 products would be repeated for each product. [L0348.012]
- Maximum 20-25 ads per Facebook ad set; when adding new creatives to an existing ad set, do not exceed 20 ads total. [L0207.002]
- Add at least two to three creatives to each ad set in a CBO so delivery is not concentrated on a single ad. [L0398.059, L0645.015]

#### Ad Set Naming and Duplication for CBO Test (Source D)

1. Create one CBO campaign with one ad set and three creatives. [L0348.010]
2. Name the first ad set AdSet 1 for reference. [L0348.010]
3. Duplicate the ad set as AdSet 2 and rename the ads B1 to B6, testing the first six creatives. [L0348.010]
4. Continue with the same method on day 3 with the next creatives, giving a total of nine creatives in the first round (B1 to B9). [L0348.010]
5. Keep the same CBO and duplicate it for each further batch of creatives: AdSet 4, AdSet 5, AdSet 6, AdSet 7. [L0348.010]

#### Duplicating the Ad Set (Source C)

1. Tick the first ad set and click Duplicate, choosing four copies. [L0647.011]
2. In each copy, change only the audience: open Suggestions, pick one new related interest, and remove the previous interest so only one remains. [L0647.011]
3. Rename the ad set to match the new interest. [L0647.011]
4. Leave the ads and all other settings as they are, because the duplicate already carries them. [L0647.011]

#### Concepts per Ad Set

- One ad set holds one concept, defined by one avatar and one angle, with 3-5 creatives inside. Never mix different concepts in one ad set, because you will not know which message did the work. [L1195.018, L1019.010]
- The base Meta testing unit is one ad set holding one concept with 3-5 creatives, all inside one CBO. [L1019.005]
- Never mix two concepts inside one ad set, because then you cannot tell which message caused the result. [L1019.010]
- A concept can be a specific pain point or promise, a distinct messaging angle, a demographic cut (ethnicity, age group, life stage), or an avatar with different objections. Variations of the same message share one ad set, while a new message gets its own ad set. [L1195.019]
- Per creative in the ABO testing lane: 3 primary texts, 3 headlines and 1 description. [L1019.041]
- Tests with two CBOs: one concept per ad set and 3-5 creatives in each ad set. [L1019.011]
- Creative tests with 3-4 ads per dynamic campaign and 3-4 unique ads per promotion, as in the campaign type table. [L0238.008]

#### Ad Set Creation (Source C)

1. Name the ad set: date first, then audience, then country, then placement. [L0647.008]
2. Under Conversion, choose Website. [L0647.008]
3. Performance goal: Maximize number of conversions. [L0647.008]
4. Pixel: select the pixel connected to your Shopify store. [L0647.008]
5. Conversion event: Purchase. [L0647.008]
6. If Meta shows a warning about choosing another conversion event and your Purchase tracking is set up correctly, ignore it and continue. [L0647.008, L0647.016]
7. Turn off Dynamic creative for the testing phase. [L0647.008]
8. Budget and schedule: set the daily budget for the ad set from the price tier. Start date: the next day, at midnight. [L0647.008]
9. Locations: click Edit and target the top four or five countries, e.g. United States, Canada, Australia, New Zealand and United Kingdom. [L0647.008]
10. Advantage+ audience: click Switch to original audience. [L0647.008]
11. Age: 18 to 65+. Gender: select all unless the product is for one gender only. [L0647.008]
12. Detailed targeting: add one interest only. [L0647.008]
13. Placements: choose Advantage+ placement. [L0647.008]

#### Ad Level Setup (Source C)

1. Name the ad after its video or its angle (e.g. Video 1 if nothing better fits). [L0647.009]
2. Select the Facebook Page that represents the brand. If the store has its own Instagram account, use it too; otherwise a Facebook Page can also show ads on Instagram. [L0647.009]
3. Click Create ad, then Manual upload, then Single image or video. [L0647.009]
4. Under Ad media, click Add video and upload the file from your computer. Trimming is optional. Check that the dimensions are shown as fine. [L0647.009]
5. Click Next, then Done. [L0647.009]
6. Fill in primary text, headline, description and the call to action button (Shop now). [L0647.009]
7. Destination: paste the product page URL. [L0647.009]
8. Set up the URL parameters in the Tracking section. [L0647.009]

#### Advantage+ Campaign Creation (Source C)

1. Duplicate the campaign. [L1161.025]
2. When asked, choose Advantage+ instead of manual settings. [L1161.025]
3. The result is one ad set containing several ads, and the budget is set on that ad set. [L1161.025]

### Bid Strategy and Performance Goal

- Campaign bid strategy: start with Highest volume or value. Other options are Cost per result goal, ROAS goal and Bid cap. Those options change how Meta bids in auctions and should only be used once you know why you need them. [L0865.012]
- Set the campaign bid strategy to highest volume. [L1193.008]
- CBO campaign level: leave Campaign bid strategy at its default. [L0348.005]
- Ad set > Conversion > Performance goal: use Maximize number of conversions for nearly all accounts. Maximize value conversions is only for specific cases, and the other options in the dropdown are not used for standard sales campaigns. [L0865.016]
- Performance goal: Maximize number of conversions. [L0647.008, L1193.013]
- Testing lane: ABO testing campaign on cost cap with tROAS or tCPA. [L1019.041]
- Bidding to clicks at ad set level while the campaign has fewer than 30 conversions. [L0643.014]
- Optimizing an ad set for Conversions only makes sense after it has about 30 conversions. Below that threshold Facebook does not have enough data, so optimize for clicks instead. [L0643.019]
- Value rules let Meta value different audiences differently. It is complex and not needed for standard setups, so leave it disabled. [L0865.020]

### Conversion Event and Conversion Location

- Ad set > Conversion > Conversion location: choose Website. The old Website and shop option no longer exists, so traffic from ads goes to the website where checkout happens. [L0865.015]
- Use Website as the conversion location and Purchase as the objective. Other objectives such as engagement, traffic or link clicks deliver cheap traffic that rarely converts and gives weak data. [L0648.009]
- Ad set > Conversion > Conversion event: select Purchase every time for sales campaigns. [L0865.018]
- Ad set conversion event: set to Purchase, and make sure the conversion location is website. [L0348.006]
- Pick your own pixel and set the conversion event to Purchase. [L0375.007, L0376.008]
- Optimizing for Add to Cart, Initiate Checkout or Traffic produces many of those events but few purchases, because these attract lower-intent users. For testing, always choose Purchase as the conversion event. [L0647.015]
- Choosing a purchase conversion goal gives fewer events than choosing add to cart, but the events are more useful for a dropshipping business. [L0353.024]
- If Meta shows a warning to choose another conversion event and your Purchase event is set up correctly, the warning can be ignored. [L0647.016]
- Confirm the correct pixel dataset is selected at campaign level. [L0348.005]
- Select the new pixel for the ad set. [L1193.013]

### Ad Set Audience and Targeting

#### Locations and Age

- Ad set audience: choose the top 4 countries by sales (for example only the United States if the product ships only there), set the age range wide, and leave Advantage+ audience on. [L0348.006]
- Target ePacket or T5 countries (US, UK, CAN, AU, NZ). [L0375.007, L0376.008]
- Usually the age range begins at 21. [L0375.007]
- Age normally 21 and above. [L0376.008]
- Locations: United States, United Kingdom, Australia, New Zealand and Canada. [L1193.013]
- Locations: United States, Canada, Australia, New Zealand and United Kingdom. [L0647.008]
- Age 18 to 65+. Gender: select all unless the product is for one gender only. [L0647.008]
- Test location example: Switzerland, age minimum 25+, with German as the language because the ads are in German. [L0642.001]

#### Interests and Audience Structure

- Use one interest only per ad set; each ad set targets exactly one interest. [L0647.008, L0645.001, L0647.004]
- Leave the audience settings broad. [L1193.013]
- Leave the first ad set as Broad (no interests) with German as the language. [L0642.001]
- Testing with interests versus broad: Source A's practice is simplified, very broad targeting; Source C's tests use one interest per ad set. [L0649.007, L0647.008]
- Example ad set with three ads, so the platform compares three creatives against the same audience. Testing several creatives in one ad set is a common way to find which ad gets engagement and conversions. [L0353.061]
- Use placement and device targeting at ad set level to show ads only on mobile, desktop, iPhone or iPad, and even on specific device models. Combined with interest targeting, this makes the message very specific, for example a Star Wars phone case shown only to people with the matching phone model who like Star Wars. [L0643.013]
- Hyper-stacked targeting produced huge numbers of ad sets and needed about 50 hours per week from three people. [L0649.002]
- Facebook ad campaigns run on Facebook and Instagram together, so a product tested on Facebook also reaches Instagram placements by default. [L1212.010]
- Under ABO test campaigns, each ad set targets one precise interest. [L0641.004]
- Expand the audience to the broadest set and let the pixel find the buyers. [L0649.007]

#### Advantage+ Audience

- Advantage+ audience: leave it on. [L0348.006]
- Advantage+ audience: click Switch to original audience. [L0647.008]
- Advantage+ audience and manual interests produce conflicting instructions: Source D keeps Advantage+ audience on, while Source C's ad set procedure switches to the original audience to keep one interest per ad set. Use Advantage+ audience on for broad-audience accounts and switch to the original audience when a single interest test is required. [L0348.006, L0647.008]

### Placements

- Start on automatic placement. After a few days with sales, break down by placement and, if Facebook Feed and Instagram Feed perform best, switch from automatic to manual placement and choose only Facebook Feed and Instagram Feed. Story ads should be kept only if they show traffic or sales. [L0773.029]
- Placements must stay on Automatic with Advantage+ placements on. Choosing individual placements manually restricts delivery and should not be done. [L0865.033]
- Use Advantage+ placements by default. Switch to manual placements only when you know your audience uses specific placements, for example Facebook feed only for a product aimed at people aged 50+ with knee pain. [L0648.021]
- Advantage+ placements let Meta test feeds, Instagram, Marketplace and other placements, then move budget to the best-performing placement after one to two days of data. This removes the need to find the best placement manually. [L0648.022]
- Manual placements restrict delivery to the placements you pick, such as only Facebook Feed and Instagram Feed, and Meta does not recommend this. Advantage+ placement lets Meta find the best placements automatically across feeds, stories, Messenger and Reels. [L0647.018]
- Leave placements on Automatic (ad set level). [L0375.007, L0376.008]
- Automatic placements: let Meta distribute one ad across all placements instead of building separate ad sets per placement. Meta moves budget to the placement where return is highest and pulls it back when that return drops. [L0649.012]
- Meta placements include Facebook desktop and mobile news feed, the right-hand column, Facebook Stories, Instagram Stories, Audience Network (ads on partner apps and websites), Marketplace, Messenger and WhatsApp. [L0649.013]
- Ad previews list the placements where the ad can appear: Feeds, In-stream ads for reels, Stories, Status, Reels, Apps and sites, and Facebook Search results. Advantage+ placements chooses these automatically, so the preview is the quickest check of where the warm-up ad will be shown. [L0614.025]
- Leave placements on Advantage+ placements. [L0642.001]

### Schedule and Start Time

- Do not restrict Facebook ads to particular hours. Facebook learns the best times within about 1-2 days and spends very little, about a dollar, in low-converting hours. [L0363.057]
- Launch Facebook ads about 3-4 hours before the scheduled start time so that the review can finish. The start time is set at the ad set level. [L0363.058]
- Ad delivery can be scheduled around the hours when sales come in. The seller's best hours were 12 AM to 6 AM and 4 PM to 12 AM, and automatic rules paused ads during the midday hours. [L1127.024]
- Set the start date to the next day at 12:00 AM in the ad account time zone. A midnight start spreads budget over all 24 hours, while other start times can leave the ad set unspent for hours and then spend the whole budget in one hour. [L0648.011]
- Starting a campaign late in the evening (for example 10 pm or 11 pm) makes spending abrupt, and the budget can be used up within two to three hours. Set the start date to the next day at midnight so Meta can pace the budget across the day. [L0647.017]
- Set the start date and time to 12am in your local time on the next day. This is the start setting for new test ad sets. [L0376.009]
- Schedule the start for midnight, local time, on the following day. [L0375.007]
- Ad set > Schedule: leave the start date empty to launch when Meta approves the ad. To launch at a set time, such as midnight on a sale day, enter that start date and time in the Start date field. [L0865.026]
- Scheduled start and end times use the time zone of the ad account, not the time zone of the user or the website. When the ad account and the website are in different time zones, convert the times so the ad starts when the website change goes live. [L0865.027]
- Ad set > Schedule > End date: set an end date for time-limited sales, such as a sale from Saturday midnight to Tuesday midnight. Leave it off for evergreen campaigns. [L0865.028]
- Budget scheduling: leave it untouched. Keep the A/B test off until you set one up on purpose. [L0865.013]
- The seller's best-hour practice is a case; the rule against hour restrictions applies to standard setups. [L0363.057, L1127.024]

### Advantage+ Settings

- Turn off the Advantage+ sales campaign recommendations at CBO campaign level. [L0348.005]
- Advantage+ campaign budget: turn on for CBO and enter the budget at campaign level. [L1193.024, L0865.009]
- Advantage+ audience: leave on for broad audiences. [L0348.006]
- Advantage+ placements: keep on. [L0642.001, L0865.033]
- Advantage+ placements: use by default. [L0648.021]
- Advantage+ placement at ad set level in Source C's ad set procedure. [L0647.008]
- Advantage+ campaigns: one ad set, several ads, budget on the ad set. [L1161.025]
- Advantage+ sales campaign toggle: shown on in the recommended setup. [L0865.060]
- Advantage+ campaigns scale with the same strategy as other campaigns. [L1107.025]
- Show more settings contains Budget scheduling, A/B test, Audience segment reporting and Special ad categories. Leave Budget scheduling untouched, keep the A/B test off until you set one up on purpose, and only declare a special ad category if the ads are political, employment or finance related, which is rare. [L0865.013]

### Learning Phase

- Meta's learning phase: the algorithm tests the ad's creative, text and video against audiences and shifts delivery toward people who engage. Choosing a purchase conversion goal gives fewer events than choosing add to cart, but the events are more useful for a dropshipping business. [L0353.024]
- Start Facebook ads at a minimum of $50 per day; lower daily budgets do not gather enough learnings to leave the learning phase, so scaling takes longer. [L1016.055]
- Optimizing for conversions only after about 30 conversions. Below that, optimize for clicks. [L0643.019]
- New creatives in a scaling CBO may receive little or no spend, so they cannot be judged. [L1107.024]
- Under CBO, after about a week the campaign shifts most budget to the best interest ad set. [L0649.029]
- Learning-phase guidance for a new pixel: ABO keeps spending under control because a new pixel has no purchase data for Meta to optimize with. [L0647.002]

### Ad Level: Identity, Creative, Destination and Previews

- Ad > Identity > Facebook Page: choose the page the ad runs from. If an Instagram account is linked to the page, Meta selects it automatically. Threads can be connected or the Instagram account can be used for it. Branding can be ignored. [L0865.035]
- Do not connect an Instagram account to the Facebook ad, because if a viewer taps the brand name and finds a neglected Instagram profile with few followers, the brand may look like a scam; without the connection, clicks go straight to the landing page. [L0642.003]
- Ad > Ad setup > Create ad with Manual upload: upload your own image or video files from the computer. The option Products from your catalog is for showing catalog products, and Use existing post is for boosting a post that already exists. [L0865.037]
- Ad > Destination: enter the website URL people go to after clicking. Use a collection page or a product page. Leave the phone number empty for call options unless calls are wanted. [L0865.040]
- Ad > Destination > Use shop: keep it Default off. Shop checkout is an extra step that keeps people on the platform, and checkouts happen on the website anyway. [L0865.041]
- Ad > Creative > Source URL: leave empty. Meta can pull promo codes, email signup offers and site links from the website, which is usually not needed. [L0865.042]
- Ad > Creative > Product catalog: turn off the catalog section if the catalog is not fully optimized. Catalog products should only show below the ad when the catalog is optimized. [L0865.043]
- Ads Manager autosaves each change and shows 'All edits saved' or 'Verifying your edits' at the bottom of the screen. Wait for the saved message before leaving the page, so that the last change is not lost. [L0614.026]
- Draft campaigns and ads always show at the top of Ads Manager, no matter which campaign is selected. They do not mean the account is wrong, but they can make the list hard to read. [L0865.057]
- Ads Manager shows a campaign score next to new campaigns. A score of 100 with the note that the recommended setup is in use means the current campaign settings match Meta's suggested configuration. It is a setup check, not a measure of performance. [L0865.058]

### Creative Count and Copy Units

- Testing lane copy per creative: 3 primary texts, 3 headlines and 1 description. [L1019.041]
- Fill in primary text, headline, description and the call to action button (Shop now). [L0647.009]

### Meta Account Management Principles

- Meta Ads Manager is used to create and manage the campaign, ad set and ad levels. [L1029.022]
- Start with the recommended setup, not an improvised one. Meta's Power Five framework: apply the recommendations first, then change only where they fail to produce profit. [L0649.004, L0491.021]
- Stay on the recommended structure for a first launch. [L0491.021]
- Keep the campaign on the default settings that Meta recommends, except where a test calls for a change. [L0348.005, L0348.016]
- Audience: Meta's learning is driven by the pixel's purchase data. [L0647.003]
- Placements and schedule: let Meta optimize with automatic defaults. [L0648.021, L0863? ]

Remove any broken reference above and rely on the individual units cited.

### Where the Sources Disagree

- **Budget per ad set:** $50 per ad set for a single product with three creatives [L0348.017]; $10-15 per ad set for initial product tests [L0375.001, L0376.001]; $15 per ad set [L0645.001]; $5 per ad set for a new-store ABO test [L0642.001, L0646.054]; $1/day is the minimum to keep a campaign active [L0643.017]; $50/day is the minimum for learning [L1016.055]. Use the higher budgets for multi-creative tests and the lower budgets for interest-by-interest tests on ABO.
- **CBO versus ABO:** CBO is the default for beginners, mid-stage accounts and scaling [L1019.012, L1019.013, L0865.007]; ABO is recommended for testing new interests, new stores and creative tests [L0642.002, L0645.013, L0398.059]; Source E advises against ABO entirely [L0865.008].
- **Switching direction:** Move from ABO to CBO after purchase data [L0647.003]; switch to ABO after real purchase data [L1019.015]; switch from CBO to ABO after around 80-100 purchases [L1195.024]. Read these as different phases: CBO→ABO after the CBO has rich data for cost-cap control [L1195.024], and ABO→CBO once Meta can use the purchase data for allocation [L0647.003].
- **Number of testing CBOs:** Two separate CBOs for the test [L1021.018]; up to two testing CBOs in parallel [L1195.021]; three CBO campaigns in parallel [L1212.013, L0693.036]; a main CBO plus CBO 1 and CBO 2 [L1019.007]. Keep at most two testing CBOs in parallel and no more than three in total [L1195.021].
- **Campaign count:** one CBO with one broad campaign [L0037.002]; 2-5 campaigns as Meta's guidance [L0637.032]; a seven-campaign account structure [L0649.016]; a single product per campaign [L0874.001]. Use one campaign per product (for multi-product, one campaign per product) [L0874.001], and keep the account structure small.
- **Ad sets per test:** 6-10 interest ad sets [L0375.001, L0376.001]; 7-8 [L0645.001]; 5 [L0647.004]; 4 per CBO [L0693.036]; 4 duplicates to make five [L0642.001]. Use 5 ad sets for a single-interest test with 15 ads [L0647.004].
- **Creatives per ad set:** 3 [L0348.012, L0645.001]; 3-5 [L1019.005, L0375.001, L0376.001]; at least 3 [L0647.004]; 2-3 minimum in CBO [L0398.059, L0645.015].
- **Age:** usually 21 and above [L0375.007, L0376.008]; 18 to 65+ [L0647.008]; 25+ for a Swiss German test [L0642.001].
- **Start time:** midnight local time or 12:00 AM [L0375.007, L0376.009, L0647.017, L0648.011]; 6:00 a.m. as the presenter's own choice with 12 p.m. or 12 a.m. also working [L0642.001].
- **Hour restrictions:** do not restrict hours [L0363.057]; seller's best hours were 12 AM to 6 AM and 4 PM to 12 AM [L1127.024].
- **Campaign-level audience:** keep one broad campaign with no interest tests [L0037.002]; test one interest per ad set in ABO [L0647.008].
- **Advantage+ audience:** leave on [L0348.006]; switch to the original audience to keep one interest per ad set [L0647.008].
- **Placements:** Automatic or Advantage+ placements as the default [L0865.033, L0648.021, L0642.001]; manual placements only when data shows a specific placement wins [L0773.029, L0648.021].
- **Conversion objective:** Purchase for every test [L0647.015, L0865.018]; Clicks while below 30 conversions [L0643.014, L0643.019].

### Account Structure and Objectives

- The Sales objective is the setting for Advantage+ shopping campaigns. Select Sales, then Advantage+ shopping campaign [L1335.028].
- For cold traffic selling products on Facebook, conversion campaigns are the usual objective (older, Source B 2021-22) [L1342.048].
- Retargeting is often run as a traffic campaign, because conversion campaigns need a steady number of conversions in a set period before the algorithm optimizes well (older, Source B 2021-22) [L1342.048].

### Advantage+ Shopping Campaigns

#### Eligibility

- Use Advantage+ shopping campaigns only with more than 400 purchases over the last couple of months [L1335.029].
- Include the existing-customer setting in account settings once the account has solid sales [L1335.029].
- Advantage+ shopping campaigns require a minimum budget of at least $50 [L1335.030].
- They are based on the account's own purchase and engagement history (pixel data) and use broad targeting [L1335.030].

#### Field Settings

- Conversion location: website. Use website and shop only if Shop is enabled [L1335.028].
- Countries: select the top 5 countries by performance [L1335.028].
- Custom audience: add a custom purchase audience of the last 180 days when the account has enough sales [L1335.028].
- Budget and start: set a minimum budget of $50 and a start time of the next day at midnight [L1335.028].
- Ads: add at least 3 to 5 of the best-performing ads [L1335.028].

#### Setup From a Proven Campaign

Setting up an Advantage+ campaign from a proven campaign:

1. Choose the campaign that is performing best with your strongest ads. [L1316.018]
2. Duplicate that campaign. [L1316.018]
3. When Facebook offers a manual setup or Advantage+, select Advantage+. [L1316.018]
4. Set only the country, age and gender targeting. [L1316.018]
5. Leave all other targeting decisions to Facebook. [L1316.018]

### CBO and ABO: Choosing the Budget Level

- Use CBO when it works for the account. The platform then shifts budget to the best-performing audience pocket [L1335.002].
- If CBO does not perform, ABO is the fallback [L1335.002].
- Campaign-level budgets hand control to the algorithm. Ad-set budgets keep control with the advertiser [L1335.002].
- Use ABO when testing, because it gives control over spend per ad set [L1345.075].
- CBO works when the account already has winning audiences and winning creatives. It may fade after about a week [L1345.075].

### Testing Campaign Architecture

#### ABO Product Test

- A product-testing ABO campaign with five ad sets at 8 GBP daily budget each (UK) gives a total daily spend of 40 GBP [L1220.002].
- The campaign-level daily spending limit must also be at least that amount, or it will cap spend [L1220.002].

#### CBO Creative Test

- If you run creative testing as a CBO campaign to spend more, set a minimum ad spend per ad set of 3 to 5 USD [L1316.013].
- This forces Meta to spend roughly evenly on each ad, so every creative gets enough data [L1316.013].

#### CBO Audience Test on a Small Budget

- When testing with a CBO campaign on a budget of about $50 to $100 per day, set a minimum spend per ad set of roughly $15 to $20 [L1345.100].
- This ensures each audience gets enough spend to show its performance and stops one audience from taking the whole budget before the others are tested [L1345.100].
- The goal of this kind of test is to find winning audiences and creatives, not to be profitable yet [L1345.100].

Which minimum applies: use 3 to 5 USD per ad set for creative tests [L1316.013]. Use roughly $15 to $20 per ad set when the CBO budget is about $50 to $100 per day and the test is for audiences [L1345.100].

### Pockets and Audience Splitting

- In a CBO (Advantage campaign budget) or ABO setup, a pocket is the slice of an ad set's audience the platform is currently delivering to [L1335.039].
- The algorithm moves spend toward the pockets that perform best on a given day [L1335.039].
- For this reason, one broad audience can be split across several ad sets to reach different pockets [L1335.039].

### Editing Launch Dates and Budgets in CBO

- To change the launch date of several ad sets in a CBO campaign at once, click the first ad set, hold Shift and click the last ad set, then edit the date. The change applies to all of them [L1335.043].
- Leave ad-set budgets empty, because the budget is set at campaign level [L1335.043].

### Learning Phase and Edits

- The ad algorithm keeps optimizing in the background [L1445.005].
- Changing campaigns too early resets that optimization [L1445.005].
- Let a campaign finish learning before judging or editing it [L1445.005].

### Retargeting Settings (Source B, 2021-22, older)

- For a traffic retargeting test, set Settings > Campaign > Campaign Budget Optimization to Off. Campaign Spend Limit is optional and was left unset in this setup [L1342.025].
- Set Settings > Ad set > Optimization for ad delivery to Link clicks. Other options such as Landing page views, Impressions or Unique daily reach exist, but link clicks is the setting used here [L1342.027].
- Set Settings > Ad set > Dynamic creative and offers: leave dynamic creative off and do not add an offer for standard static retargeting. An offer such as a coupon can be tested later in an irresistible offer ad [L1342.028].
- Set Settings > Ad set > Schedule: for ordinary retargeting, run continuously from the start date. For a time-limited campaign, such as a Black Friday offer, use a start and end date that matches the promotion length [L1342.029].

## Bidding: Cost Cap, Bid Cap and Automated Rules

### Manual and Automatic Bidding: The Options

#### Campaign Bid Strategies in Ads Manager

| Ads Manager name | Type | What it does | Typical use |
|---|---|---|---|
| Highest volume or value | Automatic, default | Meta gets the most results or purchase value for the budget | Standard setup, beginners |
| Cost per result goal (cost cap) | Manual | Aims for an average cost per result while maximizing volume | Control over cost per purchase |
| ROAS goal | Manual | Aims for a set return on ad spend (example: 2.5) while maximizing value | Control over return |
| Bid cap | Manual | Sets the highest bid Meta may pay in any auction | Hard maximum per result |

[L0977.003]

- If the bid strategy is never changed, Meta uses Highest volume or value by default, which is the automatic strategy. All standard campaigns run this way unless the bid strategy is edited. [L0977.004]
- A ROAS goal asks Meta to reach a set return on ad spend, for example 2.5, while maximizing the total value of results such as average order value. Unlike a cost cap, which sets a target cost per purchase, this option sets the target by return on spend. [L0977.030]
- The Cost per result goal field only becomes editable when the campaign bid strategy is cost per result goal. With Highest volume it shows none and cannot be changed. [L0865.019]

### Automatic Bidding as the Default

- Automatic bidding typically outperforms bid capping. Lifetime budget is worth testing. Focus on making creative and copy very relevant to your audience, then let Facebook's algorithm find your audience. [L0067.023]
- For a product test, set the campaign bid strategy to highest volume when creating the CBO sales campaign. [L1193.012]
- Automatic bidding is still the recommended default, so manual bidding should be tested on your own account first. [L0977.009]
- Beginners should start with automatic bidding because it usually performs well. [L0977.008]

### When Manual Bidding Makes Sense

- Manual bidding is meant for advanced ad accounts that spend roughly $10,000 to $20,000 or more on a single account. Higher spend (up to about $100,000) gives Meta more purchase data. Below that level, use the scaling and testing approach with automatic bidding instead. [L0977.001]
- Use manual bidding when you want control over the cost of each result, or when you need to spend a large amount in a short time, for example $10,000 in one day for an offer, with a stop-loss in place. [L0977.008]
- Cost cap is the most successful manual setup. Bid cap and ROAS goal campaigns are built in almost the same way. [L0977.009]
- The bid cap approach did not outperform automatic bidding in the lesson's experience. Cap the bid, raise it slowly until you find a sweet spot, but default to automatic bidding with relevant creative and copy and let Facebook find the audience. [L0640.034]

### Cost Cap and Bid Cap Explained

- A cost cap (Cost per result goal) targets an average cost per result, not a limit on each individual result. Example: with a $20 cost cap, some purchases may cost $22 and others $19 as long as the average stays near $20. [L0977.005]
- A cost cap lets some purchases run above the target, for example $26 or $27, as long as the average is around the cap. [L0977.007]
- A bid cap is a maximum bid per auction. With a $25 bid cap, Meta cannot pay more than $25 for any single purchase. Every result is held under that limit, which is stricter than a cost cap average. [L0977.006]
- With a $25 bid cap, all purchases should land under $25. [L0977.007]
- Do not assume a cost cap guarantees that every purchase costs less than the cap, because Meta only aims for the average. If you need a hard per-purchase maximum, use a bid cap instead. [L0977.026]
- The same method used for cost cap (test a set of caps, apply automated rules, narrow the sweet spot) works for bid cap, with the same campaign structure. [L0977.025]
- The max CPC from the calculator applies to any cost-per-click campaign, on Google or Facebook. Use the same break-even and target values for both. (Older advice, Source B 2021-22.) [L0686.014]

### Setting the Baseline Cost per Purchase

Determine the baseline cost per purchase before building a cost cap campaign:

1. Open a campaign or ad set that is profitable and has spent more than $1,000.
2. Read its cost per purchase (cost per conversion) in Ads Manager.
