- Organic follower growth is easy, but converting followers into sales is hard, so organic page growth is usually a lower priority than paid traffic. It makes sense mainly after monthly paid spend has reached a satisfactory return on ad spend. (Advice from 2024.) [L0666.016]
- Regular posting to Facebook is a positive signal for a fashion store, so posting frequency and content types should be kept up. [L0838.016]
- Post on social media a couple of times per week mainly so buyers can see the business is active and real before purchasing. This is for conversion trust, not for reaching new people. [L0941.060]
- A Facebook community about the product topic can be built first and used to get ideas for content, share it and promote offers. Content is then validated through the audience. [L0941.061]

### Algorithm Signals and Zapier Engagement Alerts

- Facebook and Instagram algorithms prioritize fast initial engagement (likes, comments, shares) on new posts. Automating team alerts to engage within minutes of publishing improves organic reach. [L0286.031]
- Create a dedicated Slack "Engagement" channel where Zapier automatically alerts team members (@channel) whenever new Instagram or Facebook posts publish, requesting likes and comments within their earliest convenience. This leverages team assets to improve algorithmic reach at no cost. [L0286.016]

### Facebook Groups for Engagement and Sales

- Use active niche Facebook groups to build sales:
  1. Identify groups in your niche that are active.
  2. Build authority by adding value to discussions and staying active.
  3. Promote your sales inside the group.
  
  This approach requires a personal account. [L0920.028]
- When looking for niche Facebook groups, choose groups with new posts at least a few times per week. Less active groups produced few prospective customers in this approach. [L1357.032]
- Participate actively in niche Facebook groups and add value on a regular basis before promoting sales. This makes sales posts look like contributions rather than spam, and you can do it yourself or hire a social media manager. [L1357.033]
- Joining these Facebook groups generally requires a personal Facebook account. If you do not want your personal account linked to the store, or you hand the work to someone else, create a separate personal account for this purpose. [L1357.034]

### Engagement Campaign Tests and Their Limits

- Engagement campaigns to build a warm audience can give cheaper clicks but lower buyer intent, and they can disturb the ad account and pixel data. Use them only for a short creative test (3 to 5 days at about $10 to $20), not as a permanent part of the funnel. [L0645.031]
- Engagement campaigns can show a CTR near 3 percent, but the same creatives in purchase campaigns often drop to about 1.2 to 2 percent. Engagement audiences have lower buying intent and cheaper clicks, so use an engagement test only to rank creatives by CTR, not to forecast purchase results, and keep it to a few days at about $10 to $20. [L0645.049]
- Page-like and engagement campaigns can quickly build follower counts, but followers and likes rarely turn into sales. Targeting English-speaking tier-three countries with about $20 over a week produces numbers, not revenue. (Advice from 2023.) [L0934.113]
- A Facebook page likes campaign with a few dollars per day for about a week can add followers and social proof. Those users rarely become customers, so do not expect sales from them. (Advice from 2024.) [L0965.053]
- A boosted post does not train the pixel or ad account to find buyers well. Keep the budget for proper conversion campaigns so the data is not wasted. [L1225.052]
- Example of a boosted reaction reel: a $55 spend produced 147 link clicks at $0.37 each, about 3,000 views, about 2,300 viewers and roughly 950 three-second video plays. These are good engagement stats, but none of the clicks converted to a sale. [L1225.053]

| Test type | Budget | Duration | Use for | Do not use for |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Engagement creative test | about $10 to $20 | 3 to 5 days | Ranking creatives by CTR | Forecasting purchases [L0645.031, L0645.049] |
| Research engagement post | $5 to $10 per day | Until about 200 comments | Collecting objections and wording [L0906.009] | Driving sales [L0906.010] |
| Engagement on existing post | $3 to $5 per day | Short test | Boosting a post to engage [L0666.015] | Conversion data [L1225.052] |

### Engagement Campaign Setup

- To run an engagement campaign on an existing Facebook post (Advice from 2024):
  1. Choose Engagement as the campaign goal.
  2. In the ad builder, click "use existing post" instead of creating a new ad.
  3. Select the page post.
  4. Set the budget to 3 to 5 dollars per day. [L0666.015]
- For a research engagement campaign on Meta:
  1. Create a new campaign for the research test.
  2. Set the optimization goal to engagement optimization instead of sales and purchases.
  3. Post one simple open question from a whitelisting page, not from the brand page, written like a real person asking for advice.
  4. Set the budget to about $5 to $10 per day.
  5. Reply in the comments as the original poster, guiding the conversation with follow-up questions.
  6. Read every comment and aim for 200 or more comments.
  7. Note the main objections, prior experiences with other brands and the wording people use.
  
  [L0906.009]
- For a research engagement campaign, set the optimization goal to engagement optimization. Do not use sales and purchases, because the aim is to collect feedback from commenters rather than to drive purchases. [L0906.010]

### Lead Campaign Foundations and Measurement

- Meta lead objectives fill the account with cheap form fills and low-quality traffic. The algorithm cannot optimize those leads toward purchases or return on ad spend. [L0797.001]
- Sending Meta a purchase conversion event lets the algorithm optimize ad sets toward buyers. For giveaway funnels, a purchase objective therefore works better than a lead objective. [L0797.002]
- A Meta traffic campaign can send cold visitors to a content page that collects email opt-ins, but the traffic objective usually brings low-quality cold visitors. If such a campaign already performs acceptably it can be left unchanged, but its budget should not be scaled up. (Advice from 2024.) [L0360.089]
- Cold traffic lead generation campaigns typically run 6 to 12 months (half a year to a year) before campaign performance requires moving to Sandbox split-testing. [L0238.011]
- The minimum starting daily budget for lead generation campaigns is $40 per day. [L0238.009]
- A lead ad can also bring in Facebook page likes, because viewers can like the advertising page while responding to the offer. Treat this as a side benefit that grows the page audience, not as the main goal of the campaign. [L0641.023]

### Building a Lead Campaign in Power Editor

- Building a lead campaign in Power Editor:
  1. Open Power Editor and go to Ads.
  2. Click the plus icon to create a new campaign, ad set and ad.
  3. Name the campaign after the industry, and create a separate ad set for each precise interest so you can split test them.
  4. In the ad, select the Facebook page to advertise from.
  5. Enter the website URL of the squeeze page or offer page.
  6. Upload the image and fill in the text above the image, then the headline, link description and display link below the image.
  7. Check the preview, which shows how the ad will appear in the news feed.
  8. Create the conversion tracking pixel with the Leads event and place it in the head section of the thank-you page.
  9. Set the audience location to the market where your store ships, and select only one precise interest per ad set.
  10. Set optimization to optimized CPM with the default bid, and select the tracking pixel as the conversion.
  11. Set the ad set budget and upload the changes to Facebook.
  12. If the ad is denied, open a support ticket and ask which change would get it approved.
  
  (Older advice, 2019 to 2022.) [L0641.005]
- Lead ad set audience: select the location where your online store operates, such as the United States for a US store, and select one precise interest per ad set. The right-hand panel shows the potential reach, which for the example niche was about 560,000 people. (Older advice, 2019 to 2022.) [L0641.010]
- Lead ad set optimization: use optimized CPM with the default bid, and select the tracking pixel in the bottom section. Manual or custom bidding can be tested later once the basics work. (Older advice, 2019 to 2022.) [L0641.011]
- Ad set budgets can start as low as 5 dollars per day. Keep budgets low at the start because many ad sets are split tested and you want to see early whether the site converts before spending heavily. (Older advice, 2019 to 2022.) [L0641.012]
- The Facebook lead ad form has a call-to-action selector at the bottom of the ad settings. A preview on the right side shows how the finished ad will appear in the news feed, so check it before publishing. (Older advice, 2019 to 2022.) [L0641.021]
- Cold lead generation campaign settings: buying type Auction, objective Conversions for leads, and campaign budget optimization turned on. The budget is set at campaign level with an amount that suits the business. [L0649.017]
- Example for a yoga mat seller: cold lead generation can use one campaign with separate interest ad sets for yoga, pilates and a related athleisure brand, plus a lookalike built from past customers. Under campaign budget optimization, Meta shifts spend toward whichever interest converts best after about a week. [L0649.070]

### Email List Building with Facebook Lead Ads

- Facebook lead ad campaigns for building an email list: set the campaign objective to Conversions and the conversion event to Lead, so people submit their details inside Facebook without visiting the website. (Advice from 2024.) [L0836.012]
- Setting up a Facebook lead ad to grow an email list (Advice from 2024):
  1. Create a campaign with the objective set to Conversions.
  2. Set the conversion event to Lead.
  3. Use a cost cap; start low, around 5 USD per lead, and never exceed the value a lead is worth to the business (for example 10 USD).
  4. Offer a valuable lead magnet, such as a buyer's guide that already converts on the store.
  5. Leads opt in on a Facebook form, and their data flows into Shopify, triggering the email flow.
  6. Run for a few hundred leads, then compare the sales they generate against the lead cost.
  
  [L0836.013]
- Before paying for leads through Facebook lead ads, calculate what an email subscriber is worth in revenue (for example from exit-intent overlay sign-ups) over 30 to 60 days, and set the cost cap below that value. (Advice from 2024.) [L0836.014]
- Facebook lead ads for growing an email list are an advanced tactic. Use them only when a store already has a proven opt-in offer and a working email flow, not as a beginner step. (Advice from 2024.) [L0836.061]
- For a list-building campaign, run Conversion campaigns (not Lead Ads) with "lead" as the conversion event, sending traffic to a custom landing page where users enter their email. This delivers higher-quality leads than Facebook's built-in lead forms. [L0307.017]
- To grow an email list, use a retargeting-led sequence:
  1. Run Facebook and Instagram retargeting ads to people who already visited the store.
  2. Run cold Facebook and Instagram ads that send new people to a top-of-funnel social opt-in.
  3. Email the people who opted in.
  4. Send the email subscribers to a product page.
  
  [L0454.053]
- Run Facebook ads to collect emails for a free offer, such as a three-page giveaway. Then:
  1. Use the email list to push a low-cost digital subscription.
  2. Aim for the tripwire offer to at least pay for the Facebook ads spent to collect the emails.
  3. Count physical sales and physical subscriptions as upside on top of that.
  
  [L1153.069]

### Lead Magnets and Targeting for Lead Ads

- For a lead magnet ad on Facebook, target people by interests tied to the niche, its events and the brands or companies in it, not by buying intent. Because these people are not searching to buy yet, the ad should offer a free resource rather than a discount. [L1076.075]

### B2B and Local Lead Examples

- For B2B or commercial products that are not aimed at consumers, such as restaurant equipment, use a branding campaign rather than a conversion campaign. Facebook's interest and demographic targeting can find business owners, which lets you build brand recognition over time. [L1243.045]
- Local dentist lead campaign, audience and ad settings: target the business's city with a radius of about 20 kilometers, ages 18 to 65 plus, and interests such as tooth beauty, salons and fitness. The ad uses a real procedure video provided by the dentist. [L0440.027]
- The dentist's ad call-to-action uses a Facebook lead ad form that collects the prospect's name, email and phone number, along with consent to use the data. The lead then gets an option to book a free consultation at the dental practice. [L0440.028]
- Example from one test: a Facebook ad campaign for a dentist's Invisalign offer generated 6 leads in its first days. The ad cost about 8 to 9 Swiss francs or dollars per lead, and each lead had a potential value of 3,000 to 5,000 dollars. [L0440.029]

### Retargeting and Paid Traffic Priority

- Paid ads are needed to reach a target audience, and the paid-traffic priority applies across the funnel: organic page growth ranks below paid traffic until monthly paid spend reaches a satisfactory return on ad spend. [L0641.001, L0666.016]

## Seasonality, Market Conditions and Special Cases

### Seasonal Planning and Q4 Cost Pressure

- Historical ROAS of 5-7 is the normal baseline. During peak seasons, when 70-80% of market budgets concentrate on holidays and seasonal events, a drop to 3.5-4.5 is normal, and sustained competition keeps ad costs high. [L0298.004]
- Q4 and Black Friday raise CPMs because large brands spend heavily, so customer acquisition gets more expensive late in the year. Plan budgets and expected costs with that increase in mind. [L0692.036]
- High CPMs on new products and new creative during peak periods, such as after Halloween, are normal because big brands spend millions at the same time. CPM falls as competitive spend normalizes. [L0298.027]
- Ad costs rise in Q4 because retailers and profitable businesses increase spending before year-end to reduce taxable profit. Black Friday through Christmas traffic is therefore more expensive. (Older advice, 2019.) [L1110.050]
- To prepare for higher Q4 costs (older advice, 2019):
  1. Move part of the normal November and December ad budget into earlier months, when costs are lower. [L1110.034]
  2. Use that budget to build an audience of website visitors. [L1110.034]
  3. Remarket to those visitors through Black Friday, Cyber Monday and Christmas, with both ads and emails, which is the lowest-cost advertising available. [L1110.034]
- For cold-weather products, raise ad spend as the cold season starts in the target market, not after demand has peaked. Demand for these products rises as temperatures fall, so the budget should follow that rise. [L1316.048]
- Increasing ad budgets is appropriate in a slow January when ROAS looks healthy, because sales typically pick up the following week after the post-Black Friday lull. [L0349.026]
- Dropshippers without warehouse stock cannot advertise during a supplier closure. An operator with stock can gain share from competitors that cannot run ads. [L0349.027]

### Ad Budget Formulas

- Monthly ad budget = target monthly revenue ÷ 10, because the target return on ad spend is 10x. (Older advice, 2020.) [L1277.086]
- Daily budget = monthly ad budget ÷ 30. [L1277.086]
- Example: a 10,000 USD revenue target gives a 1,000 USD ad budget, about 33 USD per day. [L1277.086]
- Ad spend should not produce a return below 10x. Anything lower means the ads are losing money. (Older advice, 2020.) [L1277.088]
- Example daily split for a 10,000 USD monthly revenue target (older advice, 2020):

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Monthly revenue target | 10,000 USD |
| Monthly ad budget at 10x return | 1,000 USD |
| Daily ad budget | about 33 USD |
| Google Shopping (product listing ads) | about 28 USD per day |
| Facebook remarketing | 5 USD per day |

Audit the ads every few days. [L1277.087]

- Conflict on ROAS targets: the 2020 source sets a 10x floor and says anything lower loses money. The Source C source accepts a drop to 3.5-4.5 during peak seasons as normal. The sources do not say which governs a given campaign. [L1277.088, L0298.004]
- Planning CPM: use about 15 USD CPM when estimating ad cost per 1,000 impressions for the product's price point. [L1176.015]

### CPM Thresholds and Market Selection

- Alarm threshold for a digital product: a Facebook CPM above roughly 20-25 is expensive. Above 30, interactive ad formats are recommended to lower it. [L0678.008]
- Q4 testing rule: avoid testing in countries where CPM is above 35 USD, unless the product is already profitable there. Prefer countries with lower CPM for testing. [L1219.013]
- The two thresholds apply to different decisions. The 20-25 and 30 figures are general alarm and action levels. The 35 USD figure is a cutoff for Q4 test markets. [L0678.008, L1219.013]
- Interactive ads that get comments, likes and shares from the audience tend to lower CPM. [L0678.009]
- Targeting a different market is the most basic way to reduce CPM, because some countries deliver much cheaper impressions. [L0678.009]
- Procedure: find the country with the highest CPM and check whether it delivers results. [L0678.010]
  1. Open the ad account in Meta Ads Manager. [L0678.010]
  2. On the right side, click Breakdown. [L0678.010]
  3. Choose By delivery. [L0678.010]
  4. Review age, gender and country to see which combination gives the best results. [L0678.010]
  5. Stop spending on countries that show high CPM without good results. [L0678.010]
- Case: a digital product priced at 97 USD was tested with a Facebook ad set covering many countries, with CPM of about 50-80 USD. After switching to a single ad set with several English-speaking and European countries, CPM dropped to about 18. The seller had three sales at that point. [L0678.011]
- Do not judge a campaign on CPM alone. The case above shows CPM changing with the country mix, but the seller's sales count was still very small. [L0678.011]

### Advertiser Pullouts and Falling CPMs

- Meta ad prices work like an auction. The more advertisers bid for the same audience, the higher the price of each impression. When a large group stops bidding, prices fall for everyone, including those who did not depend on that group. [L1407.013]
- Case, April 2025 (the lesson was recorded on Wednesday, April 30): Temu stopped running Meta ads on April 16. The speaker estimated that a large share of US Meta ad spend came from Chinese sellers and US dropshippers sourcing from China, and that this spend disappeared at once. A CPM drop across Meta followed within about two weeks. This is the speaker's estimate, not a verified figure. [L1407.014]
- Temu was named as one of the large low-price sellers that spent heavily on Meta before pulling its ads in April. The speaker said Shein and similar sellers were also major spenders and that their pullback mattered too. These are the speaker's unverified estimates. [L1407.027]
- Unverified estimates cited in the lesson:
  - An analyst estimated that about 7 billion USD of Meta's 132 billion USD in 2023 ad revenue came from China. The speaker assumed the real figure was higher in 2024 and 2025. [L1407.015]
  - The speaker estimated that more than 10% of the US Meta ad budget came from Chinese companies or US dropshippers sourcing from China before tariffs and the ad pullback. [L1407.016]
- When ad costs fall because large advertisers pause, the same budget reaches more people. This is temporary. Prices are expected to return to normal if the tariffs or market shift that caused the pullback end. [L1407.017]
- Ad costs also fell during COVID, when many physical businesses such as retail stores and restaurants stopped advertising. Any large group of advertisers pulling out lowers prices, whatever the reason. [L1407.018]
- Use falling CPMs to test ad goals that cost more when prices are high:
  - scaling spend on winning ads
  - lead generation ads that send visitors to an email opt-in page
  - ads that promote blog posts
  - campaigns that build social followers
  
  Do this only for stores that already have sales and profit, not for brand-new stores. [L1407.019]
- If you already run Meta ads, compare your current CPM with your CPM one, two and three months ago. A lower CPM means the same budget reaches more people. [L1407.020]

### Holiday and Event Campaigns

- Use the same ad format throughout the campaign: dark imagery with a simple sales banner overlay. Stage discounts: 50% off in pre-sale, then 60% off on Black Friday itself. The extra margin is small, only 10 percentage points, but it drives urgency. [L0308.001]
- Run the same holiday deal creative in both the Instagram and Facebook news feeds, with the same image and copy adapted to each placement. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L0799.011]
- State the exact end time in the body copy, such as midnight on the last day of the sale. A fixed, real end point makes the urgency believable. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L0799.021]
- Send holiday deal ads to a dedicated sale landing page for that event, such as a /black-friday URL, not the homepage. This keeps the click focused on the one offer the ad promises. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L0799.022]
- For a promotion lasting only a few days, rotate several creatives in bright, different colors plus one video variant, so the same audience does not see one ad for the whole sale window. Keep the same offer in every version and change only the visuals. [L0649.073]
- Run promotions on Reach and Frequency, not Conversions, and show them to warm audiences: website visitors from the past 180 days, plus Facebook and Instagram engagers. Do not exclude past customers, and never show promotions to cold audiences. [L0649.023]
- Meta remarketing for holiday promotions:
  - Create one new campaign per holiday.
  - Use static image ads, not dynamic remarketing ads.
  - Audience: all website visitors from the past 180 days.
  - Exclusion: all customers who purchased in the past 30 days.
  
  [L1223.024]
- Retargeting worked well for large events such as Black Friday, Cyber Monday or Christmas, when site traffic is high. Outside such events it may not work consistently, especially after iOS 14 changed tracking. [L0646.051]
- Black Friday and Cyber Monday CPMs rise because competitors raise budgets, which makes targeting more expensive. A useful response is an extended Black Friday or Cyber Monday discount starting the day after the event. [L1185.019]
- A Meta ad account that had spent steadily for about 1.5 years was allowed to scale to very large daily budgets when spend jumped. A new account or newly set-up business manager may face restrictions when spend increases quickly. Build a steady spend history before planning a large sale. [L1363.060]
- For a holiday-themed campaign, restrict ads to the countries where the holiday is celebrated instead of running them broadly. For Cinco de Mayo, the example pointed to Mexico and the US. [L0577.079]

### Geographic and Gifting Niches

- GCC markets such as Qatar, Saudi Arabia and the UAE showed a Facebook CPM of about 1 USD per 1,000 impressions. This is cheap, but these markets still required cash-on-delivery to convert. [L1212.032]
- For gift-type products in the holiday season, test a broad audience in one ad set, alongside audiences such as grandparents or people with a friend who has a child, since they may buy as gifts. Avoid overly narrow interests with this approach. [L0692.055]

### Digital Products

- Validate the product with paid traffic before investing time in creating it. Keep early costs low by using existing market videos. [L0513.035]
- Facebook is a strong platform for digital products, together with Pinterest and Etsy. [L0577.040]
- Pinterest and organic social media can also drive traffic to digital products, alongside paid Facebook and TikTok ads. [L0513.036]
- Run Facebook and TikTok ads for a digital product as you would for a physical product:
  - Start with interest-based targeting that matches the problem and audience profile, such as age, gender and related hobbies.
  - Use the cost per customer from these ads to decide the price and how much room the margin allows.
  
  [L0513.048]
- Facebook ads are a recommended paid traffic source for affiliate funnels. Free alternatives, if you want the first sale without spending on ads, are guest posts on other blogs, forum participation and active community engagement. [L0514.031]

### High-Ticket Products

- For high-ticket items, a lead campaign that drives people into a funnel with email capture, followed by a drip sequence, can work better than sending cold traffic straight to a sale. Higher-priced products need more patience and more spend to gather enough data. [L0678.013]
- Touchpoints before purchase:
  - Products around 50-60 USD may need about 7-10 touchpoints.
  - Products around 200 USD may need 20-30 touchpoints.
  
  Expect a higher-priced product to take more creative exposure and more time before results. [L1225.058]
- Example for a 200 USD product: one creative may need to be seen three or four times, and another about five times, before the buyer converts. Plan the testing budget around that exposure. [L1225.059]
- About 300 USD of spend was cited as a rough reference for a campaign to start learning. Higher-priced products typically need more spend and more time in market. Do not judge a higher-ticket campaign on a few days of data. [L1225.060]
- Do not start a high-ticket product with a small test budget such as 10 EUR per day. Collecting useful data at a higher cost per purchase takes too long. [L0693.054]
- For high-ticket products, organic social posts rarely convert directly. Post one or two times per week to show activity, and focus budget on proven paid ads. (Source dated 2024.) [L0806.061]

### Print-on-Demand Products

- For print-on-demand products, Facebook carousel ads and shopping ads were reported to work best. [L1214.032]

## Ad Operations, Delegation and Platform Maintenance

### Daily Tracking Routine

- Track these Meta ad metrics every day in the tracker: ad spend, CPM, CTR, cost per click, cost per purchase (compared with target cost per purchase), and ROAS. The tracker colours each metric green, amber or red against its benchmark. [L1354.009]
- Do not refresh the ad account every 20 minutes. Refreshing interrupts the algorithm's learning and drives emotional reactions. Step away from the dashboard so the campaign runs long enough to produce usable data. [L1445.006]
- Compare the set budget with actual spend in Ads Manager instead of trusting the displayed budget. A campaign can keep showing a daily budget that the account no longer spends after a failed payment. One account went from a 50 USD daily budget rising to about 100 USD back to a displayed 50 USD, while delivery stayed near 10 USD in 24 hours. [L1432.041]

### Ad Account Limits and Ad Housekeeping

- Meta limits active or in-review ads to around 250 per ad account. Turn off non-performing ads to free space, rather than creating new copies of the same creative. [L0637.030]
- To reuse an existing post and avoid creating new ads: [L0637.031]
  1. Open the ad set and duplicate it. [L0637.031]
  2. At the ad level, choose "Use existing post" instead of creating a new ad. [L0637.031]
  3. Select the ad or paste its Post ID from the Page post column. [L0637.031]
  4. Keep the same creative, so the engagement from the original ad is combined into one post. [L0637.031]
- To apply a new offer to all ads: [L0650.019]
  1. Copy the offer sentence from the store. [L0650.019]
  2. Go to the ads and select all of them. [L0650.019]
  3. Click edit on the ad and paste the offer into the primary text. [L0650.019]
- If many advertisers report the same platform-side inconsistency, pause the affected campaigns and relaunch them after one or two days, since the problem is often solved within that time. [L0692.025]

### Batch Launching

- Batch processing applies the same targeting, budget and placement settings to an entire group of creatives and launches them all simultaneously, instead of creating each ad individually in Meta Ads Manager. This eliminates repetitive configuration steps and drastically reduces launch time. [L0207.010]
- The built-in Meta Ad Manager integration in Ad Manage.ai loads campaign data and analytics in 2.9 seconds. [L0207.006]

### Account Structure and Spend Control

- A new ad account may start with a daily spend limit of about $50. Split that daily budget across several interest audiences so each one gets enough spend to show usable metrics. [L0640.047]
- After about 100-200 sales, switch to a dedicated ad account with a simple structure of two to three main campaigns. Use one pixel dedicated only to that store. [L1217.043]
- Products from the same niche can be tested in parallel in the same ad account and pixel. Separate ad accounts when a winning product belongs to a different niche. [L1217.044]
- *Older advice (2020):* Facebook supports multiple ad accounts within one Business Manager. Keep one credit card per business network and separate ad accounts for each store. [L1277.089]

### Billing Charges and Payment Methods

- A Facebook ad account can start spending and bill the card on file even when no campaigns are switched on, with one case reported at about £10,000. Remove unused payment cards from ad accounts you no longer use. [L1214.031]
- Threshold charges on new accounts:

  | Case | Amount | Assessment |
  |---|---|---|
  | Typical threshold charge on a new ad account | About 10 USD | Signals that the account will pay in the future. [L1347.012] |
  | Threshold charge on a new account | 200 EUR | Not normal and should be disputed. [L1347.012] |

- Example of an unexplained charge: a seller bought a Business Manager, created a new ad account and started spending. When the spend reached the threshold, Facebook charged 200 EUR that had not been spent, and the money was not held as a budget. Support forwarded the case to an internal team and no quick fix followed. The ad kept running and Facebook then tried to charge another 150 EUR for money not spent. The seller created a second ad account, and the third account was charged 16 EUR. The seller asked for a screenshot of the billing page to investigate, and the issue was described as rare and unusual. [L1347.013]
- If Facebook charges an unexplained amount on an ad account: [L1347.014]
  1. Take a screenshot of the billing page to document the charge. [L1347.014]
  2. Contact Facebook support and ask for the charge to be reviewed. [L1347.014]
  3. Do not assume the issue is resolved while ads are still running. Monitor the billing page for further charges. [L1347.014]

### Facebook Shop Refunds

- Orders can come in through Facebook Shop without the buyer visiting the Shopify store, so refunds and returns for these orders must still be handled. Facebook can side with the buyer in disputes, so keep the order records in Shopify ready. [L1128.009]
- Case: a seller issued a refund for a Facebook Shop order to avoid trouble with Facebook. The buyer then appealed with Facebook and received a second refund, pulling money back from the seller's account. The seller appealed with Facebook by sending the Shopify order information and records, and Facebook reversed the refund and returned the money to the seller. [L1128.010]

### Delegating Ad Setup

- Delegate traffic and ads only after you know which Facebook or TikTok ad strategy works for your store. Otherwise a contractor who leaves or suddenly raises rates leaves you with no way to keep the ads running. [L1358.014]
- To delegate ad campaign setup: [L1358.015, L1360.015]
  1. Have the team member prepare the creatives first, including videos and pictures for the ads. [L1358.015, L1360.015]
  2. Give the team member access to your Business Manager so they can build the campaign structure. [L1358.015, L1360.015]
  3. Tell them exactly how many ad sets you want, the budget, and how many creatives per ad set have been successful for you in the past. [L1358.015, L1360.015]
  4. Let them run the campaigns for a couple of days, while you monitor them closely. [L1358.015, L1360.015]
  5. Give feedback. [L1358.015, L1360.015]
  6. Once you have a proven, experienced team member and results are stable, let them manage the campaigns independently. [L1358.015, L1360.015]

## Other details

### Platform reps and budget scaling

- Advertising platform reps are usually paid to increase spend. Meta's higher-level reps tend to focus on long-term results and give advice that produces faster results, while reps from other platforms often push large budgets toward new cold audiences. [L0801.078] (older, 2021)
- When a platform rep suggests tripling a profitable campaign budget, test the advice cautiously. Scale what already works manually, and add prospecting only in small amounts instead of putting most of a larger budget into cold traffic. [L0801.079] (older, 2021)
- Case: a store's Pinterest account manager advised raising monthly ad spend from 300 to 1,000 dollars, with most of the new money going to prospecting new cold customers and a small part to retargeting. The store's ads were already profitable, so the advice was to test prospecting carefully rather than quadruple the budget at once. [L0801.080] (older, 2021)

### Meta ad setup and checks

- Meta changes the labels, defaults and options in Ads Manager often, so a setup guide can be outdated within months. Check every field against the current screen before applying a setting, and treat old screenshots and names as a guide only. [L0865.065]
- To see why you are being shown or retargeted by an ad, click the small arrow in the top-right corner of any Facebook or Instagram ad. This shows which pixel, page or audience triggered the ad. [L1243.010]
- Run all campaigns in the background rather than posting them to your Facebook business page with threads. [L0238.002]
- Advanced Meta ad strategies are later-stage options. Add them only after the core Meta setup is maxed out and still needs more levers. [L0544.011]

### Feed training and organic reach

- The same feed-training approach works on Instagram Reels and Facebook. On Facebook, you can engage with ads from your own account to get more ads of the same type, so a separate account is optional. [L0706.022]
- To target a specific country when posting organic Instagram reels, use VPN-based account setup. Without a VPN, reach is mostly in the account's home region, such as Switzerland, Germany, and Austria. [L1212.043]
- As of the week of 29 June to 5 July 2026, Instagram's "Your Algorithm" control sits in the top-right corner of the Explore feed. Users can use it to tell Instagram which kinds of content they want to see more or less of, which helps brands find relevant creators and audiences. [L1504.001]
- Instagram was reported to be adding "more like this" and "less like this" buttons to Reels, with the "Your Algorithm" control testable from the Home feed. These feedback buttons were described as coming soon, not yet fully available. [L1504.002]

### Google Shopping campaign types (older, 2022)

Google's campaign types changed in 2022. The guidance below is from that period and may no longer match Google's current options.

| Campaign type | Guidance | ID |
|---|---|---|
| Smart Shopping | Being discontinued between July and September 2022, replaced by Performance Max. | [L0249.048] |
| Smart Shopping | Results start strong but diminish over time, because Google exhausts warm and hot audiences (retargeting lists and brand searches) and then expands into colder audiences. Do not launch now due to discontinuation. | [L0249.049] |
| Performance Max | Currently in testing phase at small budgets. Do not recommend for shopping. It acts as a black box without data on why campaigns work or don't work, making scaling impossible. | [L0249.050] |
| Standard Shopping | No discontinuation planned. Use these as your primary campaign type. | [L0249.051] |
| Smart Shopping | If testing, run in parallel with your alpha/beta campaigns. Never replace your main campaigns with Smart Shopping. | [L0249.052] |

### Spark ads on TikTok

- Spark ads may slow the organic growth of an account if they run for a long time, though opinions on this are divided. Testing with two separate accounts, one for ads and one for organic, can show the effect on your own account. [L0352.048]
- Run spark ads for a short initial period, such as the first day or first week, to get past the early slump, then pause the ads. Continuous ad spending may tell TikTok to push organic content less. [L0352.049]
- Skip Spark Ads on a TikTok video that is already spreading organically, because boosting it adds little. Consider Spark Ads only when a new video shows solid early views but has not taken off on its own. [L1186.038]
- Spark ads budget example: load $100 into the account, set a $50 daily budget, and run about $20 per day per ad group. The minimum per ad group was reported as $20 to $25, and budget can be raised once a video produces strong impressions. [L0352.050]
- Spark ads require a TikTok Ads Manager account, with the TikTok account added or a code generated in the Ads Manager. This method is more efficient than other promotion options but carries a higher risk to organic reach. [L0352.051]

### TikTok campaign structure, timing and upkeep

- Testing structure: one campaign with three to four ad groups (open, stacked interest, hashtags, open or stacked interest again). Give each ad group a budget of around $25 to $30, because a $20 ad group budget often stalled spending on new accounts. Launch ad groups at 6 a.m. or 8 a.m. Eastern time. [L1382.036]
- Best performance times for TikTok ads: 9 to 11 a.m., 12 to 3 p.m., and 7 to 11 p.m. Ads launched at 8 a.m. often start spending after about 30 to 40 minutes, so they effectively begin around 9 a.m. [L1382.037]
- CBO campaigns on TikTok reach different audience pockets within a huge audience. Each campaign with several ad groups tends to hit different pockets, and the campaign's budget flows to the pocket that performs. Overlap between campaigns is therefore low on TikTok compared to Facebook. [L1382.047]
- Check and rotate TikTok campaigns frequently, often every two to three days, because they lose performance over time. New campaigns with the same structure can replace the old ones without changing the store. [L1382.048]
- If a campaign's budget keeps decreasing while CTR and CPM show zero, there may be a tracking issue. Check the date range in the top right of the dashboard. If the data still does not appear, stop the campaign and duplicate it to restart the data flow. [L1347.017]

### TikTok ad rules and comments

- TikTok scans landing pages and enforces strict rules for health and fitness claims such as weight loss. Keep claims compliant and make product pages distinct. [L1213.074]
- Turn off comments on TikTok ads. The 18 to 24 age group discusses openly, and comments can reveal dropshipping publicly. [L1213.075]

### Store landing pages

- A landing page should combine a large value-proposition hero, early trust signals, benefit-focused copy, a clear offer, and calls to action repeated lower on the page. Each element exists to make a buyer with existing intent cross the decision threshold without friction. [L0918.001]
- The hero section should give a snapshot of the main outcome the customer gets, followed by heavy trust-building directly around it. The goal is that visitors believe the promised result is achievable with this brand. [L0918.003]
- On a Shopify free theme such as Dawn, a quick add to cart button on the collection page can be enabled without installing an app. Products with options open a small page for choosing the variant before adding to cart. [L0642.026]

### Order value and return on ad spend

- When the paid ad cost per customer stays the same, a higher order value from that same customer directly improves the return on ad spend. Raising AOV through bundles, cross-sells and upsells therefore gets more out of every paid acquisition. [L0435.019]
- Example Facebook ad spend over 90 days: $300,000. [L0222.009]
- Paid traffic for high-ticket stores runs on Google Ads, Bing Ads and Facebook Ads, and the same operator can manage budgets across all three. Being able to manage several ad platforms is an advantage when scaling. [L1014.018] (older, 2021-2022)

### Audience timing and urgency

- For financial products affecting retirees, customer decision urgency and timing follow demographic-specific patterns. Urgency peaks sharply 90 days before the filing deadline, and again at age 64.5-65 when benefits become available. Adjust ad frequency, budget allocation, and messaging intensity to align with these psychological urgency peaks. [L0221.023]
- Volume of decision-makers also signals opportunity: 71 million people currently receive social security, with Boomers moving through peak claiming ages now. [L0221.023]

### Customer comment research

Use the prompt below on comments copied from beneath an ad.

```
Analyze the comments copied from beneath one of our Facebook or Instagram ads and turn them into a professionally structured document. Organization rules: 1. Remove every username and personal identifier completely. 2. Build a professional document with a clear hierarchy. 3. Sort comments into main categories with descriptive headings (for example 'Product Ingredients Questions', 'Usage Instructions', 'Effectiveness Feedback'). 4. Create focused subcategories that cluster very similar topics. 5. Cap each category at 8-10 comments so it stays readable. 6. Order the categories by marketing importance, with the most valuable customer insights first. Formatting rules: 1. Format primary comments with '●' bullets. 2. Format replies with '○' indentation. 3. Use a clear heading hierarchy with proper spacing between sections. 4. If a thread has more than 3 replies, include only the most informative exchanges. 5. Group similar questions and comments together even when they were not sequential in the original. 6. Keep the document clean and easy to scan, highlighting patterns. Content guidelines: 1. Preserve EXACTLY what people said, including slang, spelling errors and emojis. 2. Include ALL substantive comments about product questions and concerns, personal experiences (positive and negative), objections or skepticism, testimonials and results, and feature requests or suggestions. 3. Omit generic comments with no informational value (such as 'following' or a single emoji). 4. For long threads on the same topic, summarize duplicate points. Analysis section (after organizing all comments), provide: 1. Overall sentiment patterns: the positive, negative and neutral proportions, plus emotionally charged topics. 2. Audience insights: demographic patterns from language and context; distinct user segments with their interests and concerns; the level of product understanding and education. 3. Key questions and concerns: the top 5 most frequent questions or objections; areas of confusion or misunderstanding; scientific or technical concerns. 4. Pain points and desires: problems users want solved; before-and-after scenarios mentioned; explicit and implicit needs. 5. Social sharing patterns: reasons people tag friends; content that generates engagement; elements that make the product shareable. 6. Response to product features: features generating the most excitement; features causing confusion or skepticism; missing features users request. 7. Most valuable testimonials: direct quotes from compelling success stories; recurring patterns in positive experiences; third-party validation elements. 8. Marketing strategy insights: language patterns to adopt in marketing copy; topics needing more education or clarification; positioning opportunities against competitors; audience segments to prioritize. Raw comment section: [PASTE COMMENT SECTION HERE]
```

[L0613.063]

### Local agency outreach and offline ads

Cold outreach to a local dentist using Facebook ads follows these steps:

1. Find dentists in the chosen city with D7 Lead Finder. [L0440.026]
2. Check whether each business is running Facebook ads, and review those ads. [L0440.026]
3. If the ads are weak or missing, call the business and ask for 10 minutes to present the agency. [L0440.026]
4. Show a short presentation on what the agency can do. [L0440.026]
5. Offer to test the ads at no up-front cost to the client, with the client paying the ad spend directly. [L0440.026]
6. Set up a Facebook campaign focused on the Invisalign product. [L0440.026]
7. Once the test produces leads, set a monthly fee for managing the ads. [L0440.026]

- Run the client's ad test for about two weeks at no charge, with the ad spend paid directly by the client. Aim for around 30 to 40 leads in that period before stopping the ads and presenting results. [L0440.030]
- Billboards, mall events and similar offline advertising are generally not worth it for sales. Consider them only if very cheap and used for photos and social proof. [L0969.080] (older, 2022)
